Pudgalas, Trainings, and Awakening Topics

Śrāvakabhūmi · Sanskrit (Shukla 1973) ↔ AI-assisted English
← All topicsView English only →

The second yogasthāna. After a verse summarizing the stage of renunciation (1.1–2), the text puts seventeen questions (2.1–17) and answers each: the twenty-eight persons who go forth from that stage (2.18–19.9); the delineation of persons by eleven divisions (20.1–34.9); the defilement-purifying object, the fourth of the four objects (35.1–46.19); instruction (47.1–48.37); the three trainings (49.1–55.31); the dharmas conducive to training (56.1–59.2); loss of yoga, yoga, and attention (59.3–60.7), with the signs and the resolves (60.8–64.11); the tasks of yoga (64.12–36); the three practitioners of yoga (65.1–67.26); the cultivation of yoga, as cultivation of perception and of the thirty-seven dharmas on the side of awakening (67.27–86.57); the fruit of cultivation, the four fruits of asceticism (86.58–88.5; 88.6–88.100 return to the conduct-types and their marks); the six person-designations (89.1–93.28); the four māras and māra-actions (94.1–97.15); and the three conditions on which an undertaking is fruitful or fruitless (98.1–22). A summary verse (99.1–102.1) and the colophon (103.1) close the yogasthāna. The answer on the object begins at sbh2.1 1.1, and the first three objects fill sbh2.1–sbh2.7; 35.1 resumes with the fourth, and 46.18–19 names all four and closes the answer.

sbh2.0:1.1

and their requisite-stage, called renunciation.

sbh2.0:3.1
rāgonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the lust-intoxicated person?
sbh2.0:3.2
yena pudgalena pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu rāga āsevito, bhāvito, bahulīkṛtaḥ sa tena hetunā tena pratyayainaitarhi raṁjanīye vastuni tīvrarāgaśca, bhavatyāyata na rāgaśca |
The person by whom, in previous other births, lust was resorted to, cultivated, [and] made much of — he, by that cause, by that condition, now has intense lust and prolonged lust toward a lust-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:3.3
ayamucyate rāgonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the lust-intoxicated person.
sbh2.0:3.4
dveṣonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the hatred-intoxicated person?
sbh2.0:3.5
yena pudgalena dveṣaḥ pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu āsevito, bhāvito, bahulīkṛtaḥ |
The person by whom, in previous other births, hatred was resorted to, cultivated, [and] made much of —
sbh2.0:3.6
tena hetunā tena pratyayenaitarhi dveṣaṇīye vastuni tīvradveṣaśca bhavatyāyatadveṣaśca |
by that cause, by that condition, [he] now has intense hatred and prolonged hatred toward a hatred-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:3.7
ayamucyate |
This is called
sbh2.0:3.8
dveṣonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ |
the hatred-intoxicated person.
sbh2.0:3.9
mohonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the delusion-intoxicated person?
sbh2.0:3.10

The person by whom, in previous other births, delusion was resorted to, cultivated, [and] made much of —
sbh2.0:3.11
tena ca hetunā tena pratyayena |
by that cause, by that condition,
sbh2.0:3.12
etarhi mohanīye vastuni tīvramohaśca bhavatyāyata mohaścāyamucyate mohonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ |
[he] now has intense delusion and prolonged delusion toward a delusion-arousing thing. This is called the delusion-intoxicated person.
sbh2.0:3.13
mānonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the conceit-intoxicated person?
sbh2.0:3.14
yena pudgalena pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu māna āsevito, bhāvito, bahulīkṛtastena hetunā tena pratyayena |
The person by whom, in previous other births, conceit was resorted to, cultivated, [and] made much of — by that cause, by that condition,
sbh2.0:3.15
etarhi manyanīye mānanīye vastuni tīvramānaśca bhavatyāyatamānaśca |
[he] now has intense conceit and prolonged conceit toward a conceit-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:4.1
ayamucyate mānonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the conceit-intoxicated person.
sbh2.0:4.2
vitarkonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the applied-thought-intoxicated person?
sbh2.0:4.3
yena pudgalena pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu vitarka āsevito, bhāvito, bahulīkṛtastena ca hetunā tena ca pratyayena etarhi vitarka sthānīye vastuni tīvravitarkaśca bhavatyāyatavitarkaśca |
The person by whom, in previous other births, applied thought was resorted to, cultivated, [and] made much of — by that cause, by that condition, [he] now has intense applied-thought and prolonged applied-thought toward an applied-thought-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:4.4
ayamucyate vitarkonmadaḥ |
This is called the applied-thought-intoxicated
sbh2.0:4.5
pudgalaḥ |
person.
sbh2.0:5.1
samaprāptaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the balanced person (samaprāpta)?
sbh2.0:5.2
yena pudgalena pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu rāgo, dveṣo, moho, māno, vitarko nāsevito, bhāvito, bahulīkṛto, na caite dharmā ādīnavato dṛṣṭāḥ |
The person by whom, in previous other births, lust, hatred, delusion, conceit, [and] applied-thought were not resorted to, cultivated, [or] made much of, and [for whom] these dharmas were not seen as dangerous,
sbh2.0:5.3
vidūṣitāḥ, santīritāḥ |
[not] found fault with, [not] thoroughly examined —
sbh2.0:5.4
sa tena hetunā, tena ca pratyayena raṁjanīye vā vastuni, dveṣaṇīye vā vastuni, manyanīye mānanīye , vitarkaṇīye vā vastuni na tīvrarāgo bhavati |
he, by that cause, by that condition, in a lust-arousing or hatred-arousing [Lacuna] or conceit-arousing or applied-thought-arousing thing, does not have intense lust,
Printed text omits the delusion member (mohanīye vā vastuni); Xuanzang has it (所愚事).
sbh2.0:5.5
nāpyāyatarāgaḥ |
nor prolonged lust,
sbh2.0:5.6
na cāsya samudācarati rāgo, yaduta tena vastunā |
and lust does not become manifest in him through that thing.
sbh2.0:5.7
yathā rāga evaṁ dveṣo, moho, māno, vitarkaḥ |
As with lust, so with hatred, delusion, conceit, [and] applied-thought.
sbh2.0:5.8
ayamucyate samaprāptaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the balanced person.
sbh2.0:5.9
mandarajaskaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the slight-dust person (mandarajaska)?
sbh2.0:5.10
yena pudgalena pūrvamanyāsu jātiṣu na rāga āsevito bhavati, bahulīkṛtaḥ |
The person by whom, in previous other births, lust was not resorted to, [not] made much of,
sbh2.0:5.11
ādīnavataśca bahulaṁ dṛṣṭo bhavati |
and [who] has often seen it as dangerous,
sbh2.0:5.12
vidūṣitaḥ santīritaḥ |
found fault with it, thoroughly examined it —
sbh2.0:5.13
sa tena hetunā tena pratyayenaitarhi raṁjanīye vastuni samavahite, saṁmukhībhūte, adhimātre, pracure, udva ulva ṇe rajyaṁ rāgamutpādayati |
he, by that cause, by that condition, now toward a lust-arousing thing [that is] encountered, present, strong, abundant, [and] glaring, produces [Conjectural: slight] lust;
Printed rajyaṁ rāgam, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 起微劣貪 (T1579 juan 26).
sbh2.0:5.14
madhye parītte naivotpādayati |
toward a middle or small one, he does not produce [it].
sbh2.0:5.15
yathā rāga evaṁ dveṣo, moho, māno, vitarkā rko , veditavyāḥ vyaḥ |
As with lust, so with hatred, delusion, conceit, [and] applied-thought are to be understood.
sbh2.0:5.16
ayamucyate mandarajaskaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the slight-dust person.
sbh2.0:6.1
pratipannakaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the practitioner person?
sbh2.0:6.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:6.3
pratipannakāḥ pudgalāścatvāraḥ |
there are four practitioner persons,
sbh2.0:6.4
tadyathā srota āpattiphalapratipannakaḥ |
namely: the practitioner for the fruit of stream-entry,
sbh2.0:6.5
sakṛdāgāmiphalapratipannakaḥ |
the practitioner for the fruit of once-returning,
sbh2.0:6.6
anāgāmiphalapratipannakaḥ |
the practitioner for the fruit of non-returning,
sbh2.0:6.7
arhattvaphalapratipannakaḥ |
[and] the practitioner for the fruit of arhatship.
sbh2.0:6.8
ayamucyate pratipannakaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the practitioner person.
sbh2.0:7.1
phalastha pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the fruit-attainer person?
sbh2.0:7.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:7.3
strota āpannaḥ, sakṛdāgāmī, anāgāmī arhan |
the stream-enterer, the once-returner, the non-returner, [and] the arhat.
sbh2.0:7.4
ayamucyate phalasthaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the fruit-attainer person.
sbh2.0:8.1
śraddhānusārī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the faith-follower person?
sbh2.0:8.2
yaḥ pudgalaḥ parata ava to ’va vādānuśāsanīṁ paryeṣate |
The person who seeks instruction-and-admonition from another
sbh2.0:8.3
tadvalena ca pratipadyate |
and by its force practices,
sbh2.0:8.4
yaduta phalasyādhigamāya |
namely, for the attainment of the [path-]fruit,
sbh2.0:8.5
no tūdgṛhīteṣu dharmeṣu |
[because] in the dharmas as grasped,
sbh2.0:8.6
paryavāpteṣu, cintiteṣu, tuliteṣūpaparīkṣiteṣu svayamevaśakto bhavati |
as mastered, reflected on, weighed, [and] examined, he is not himself capable,
sbh2.0:8.7
pratibalaḥ |
[not] fit,
sbh2.0:8.8
tān dharmān bhāvanākāreṇānusarttuṁ |
to follow those dharmas by way of cultivation.
sbh2.0:8.9
nānyatra pudgalānusāriṇyā prajñayā pratipadyate |
He practices only with a wisdom that follows [another] person —
sbh2.0:8.10
tasmācchraddhānusārītyucyate |
therefore he is called faith-follower.
sbh2.0:9.1
dharmānusārī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the dharma-follower person?
sbh2.0:9.2
yatpu yaḥ pu dgalo yathāśruteṣu dharmeṣu, paryavāpteṣu, cintiteṣu |
The person who, in the dharmas as heard, mastered, reflected on,
sbh2.0:9.3
tuliteṣūpaparīkṣiteṣu, svayameva śakto bhavati |
weighed, [and] examined, is himself capable,
sbh2.0:9.4
pratibalastāndharmānbhāvanākāreṇānusarttam |
fit to follow those dharmas by way of cultivation, [and]
sbh2.0:10.1
no tu parato ’vavādānuśāsanīmparyeṣate |
does not seek instruction-and-admonition from another,
sbh2.0:10.2
yaduta phalasyādhigamāyāyamucyate dharmānusārī pudgalaḥ |
namely, for the attainment of the [path-]fruit — this is called the dharma-follower person.
sbh2.0:10.3
śraddhādhimuktaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the faith-resolved person (śraddhādhimukta)?
sbh2.0:10.4
sa śraddhānusārī pudgalaḥ |
That faith-follower person,
sbh2.0:10.5
yasminsamaye śrāmaṇya bhāva madhigacchati |
at the moment when he attains [Conjectural: the recluse-fruit],
Printed śrāmaṇya[bhāva]m, the bhāva supplied by Shukla in a damaged place; read śrāmaṇyaphalam (11.3; Xuanzang 沙門果).
sbh2.0:10.6
sparśayati spṛśati |
touches it,
sbh2.0:10.7
sākṣīkaroti |
[and] realizes it
sbh2.0:10.8
paratovavādānuśāsanīmārgasya tasminsamaye śraddhādhimukta ityucyate |
by the path of instruction-and-admonition from another — at that moment he is called faith-resolved.
sbh2.0:11.1
dṛṣṭiprāptaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the view-attainer person (dṛṣṭiprāpta)?
sbh2.0:11.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:11.3
sa eva dharmānusārī pudgalo yasmin samaye śrāmaṇyaphalamadhigacchati |
that very dharma-follower person, at the moment when he attains the recluse-fruit,
sbh2.0:11.4
sparśayati spṛśati |
touches it,
sbh2.0:11.5
sākṣīkaroti |
[and] realizes it
sbh2.0:11.6
paratovavādānuśāsanīmārgasya tasminsamaye dṛṣṭiprāpta ityucyate |
— at that moment he is called view-attainer.
Printed parato ’vavādānuśāsanīmārgasya, repeated from 10.8, is not rendered; Xuanzang’s definition (見至) lacks it.
sbh2.0:12.1
kāyasākṣī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the body-witness person?
sbh2.0:12.2
yoyaṁ pudgalaḥ |
This person
sbh2.0:12.3
anulomapratilomamaṣṭau vimokṣānsamāpadyate |
attains the eight liberations (vimokṣa) in forward and reverse order,
sbh2.0:12.4
vyuttiṣṭhate ca, kāyena ca sākṣātkṛtya bahulaṁ viharati, na ca sarveṇa sarvamāsravakṣayamanuprāpnotyayamucyate kāyasākṣī pudgalaḥ |
and rises out [of them], and, having realized [them] with the body, dwells much [therein], but does not entirely attain the destruction of the fluxes — this is called the body-witness person.
sbh2.0:13.1
saptakṛdbhavaparamaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the seven-times-at-most person (saptakṛdbhavaparama)?
sbh2.0:13.2
yoyaṁ pudgalastrayāṇāṁ saṁyojanānāṁ prahāṇātsatkāyadṛṣṭeḥ, śīlavrataparāmarśasya, vicikitsāyāḥ strota āpanno bhavati |
This person, by the abandonment of the three fetters — personality-view (satkāya-dṛṣṭi), clinging-to-virtue-and-vow (śīlavrata-parāmarśa), [and] doubt (vicikitsā) — becomes a stream-enterer,
sbh2.0:13.3
avinipātadharmā, niyataḥ saṁbodhiparāyaṇaḥ |
of non-falling nature, certain, bound for awakening.
sbh2.0:13.4
saptakṛdbhavaparamaḥ |
[As] one whose maximum is seven times,
sbh2.0:13.5
saptakṛtvā devāṁśca, manuṣyāṁśca, saṁbādhya, saṁsṛtya duḥkhasyāntaṁ karoti |
[after] seven times transmigrating among the gods and humans, he makes an end of suffering. [Lacuna]
Printed text omits the definition of the family-to-family person; Xuanzang has it in two kinds, divine and human (天家家、人家家), the ‘both’ of 13.6 (T1579 juan 26).
sbh2.0:13.6
ubhāvapi strota āpannau pudgalau veditavyau |
Both are to be understood as stream-enterer persons.
sbh2.0:13.7
tatraikavīcikaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
Now, what is the one-interval person (ekavīcika)?
sbh2.0:13.8
yasya sakṛdāgāminaḥ pudgalasya anāgāmiphalapratipannakasya kāmāvacarāṇāṁ kleśānāmadhimātramadhyadeśāḥ prahīṇā bhavanti |
The once-returner person practicing for the fruit of non-returning, for whom the strong and middling realm-of-desire defilements are abandoned,
sbh2.0:13.9
mṛdukāścaikāvaśiṣṭā caikā avaśiṣṭā bhavanti |
[and] only the mild ones remain;
sbh2.0:13.10
sakṛcca kāmāvacarameva bhavamabhinirvṛtya tatraiva parinirvāti |
and, having produced only one realm-of-desire existence, there itself he attains final nirvāṇa.
sbh2.0:13.11
sa punaḥ sakṛdāgacchatīmaṁ lokamayamucyate ekavīcikaḥ |
He once returns to this [world] — this is called the one-interval [one].
sbh2.0:14.1
antarāparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the final-nirvāṇa-in-between person (antarāparinirvāyin)?
sbh2.0:14.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:14.3
antarāparinirvāyiṇaḥ pudgalāstrayaḥ |
[there are] three [kinds of] final-nirvāṇa-in-between persons.
sbh2.0:14.4
ekontarāparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ |
The first in-between-nirvāṇa person:
sbh2.0:14.5
cyutamātra evāntarābhavābhinirvṛttikāle antarābhavamabhinirvatayatyabhinirvṛtte ḥ samakālameva parinirvāti |
just upon decease, at the time of intermediate-existence-generation, [he] generates the intermediate existence and at the very moment of generation attains final nirvāṇa —
sbh2.0:14.6
tadyathā parīttaḥ śakalikāgnirutpannaiva nna eva parinirvāti |
like a small spark of fire, which, when arisen, is at once extinguished.
sbh2.0:14.7
dvitīyontarāparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ |
The second in-between-nirvāṇa person:
sbh2.0:14.8
antarābhavamabhinirvarttayatyabhinirvṛtteḥ samakālā kālama ntarā bhavettatrastha eva kālāntareṇa parinirvāti |
having generated the intermediate existence, [and] standing there some time after the generation, after some time attains final nirvāṇa —
sbh2.0:14.9
no tu yenopapattibhavastenāsyāpyuparato bhavati |
but not [so that] he has [Conjectural: approached] where the rebirth-existence is —
Printed uparato; read upanato (14.11; Xuanzang 未趣生有).
sbh2.0:14.10
tadyathā aśubhānāmvā ayaḥsthālānāmvā, dīptāgnisaṁprataptānāmayoghanairhanyamānānāmayaḥprapāṭikā utpatatyeva vaṁ parinirvāti |
like an iron spark that flies out from [Conjectural: iron lumps] or iron plates, heated glowing and beaten with iron hammers — so he attains final nirvāṇa.
Printed aśubhānāṁ, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 鐵摶 (T1579 juan 26).
sbh2.0:14.11
tṛtīyontarāparinirvāyi yī pudgalaḥ antarābhavamabhinirvarttya yenopapatti bhavastenopanamati |
The third in-between-nirvāṇa person: having generated the intermediate existence, he approaches [the place] where the rebirth-existence [will be],
sbh2.0:14.12
upanataśca punaranupapanna eva parinirvāti |
and, having approached, [but] not yet reborn, attains final nirvāṇa —
sbh2.0:14.13
tadyathā |
like
sbh2.0:14.14
ayasprapāṭikā utpatya pṛthivyām |
an iron-spark that has flown out [and], before falling on the earth,
sbh2.0:14.15
apatitaivamabhinirvāti |
[is extinguished] — [thus] he attains final nirvāṇa.
sbh2.0:14.16
ta ime trayo ’ntarābhavaparinirvāyiṇaḥ pudgalāḥ ekatyamabhi saṁkṣipya antarāparinirvāyī pudgala ityucyate |
These three intermediate-existence-nirvāṇa persons, gathered into one, are called the in-between-nirvāṇa person.
sbh2.0:14.17
upapadyaparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the upon-rebirth-nirvāṇa person (upapadya-parinirvāyin)?
sbh2.0:14.18
ya upapannamātra eva parinirvāti |
He who, just upon being reborn, attains final nirvāṇa.
sbh2.0:15.1
anabhisaṁskāraparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the without-effort-nirvāṇa person (an-abhisaṁskāra-parinirvāyin)?
sbh2.0:15.2
yonabhisaṁskāreṇāprayatnenākhedena mārgaṁ saṁmukhīkṛtya tatropapannaḥ parinirvātyayamucyate anabhisaṁskāraparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ |
He who, reborn there, brings the path into presence without effort, without exertion, without exhaustion, and attains final nirvāṇa — this is called the without-effort-nirvāṇa person.
sbh2.0:15.3
sābhisaṁskāraparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the with-effort-nirvāṇa person (sābhisaṁskāra-parinirvāyin)?
sbh2.0:15.4
yobhisaṁskāreṇa prayatnena khedamārgaṁ saṁmukhīkṛtya tatropapannaḥ parinirvātyayamucyate sābhisaṁskāraparinirvāyī pudgalaḥ |
He who, reborn there, brings the path into presence with effort, with exertion, with exhaustion, and attains final nirvāṇa — this is called the with-effort-nirvāṇa person.
sbh2.0:16.1
ūrdhvasrotāḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the upstream-goer person (ūrdhvasrotas)?
sbh2.0:16.2
yaḥ pudgalo ’nāgāmī |
The person who is a non-returner,
sbh2.0:16.3
prathame dhyāne upapannaḥ sa na tatrastha eva parinirvāti |
[and] who, reborn in the first dhyāna, does not attain final nirvāṇa there itself,
sbh2.0:16.4
api tu tasmāccyavitvā uttarottaramabhinirvarttayabhya nyā vadakaniṣṭhānvā devāngacchati |
but, having passed away from there, by progressively higher rebirth, goes up to the Akaniṣṭha gods,
sbh2.0:16.5
naiva saṁjñā |
or [up to] the neither-perception-
sbh2.0:17.1
nāsaṁjñāyatanādvā |
nor-non-perception sphere.
sbh2.0:17.2
ayamucyate urdhvasrotāḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the upstream-goer person.
sbh2.0:17.3
samayavimuktaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the sometimes-liberated person (samaya-vimukta)?
sbh2.0:17.4
yo mṛdvindviyagātraḥ gotraḥ pudgalaḥ laukikebhyo dṛṣṭadharmasukhavihārebhyaḥ parihīyate |
The mild-faculty-lineage person who falls back from the worldly seen-dharma pleasure-dwellings (dṛṣṭa-dharma-sukha-vihāra),
sbh2.0:17.5
cetayati vā maraṇāya |
or thinks of [his own] death,
sbh2.0:17.6
anurakṣate vā vimuktimatyarthapramādabhayāpannayukto bhavati |
or protects [his] liberation, [having] fallen into intense fear of heedlessness;
sbh2.0:17.7
yaduta etāmeva parihāṇimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā tanmātro vāsya kuśalapakṣo bhavati |
namely, taking that very [tendency to] falling-back as predominant, [only] that much is his wholesome side,
sbh2.0:17.8
no tu teṣāṁ teṣāṁ rātriṁdivasānāṁ kṣaṇalavamuhūrttānā ṇā matyayādatyarthaṁ viśeṣāya paraiti |
and, through the passing of those nights and days, moments, instants, [and] hours, he does not greatly proceed to distinction,
sbh2.0:17.9
yāvanna tīvramabhiyogaṁ karoti |
so long as he does not make intense effort.
sbh2.0:17.10
ayamucyate samayavimuktaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the sometimes-liberated person.
sbh2.0:18.1
akopyadharmā pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the unshakable-dharma person (akopya-dharman)?
sbh2.0:18.2
etadviparyayeṇā ko ’pyadharmā pudgalo veditavyaḥ |
By the reverse of [the above], the unshakable-dharma person is to be understood.
sbh2.0:19.1
prajñāvimuktaḥ pudgala katamaḥ |
What is the wisdom-liberated person (prajñā-vimukta)?
sbh2.0:19.2
yaḥ pudgalaḥ sarveṇa sarvamāsravakṣayamanuprāpnoti |
The person who entirely attains the destruction of the fluxes,
sbh2.0:19.3
no tvaṣṭau vimokṣān kāyena sākṣātkṛtyopasampadya viharati |
but does not dwell, having attained, realizing the eight liberations with the body.
sbh2.0:19.4
ayamucyate prajñāvimukti ktaḥ pudgalaḥ |
This is called the wisdom-liberated person.
sbh2.0:19.5
ubhayatobhāgavimuktaḥ pudgalaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the both-ways-liberated person (ubhayato-bhāga-vimukta)?
sbh2.0:19.6
yaḥ pudgala sarveṇa sarvamāsravakṣayamanu prāpnoti |
The person who entirely attains the destruction of the fluxes [and]
sbh2.0:19.7
aṣṭau vimokṣānkāyenopasampadya viharati |
dwells having attained the eight liberations with the body.
sbh2.0:19.8
tasya kleśāvaraṇācca cittaṁ muktaṁ bhavati |
His mind is liberated from the obstacle of defilement (kleśa-āvaraṇa)
sbh2.0:19.9
vimokṣāvaraṇāccā yamucya te ubhayatobhāgavimuktaḥ pudgalaḥ |
and from the obstacle to liberation (vimokṣa-āvaraṇa) — this is called the both-ways-liberated person.
sbh2.0:20.1
pudgalavyavasthānaṁ katamat |
What is the delineation of persons?
sbh2.0:20.2
ekādaśabhiḥ prabhedaiḥ pudgalavyavasthānaṁ veditavyam |
The delineation of persons is to be understood by eleven divisions.
sbh2.0:20.3
katamairekādaśabhistadyathā indriyaprabhedena, nikāyaprabhedena, caritaprabhedena praṇidhānaprabhedena prapripatprabhedena mārgaphala prabhedena prayogaprabhedena |
By which eleven? Namely: by division of faculty, by division of community (nikāya), by division of conduct, by division of vow, by division of practice, by division of path-fruit, by division of application,
Printed prapripat-; read pratipat- (30.1).
sbh2.0:20.4
samāpattiprabhedena upapattiprabhedena parihāṇi prabhedenā varaṇaprabhedena ca |
by division of attainment, by division of rebirth, by division of falling-back, [and] by division of obstacle.
sbh2.0:21.1
indriyaprabhedena tāvat |
By division of faculty,
sbh2.0:21.2
mṛdvindriyastī yatī kṣṇendriyapudgalayorvyavasthānaṁ |
[there is] the delineation of mild-faculty and sharp-faculty persons.
sbh2.0:22.1
nikāyaprabhedena saptavidhapudgalavyavasthānaṁ |
By division of community (nikāya), the delineation of seven kinds of person:
sbh2.0:22.2
bhikṣu r bhikṣuṇī, śikṣamāṇā śrāmaṇeraḥ śrāmaṇerī upāsaka upāsikā ca |
monk, nun, female probationer, male novice, female novice, layman, laywoman.
sbh2.0:23.1
tatra caritaprabhedena saptānāṁ pudgalānāṁ vyavasthānaṁ |
Now, by division of conduct, the delineation of seven persons:
sbh2.0:23.2
yoyaṁ rāgonmadaḥ pudgalaḥ sa rāgacaritaḥ |
he who is the lust-intoxicated person is lust-conducted;
sbh2.0:23.3
yo dveṣonmadaḥ sa dveṣacaritaḥ |
he who is hatred-intoxicated is hatred-conducted;
sbh2.0:23.4
yo mohonmadaḥ sa mohacaritaḥ |
he who is delusion-intoxicated is delusion-conducted;
sbh2.0:23.5
yo mānonmadaḥ sa mānacaritaḥ |
he who is conceit-intoxicated is conceit-conducted;
sbh2.0:23.6
yo vitarkonmadaḥ sa vitarkacaritaḥ |
he who is applied-thought-intoxicated is applied-thought-conducted;
sbh2.0:23.7
yaḥ samaprāptaḥ samabhāgacaritaḥ |
the balanced person (samaprāpta) is even-balanced in conduct;
sbh2.0:23.8
yo mandarajaskaḥ so mandacarito veditavyaḥ |
the slight-dust person (mandarajaska) is slight in conduct — [thus they are] to be understood.
sbh2.0:24.1
tatra rāgacaritasya pudgalasya katamāni liṁgāni |
Now, what are the marks of the lust-conducted person?
sbh2.0:24.2
iha rāgacaritaḥ pudgalaḥ parītte sarvanihīne raṁjanīye vastuni ghanamadhimātraṁ rāgaparyavasthānamutpādayati |
Here the lust-conducted person, [even] toward a slight, wholly inferior lust-arousing thing, produces a dense, strong lust-obsession —
sbh2.0:24.3
kaḥ punarvādo madhyapraṇīte |
what more toward a middle or fine [thing]?
sbh2.0:24.4
tacca punārāgaparyavasthānaṁ saṁtatyā cirakālamavasthāpayati |
And that lust-obsession he maintains in continuity for a long time,
sbh2.0:24.5
dīrghakālamanubaddho bhavati |
[remains] bound [by it] for a long time.
sbh2.0:24.6
tena paryavasthānena, raṁjanīyairdharmairabhibhūyate |
Through that obsession, he is overcome by lust-arousing dharmas,
sbh2.0:24.7
no tu śakto a tiraṁjanīyāndharmānabhibhavituṁ |
but he is not able to overcome the lust-arousing dharmas.
sbh2.0:24.8
snigdhendriyaśca bhavatyakharendriyaḥ |
He is of smooth faculties, not of stiff faculties,
sbh2.0:24.9
karkaśendriyaḥ, aparuddhendriyaḥ |
[Conjectural: not] of rough faculties, not of [Conjectural: coarse] faculties;
Printed karkaśendriyaḥ, aparuddhendriyaḥ; read akarkaśendriyaḥ, aparuṣendriyaḥ (24.27; Xuanzang 不澀、不麁).
sbh2.0:24.10
nātyarthaṁ pareṣāṁ viheṭhanajātīyo yaduta kāyena, vācā durvivejyaśca bhavati, duḥsaṁvejyaśca |
not too much harming others — namely by body or by speech; [yet he is] hard to detach, hard to stir;
sbh2.0:24.11
hīnādhimuktikaśca bhavati |
of inferior conviction;
sbh2.0:24.12
dṛḍhakarmāntaḥ |
firm in undertakings,
sbh2.0:24.13
sthirakarmāntaḥ |
steady in undertakings;
sbh2.0:24.14
dṛḍhavrataḥ |
of firm vow,
sbh2.0:24.15
sthiravrataḥ |
of stable vow;
sbh2.0:24.16
mahiṣṭhaśca bhavatyupakaraṇeṣu pariṣkāreṣu lolupajātīyastadgurukaśca, saumanasyabahulaśca bhavatyānandībahulo vigatabhṛkuṭiruttānamukhavarṇaḥ, smitapūrvaṁgama ityevaṁbhāgīyāni rāgacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni veditavyāni |
magnanimous; greedy by nature for requisites and provisions, giving great weight to them; full of joy, full of delight, free from frowning, of open-faced complexion, accompanied by a smile — these and the like are to be understood as the marks of the lust-conducted person.
sbh2.0:24.17
dveṣacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni katamāni |
What are the marks of the hatred-conducted person?
sbh2.0:24.18
iha dveṣacaritaḥ pudgalaḥ |
Here the hatred-conducted person,
sbh2.0:24.19
dveṣaṇīye vastuni parīttena pratighavastunimittena ghanaṁ,prabhūtaṁ pratighaparyavasthānamutpādayati |
toward a hatred-arousing thing, [even] by a slight sign of an aversion-provoking thing (pratigha-vastu-nimitta), produces a dense, abundant aversion-obsession —
sbh2.0:24.20
kaḥ punarvādo madhyādhimātre ṇa |
what more by a middle or strong [one]?
sbh2.0:24.21
tasya ca pratighaparyavasthānasya dīrghakālaṁ santatimavasthāpayati |
And that aversion-obsession he maintains in continuity for a long time,
sbh2.0:24.22
cirakālamanubaddho bhavati |
[remains] bound [by it] for a long time.
sbh2.0:24.23
pratighaparyavasthānena |
Through aversion-obsession,
sbh2.0:24.24
sa dveṣaṇīyairdharmairabhibhūyate |
he is overcome by hatred-arousing dharmas,
sbh2.0:24.25
no tu dveṣaṇīyāndharmānchakno ñchakno ti abhibhavituṁ |
but cannot overcome the hatred-arousing dharmas.
sbh2.0:24.26
rukṣendriyaśca bhavati |
He is of withered faculties,
sbh2.0:24.27
kharendriyaḥ, karkaśendriyaḥ paruṣendriyaśca bhavatyatyarthaṁ pareṣāṁ viheṭhanajātīyo bhavati |
of stiff faculties, of rough faculties, of coarse faculties, and is excessively harming others by nature —
sbh2.0:24.28
yaduta kāyena, vācā |
namely by body and by speech.
sbh2.0:24.29
suvivenya jya śca bhavati |
Yet he is easy to detach,
sbh2.0:24.30
susaṁvejyaḥ |
easy to stir;
sbh2.0:24.31
dhvāṅkṣo bhavati mukharaḥ |
[but] he is clamorous, talkative,
sbh2.0:24.32
pragalbhaḥ anadhimuktibahulaḥ |
brazen, of much non-conviction;
sbh2.0:24.33
na dṛḍhakarmānto, nasthirakarmāntaḥ |
not firm in undertakings, not steady in undertakings;
sbh2.0:24.34
na dṛḍhavrato, na sthiravrataḥ |
not of firm vow, not of stable vow;
sbh2.0:24.35
daurmanasyabahulaśca bhavatyupāyāsabahulaḥ |
full of dejection, full of distress;
sbh2.0:24.36
akṣamo bhavatyamahiṣṭhaḥ |
impatient, not magnanimous;
sbh2.0:24.37
vilomanajātīyaḥ |
of perverse nature;
sbh2.0:24.38
apradakṣiṇagrāhī duḥpratyāneya jātīya upanāhabahulaḥ |
one who takes things the wrong way, hard to bring around, full of grudges;
sbh2.0:24.39
krūrāśaṁsaścaṇḍaśca bhavatyādā yī pratyakṣaravādī so ’lpamātramapyuktaḥ sannabhiṣajyate |
of cruel intent, fierce, taking offense, replying word-for-word — even when slightly addressed, he is offended,
sbh2.0:24.40
kupyati |
becomes angry,
sbh2.0:24.41
vyāpadyate |
bears ill will;
sbh2.0:24.42
madgu ḥ pratitiṣṭhati |
sullen, [he] stands [thus],
sbh2.0:24.43
kopaṁ saṁja na yati |
generates anger;
sbh2.0:24.44
vi kṛtabhṛkuṭiśca bhavati |
of frown-marked face,
sbh2.0:24.45
anuttānamukhavarṇa ḥ parasampattidveṣṭā, īrṣyābahula ityevaṁbhāgīyāni dveṣa caritasya pudgalasya liṅgāni veditavyāni |
of non-open face, hating others’ fortune, full of envy — these and the like are to be understood as the marks of the hatred-conducted person.
sbh2.0:25.1
tatra katamāni mohacaritasya pudgalasya liṅgāni |
Now, what are the marks of the delusion-conducted person?
sbh2.0:25.2
iha moha caritaḥ pudgalaḥ mohasthānīye vastuni parīttaṁ, ghanaṁ, prabhūtaṁ mohaparyavasthānamutpādayati |
Here the delusion-conducted person, [even] toward a slight delusion-arousing thing, produces a dense, abundant delusion-obsession —
sbh2.0:25.3
prāgeva madhyādhimātre, dṛḍhaṁ ca kālaṁ tasya mohaparyavasthānasya santatimavasthāpayati |
[and] still more toward a middle or strong [thing]; and he maintains the continuity of that delusion-obsession for a long time,
sbh2.0:25.4
tena cānubaddho bhavati |
and is bound by it.
sbh2.0:25.5
sa mohanīyairdharmairabhibhūyate |
He is overcome by delusion-arousing dharmas,
sbh2.0:25.6
no tu mohanīyān dharmāṁcchaknotyabhibhavituṁ dharmāñchaknotyabhibhavituṁ |
but cannot overcome the delusion-arousing dharmas.
sbh2.0:25.7
baddhe dhandhe ndriyaśca bhavati |
He is of slow faculties,
sbh2.0:25.8
jaḍendriyaśca bhavati |
of dull faculties,
sbh2.0:25.9
mattendriyaśca, śithilakāyakaryānta nto duścintitacintī, durbhāṣitabhāṣī, duṣkṛtakarmakārī, alaso ’nutthānasampannaḥ |
of intoxicated faculties, of loose bodily action; an ill-thought-thinker, ill-speech-speaker, ill-deed-doer; lazy, not endowed with [the capacity for] rising;
sbh2.0:25.10
mandabhāgī, durmedho dhaḥ |
of limited capacity, dull-witted;
sbh2.0:25.11
muṣitasmṛtiḥ |
of lost mindfulness;
sbh2.0:25.12
asaṁprajānadvihārī |
not dwelling with full awareness;
sbh2.0:25.13
vāmagrāhī, durvivejyo, duḥsaṁvejyo, hīnādhimuktikaḥ |
a wrong-grasper, hard to detach, hard to stir, of inferior conviction;
sbh2.0:25.14
jāta eḍamūko hastasaṁbādhikaḥ |
born deaf-mute, [Conjectural: speaking by hand signs],
Printed hastasaṁbādhika, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 以手代言 (also 88.42).
sbh2.0:25.15
apratibalaḥ |
unfit
sbh2.0:25.16
subhāṣitadurbhāṣitānāmarthamājñātuṁ pratyaya pa hāyaśca bhavati |
to understand the meaning of well-spoken and ill-spoken [teachings], and by nature led along by conditions,
sbh2.0:25.17
parahāyaḥ parapraṇeya ityevaṁ bhāgīyāni mohacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni veditavyāni |
other-led, other-driven — these and the like are to be understood as the marks of the delusion-conducted person.
sbh2.0:26.1
tatra katamāni mānacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni |
Now, what are the marks of the conceit-conducted person?
sbh2.0:26.2
iha mānacaritaḥ pudgalo mānasthānīye vastuni parīttepi ghanaṁ mānaparyavasthānamutpādayati |
Here the conceit-conducted person, [even] toward a slight conceit-arousing thing, produces a dense conceit-obsession —
sbh2.0:26.3
kaḥ punarvādo madhyādhimātre |
what more toward a middle or strong [one]?
sbh2.0:26.4
tasya ca mānaparyavasthānasya dīrghakālaṁ santatimavasthāpayati |
And the continuity of that conceit-obsession he maintains for a long time,
sbh2.0:26.5
tena cānubaddho bhavati |
and is bound [by it].
sbh2.0:26.6
katamāni mānacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni |
Printed text repeats 26.1–26.5 (bracketed by Shukla).
sbh2.0:26.7
iha mānacaritaḥ pudgalaḥ mānasthānīye vastuni parīttepighanaṁ mānaparyavasthānamutpādayati kaḥ punarvādo madhyādhimātre |
sbh2.0:26.8
tasya mānaparyavasthānasya dīrghakālaṁ santatimavasthāpayati |
sbh2.0:26.9
tena cānubaddho bhavati sa mānasthānīyairdharmairabhibhūyate |
He is overcome by conceit-arousing dharmas,
sbh2.0:26.10
no tu mānasthānīyāndharmāñchaknotyabhibhavituṁ |
but cannot overcome the conceit-arousing dharmas.
sbh2.0:26.11
uddhatendriyaśca bhavatyunnatendriyaśco nnatendriyaśca kāyamaṇḍanānuyuktaśca bhavatyadhimātramunnatāñca vācaṁ bhāṣate, nāvanatāṁ, sātāpikagurusthānīyānāṁ ca na kālena kālaṁ yathārūpāmapacitiṁ kartā bhavati |
He is of restless faculties, of elevated faculties; engaged in adorning the body, he speaks an excessively haughty speech, not lowered [in tone]; and toward [Conjectural: parents,] teachers and those in the place of teacher he does not, from time to time, pay the proper respect.
Printed unnatendriya twice (bracketed by Shukla as a repetition); Xuanzang 散亂 has a third member, ‘scattered’. Printed sātāpika-, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 父母、眷屬 ‘parents, kin’.
sbh2.0:26.12
stabdho bhavati |
He is stiff,
sbh2.0:26.13
apraṇatakāyo nābhi vādanavandanapratyutthānānāṁ jālisāmīcīkarmaśīla, ātmapragrāhako bhavatyātmotkarṣo parapaṁsakaḥ |
of unbowed body, not given to salutation, bowing, rising, joined palms, and proper service; self-grasping, self-exalting, other-deprecating;
sbh2.0:26.14
lābhakāmaḥ satkārakāmaḥ kīrtiśabdaślokakāmaḥ |
desirous of gain, desirous of honor, desirous of fame, voice, [and] eulogy;
sbh2.0:26.15
utplāvanābhāṇḍonuvivedhyaśca jyaśca bhavati |
frivolous, given to jesting, [Conjectural: hard to detach],
Printed -nuvivedhyaś ca jyaś ca, corrupt; read durvivejyaś ca (24.10; Xuanzang 難使遠離).
sbh2.0:26.16
duḥsaṁvejya udārādhimuktiśca bhavati |
hard to stir, of lofty inclination,
sbh2.0:26.17
mandakāruṇyaḥ |
of weak compassion,
sbh2.0:26.18
adhimātraṁ cātmasattvajīvapo ṣapu ruṣapudgaladṛṣṭimanyubahalo bhavati |
and is full of strong views of self, being, soul, nourisher, person, and individual, and of resentment,
sbh2.0:26.19
upanāhī cetyevaṁbhāgīyāni mānacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni veditavyāni || tatra katamāni vitarkacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni |
and full of grudges — these and the like are to be understood as the marks of the conceit-conducted person. Now, what are the marks of the applied-thought-conducted person?
sbh2.0:26.20
iha vitarkacaritaḥ pudgalaḥ vitarka sthānīye vastuni parīttepi ghanaparyavasthānamutpādayati |
Here the applied-thought-conducted person, [even] toward a slight applied-thought-arousing thing, produces a dense obsession,
sbh2.0:26.21
tacca paryavasthānaṁ dīrghakālamavasthāpayati |
and that obsession he maintains for a long time,
sbh2.0:26.22
tena cānubaddho bhavati |
and is bound [by it].
sbh2.0:26.23
sa vitarkasthānīyairdharmairabhibhūyate |
He is overcome by applied-thought-arousing dharmas,
sbh2.0:26.24
no tu vitarkasthānīyān dharmāṁ rmāñ chaknotyabhibhavituṁ |
but cannot overcome the applied-thought-arousing dharmas.
sbh2.0:26.25
asthirendriyaśca bhavati |
He is of unstable faculties,
sbh2.0:26.26
capalendriyaḥ |
of fickle faculties,
sbh2.0:26.27
traṁ caṁ calendriyaḥ |
of restless faculties,
sbh2.0:26.28
vyākulendriyaḥ |
of distracted faculties;
sbh2.0:26.29
asthira kāyakarmāntaḥ |
of unstable bodily action,
sbh2.0:26.30
cha du ritavākkarmāntī, durvivejyo duḥsaṁvejyaḥ, prapaṁcārāmaḥ prapaṁcarataḥ |
[Conjectural: erring in verbal action]; hard to detach, hard to stir; fond of [conceptual] proliferation (prapaṁca), delighting in proliferation;
Printed cha du rita-vākkarmāntī, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 語業誤失.
sbh2.0:26.31
kāṁkṣābahulo, vicikitsābahulaḥ |
full of doubt, full of indecision;
sbh2.0:26.32
chandikaśca bhavatyasthiravrataḥ |
and of varied desire, of unstable vow,
sbh2.0:26.33
aniścitavrataḥ |
of undetermined vow;
sbh2.0:26.34
asthirakarmāntaḥ |
of unstable action,
sbh2.0:26.35
aniścitakarmāntaḥ |
of undetermined action;
sbh2.0:26.36
śaṁkābahulaḥ, pramuṣitasmṛtiḥ |
full of suspicion, of lost mindfulness;
sbh2.0:26.37
vivekānabhirato, vikṣepabahulaḥ |
non-delighting in seclusion, full of distraction;
sbh2.0:26.38
lokacitreṣu chandarāgānusṛtaḥ |
following desire-and-lust in worldly variety;
sbh2.0:26.39
dakṣonalasa ityevaṁ bhāgīyāni vitarkacaritasya pudgalasya liṁgāni veditavyāni |
skilful, not lazy — these and the like are to be understood as the marks of the applied-thought-conducted person.
sbh2.0:27.1
idaṁ caritaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ veditavyam |
This is the delineation of persons by division of conduct, [thus] to be understood.
sbh2.0:28.1
tatra praṇidhānaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ |
Now, the delineation of persons by division of vow (praṇidhāna):
sbh2.0:28.2
asti pudgalaḥ śrāvakayāne kṛtapraṇidhānaḥ |
there is a person who has made his vow in the disciple-vehicle,
sbh2.0:28.3
asti pratyekabuddhayāne |
there is one in the solitary-buddha-vehicle,
sbh2.0:28.4
asti mahāyāne |
[and] there is one in the great vehicle.
sbh2.0:28.5
tatra yoyaṁ pudgalaḥ |
Now, this person
sbh2.0:28.6
śrāvakayāne kṛtapraṇidhānaḥ samyakchrāvakagotraḥ syātpratyekabuddhagotraḥ |
who has made his vow in the disciple-vehicle may be of disciple-lineage, may be of solitary-buddha-lineage,
sbh2.0:28.7
syānmahāyānagotraḥ |
[or] may be of great-vehicle-lineage.
sbh2.0:28.8
tatrayoyaṁ pudgalaḥ pratyekāyāṁ bodhau kṛtapraṇidhānaḥ so ’pi syātpratyekabuddhagotraḥ, syācchāvakagotraḥ, syānmahāyānagotraḥ |
[Likewise,] this person who has made his vow in [the path of] solitary awakening may also be of solitary-buddha-lineage, of disciple-lineage, [or] of great-vehicle-lineage.
sbh2.0:28.9
tatra yoyaṁ pudgalo mahāyāne kṛtapraṇidhānaḥ so ’pi syāchrāvakagotraḥ, syātpratyekabuddhagotraḥ, syānmahāyānagotraḥ |
[And] this person who has made his vow in the great vehicle may also be of disciple-lineage, may be of solitary-buddha-lineage, [or] may be of great-vehicle-lineage.
sbh2.0:28.10
tatra yoyaṁ śrāvakagotraḥ pudgalaḥ pratyekayāmbodhau, anuttarāyāmvā samyaksaṁbodhau kṛtapraṇidhānaḥ |
[Now,] this disciple-lineage person who has made his vow in solitary awakening or in supreme perfect awakening —
sbh2.0:28.11
sa śrāvakagotratvādavaśyamante kāle tatpraṇidhānaṁ vyāvartyaṁ śrāvakayānapraṇidhāna evāvatiṣṭhate |
he, on account of his disciple-lineage, necessarily at the final time will turn back that vow and stand only in the disciple-vehicle vow.
sbh2.0:28.12
evaṁ pratyekabuddhayānagotro mahāyānagotro veditavyaḥ |
Thus the solitary-buddha-lineage and great-vehicle-lineage [persons] are to be understood.
sbh2.0:28.13
tatra bhavatyeṣāṁ pudgalānāṁ praṇidhānasaṁbhāraḥ praṇidhānavyatikaraḥ |
For these persons there is accumulation of the vow and alteration of the vow,
sbh2.0:28.14
no tu gotrasaṁbhāro, gotravyatikaraḥ |
but no accumulation of lineage or alteration of lineage.
sbh2.0:28.15
asmiṁstvarthe śrāvakayānapraṇidhānā ḥ śrāvakagotrāścaite pudgalā veditavyāḥ |
In this context, these persons are to be understood as of disciple-vehicle vow and of disciple-lineage.
sbh2.0:29.1
evaṁ praṇidhānaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of vow.
sbh2.0:30.1
kathaṁ pratipatprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
How is the delineation of persons by division of practice (pratipat-prabheda)?
sbh2.0:30.2
eṣāṁ yathoddiṣṭānāṁ yathāparikīrttitānāṁ pudgalānāṁ catasṛbhiḥ pratipadbhirniryāṇaṁ bhavati |
Of these persons just pointed out [and] proclaimed, [their] going forth is by four practices.
sbh2.0:30.3
katamābhiścatasṛbhiḥ |
By which four?
sbh2.0:30.4
asti pratipad duḥkhā dhandhābhijñā |
There is the painful practice with slow knowledge;
sbh2.0:30.5
asti pratipad duḥkhā kṣiprābhijñā |
there is the painful practice with quick knowledge;
sbh2.0:30.6
asti pratipatsukhā dhandhābhijñā asti pratipatsukhā kṣiprābhijñā |
there is the pleasant practice with slow knowledge; [and] there is the pleasant practice with quick knowledge.
sbh2.0:31.1
tasya mṛdvindriyasya pudgalasya mauladhyānalābhino yā pratipadiyamucyate duḥkhā dhandhābhijñā |
Of the mild-faculty person [Conjectural: who has not attained the basic dhyāna], the practice is called painful, with slow knowledge.
Printed mauladhyānalābhino (31.1, 31.2), as in 31.3–4; Xuanzang has 未得根本靜慮 ‘not having attained the basic dhyāna’ for the painful pair.
sbh2.0:31.2
tatra tīkṣṇendriyasya pudgalasya mauladhyānalābhino yā pratipadiyamucyate duḥkhā kṣiprābhijñā |
Of the sharp-faculty person [Conjectural: who has not attained the basic dhyāna], the practice is called painful, with quick knowledge.
sbh2.0:31.3
tatra mṛdvindriyasya pudgalasya mauladhyānalābhino yā pratipadiyamucyate sukhā dhandhābhijñā |
Of the mild-faculty person who has attained the basic dhyāna (mauladhyāna), the practice is called pleasant, with slow knowledge.
sbh2.0:31.4
tatra tīkṣṇendrisya pudgalasya mauladhyānalābhino yā pratipadiyamucyate sukhā kṣiprābhijñā |
Of the sharp-faculty person who has attained the basic dhyāna, the practice is called pleasant, with quick knowledge.
sbh2.0:31.5
evaṁ pratipatprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ veditavyaṁ |
Thus the delineation of persons by division of practice is to be understood.
sbh2.0:32.1
tatra kathaṁ mārgaphalaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ veditavyaṁ |
Now, how is the delineation of persons by division of path-fruit to be understood?
sbh2.0:32.2
tadyathā |
Namely,
sbh2.0:32.3
caturṇāṁ pratipannakānāṁ strota āpattiphalapratipannakasya sakṛdāgāmiphalapratipannakasya anāgāmiphalapratipannakasya, arhatta arhattva phalapratipannakasya, caturṇāṁ phalasthānāṁ srota āpannasya, sakṛdāgāminaḥ |
of the four practitioners — practitioner for the fruit of stream-entry, of once-returning, of non-returning, [and] of arhatship — and of the four fruit-attainers — stream-enterer, once-returner,
sbh2.0:32.4
anāgāminorhataśca ye pratipannakamārgā vartante |
non-returner, [and] arhat — and of those for whom the practitioner-paths [still] proceed:
sbh2.0:32.5
te pratipannakāsteṣāṁ pratipannakamārgeṇa vyavasthānaṁ |
they are practitioners; their delineation is by the practitioner-path.
sbh2.0:32.6
ye ’rhatphalaśrāmaṇyaphalavyavasthitāsteṣāṁ mārgapha lavyavasthānamevaṁ mārgaphalaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Those established in the recluse-fruits, [up to] the arhat-fruit — their delineation is by the path-fruit. Thus is the delineation of persons by division of path-fruit.
sbh2.0:33.1
kathaṁ prayogaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
How is the delineation of persons by division of application?
sbh2.0:33.2
tadyathā śraddhānusāridharmānusāriṇā |
Namely, by [the categories of] faith-follower and dharma-follower.
sbh2.0:33.3
yaḥ pudgalaḥ śraddhānusāreṇa prayuktaḥ |
The person who is applied [to the path] by faith-following
sbh2.0:33.4
sa śraddhānusārī, yo dharmeṣu parapratyayavinayānusāreṇa prayuktaḥ sa dharmānusārī |
is the faith-follower; the one applied to the dharmas by following a discipline [Conjectural: not] dependent on another’s condition is the dharma-follower.
Printed parapratyaya-; read aparapratyaya- (Xuanzang 不待他緣).
sbh2.0:33.5
evaṁ prayogaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of application.
sbh2.0:33.6
tatra kathaṁ samāpattiprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Now, how is the delineation of persons by division of attainment (samāpatti)?
sbh2.0:33.7
tadyathā kāyasākṣiṇaḥ sākṣī aṣṭau vimokṣān kāyena sākṣātkṛtyopasampadya viharati |
Namely, of the body-witness, who, having realized the eight liberations with the body, dwells having attained [them],
sbh2.0:33.8
na ca sarveṇa sarvamāsravakṣayamanuprāpto bhavati |
and does not entirely attain the destruction of the fluxes.
sbh2.0:33.9
rūpī rūpāṇi paśyati |
[Being] formed [himself], he sees forms.
sbh2.0:33.10
adhyātmamarūpasaṁjñāṁ bahirdhā rūpāṇi paśyati |
Inwardly with non-form perception, [yet] externally he sees forms.
sbh2.0:33.11
śubhavimokṣaṁ kāyena sākṣātkṛtyopasampadya viharati |
He realizes the pure liberation with the body and dwells having attained [it].
sbh2.0:33.12
ākāśānantyāyatanaṁ, vijñānānantyāyatanaṁ, ākiñcanyāyatanaṁ, naiva saṁjñā nāsaṁjñāyatanaṁ, saṁjñāvedayitanirodhamanulomapratilomaṁ samāpadyate ca, vyuttiṣṭhate ca |
The sphere of infinite space, of infinite consciousness, of nothingness, of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, and the cessation of perception-and-feeling he attains and rises out of, in forward and reverse order.
sbh2.0:33.13
evaṁ samāpattiprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of attainment.
sbh2.0:33.14
kathamupapattiprabhedena |
How is the delineation of persons by division of rebirth?
sbh2.0:33.15
pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
sbh2.0:33.16
tadyathā saptakṛdbhavaparamasya, kulaṁkulasya, ekavīcikasyāntarāparinirvāyiṇaḥ upapadyaparinirvāyiṇaḥ, ūrdhvaṁsrotasaśca |
Namely, [the delineation] of the seven-times-at-most, of the family-to-family, of the one-interval, of the in-between-nirvāṇa, of the upon-rebirth-nirvāṇa, [Lacuna] [and] of the upstream-goer.
Printed list omits two members after the upon-rebirth one; Xuanzang has 無行般涅槃、有行般涅槃, the without-effort and with-effort ones (15.1–15.4).
sbh2.0:33.17
evamupapattiprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of rebirth.
sbh2.0:33.18
kathamaparihāṇiprabhedena |
How is the delineation of persons by division of falling-back [and non-falling-back]?
sbh2.0:33.19
pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
sbh2.0:33.20
tadyathā samayavimuktasyārhataḥ yo bhavyo dṛṣṭadharmasukhavihārebhyaḥ parihāṇāya, aparihāṇiprabhedena punarvyavasthānaṁ |
Namely, of the sometimes-liberated arhat, who is liable to fall back from the pleasant dwellings of the present life; and the delineation by the division of non-falling-back [is]
sbh2.0:34.1
akopyadharmakasyārhataḥ yo na bhavyo dṛṣṭadharmasukhavihārebhyaḥ parihāṇāya |
of the unshakable arhat, who is not liable to fall back from the pleasant dwellings of the present life.
sbh2.0:34.2
eva maparihāṇiprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of falling-back [and non-falling-back].
sbh2.0:34.3
tatra kathamāvaraṇaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Now, how is the delineation of persons by division of obstacle (āvaraṇa)?
sbh2.0:34.4
tadyathā prajñāvimuktasyobhayatobhāgavimuktasyārhataḥ |
Namely, of the wisdom-liberated and both-ways-liberated arhats.
sbh2.0:34.5
tatra prajñāvimuktorhan kleśāvaraṇavimukto, na samāpattyāvaraṇāt |
The wisdom-liberated arhat is liberated from the obstacle of defilement, [but] not from the obstacle of attainment (samāpatti-āvaraṇa);
sbh2.0:34.6
ubhayatobhāgavimuktastu |
the both-ways-liberated, however,
sbh2.0:34.7
arhan |
the arhat,
sbh2.0:34.8
kleśāvaraṇācca vimuktaḥ, samāpattyāvaraṇācca tasmādubhayatobhāgavimukta ityucyate |
is liberated from the obstacle of defilement and from the obstacle of attainment — therefore he is called both-ways-liberated.
sbh2.0:34.9
evamāvaraṇaprabhedena pudgalavyavasthānaṁ bhavatyebhistribhirbhedairyayoddiṣṭairyathoddiṣṭānāṁ pudgalānāṁ yathākramaṁ vyavasthānaṁ veditavyam |
Thus is the delineation of persons by division of obstacle. — By these three divisions, as pointed out, the delineation of the persons as pointed out is, in order, to be understood.
sbh2.0:35.1
tatra kleśaviśodhanamālambanaṁ katamat |
What is the defilement-purifying object?
sbh2.0:35.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:35.3
adhobhūmīnāmaudārikatvaṁ tadyathā kāmadhātau prathamadhyānasya evaṁ yāvannaiva saṁjñānāsaṁjñāyatanasya |
The grossness of the lower planes: namely, [that] of the realm of desire relative to the first dhyāna, and so on up to [that of the sphere of nothingness relative to] the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception.
sbh2.0:35.4
tatra katamā audārikatā |
What, in this context, is grossness?
sbh2.0:35.5
audārikatā dvividhā |
Grossness is of two kinds:
sbh2.0:35.6
svabhāvodārikatā svabhāvaudārikatā saṁkhyaudārikatā ca |
grossness in essential nature (svabhāva-audārika), and grossness in number/quantity (saṁkhyā-audārika).
sbh2.0:35.7
tatra svabhāvaudārikatā kāmadhātāvapi pañca skandhāḥ saṁvidyante |
As for grossness in essential nature: in the realm of desire the five aggregates are [also] present,
sbh2.0:35.8
prathame tu dhyāne ye kāmāvacarāste sādīnavatarāśca duḥkhavihārata rāś ca |
but relative to the first dhyāna, those that pertain to the realm of desire are more flawed and more painful in their abiding,
sbh2.0:35.9
alpakāvasthāyitarāśca |
and shorter-lasting,
sbh2.0:35.10
hīnatarāḥ pratikruṣṭatarāśca |
and inferior, and more rejected.
sbh2.0:35.11
iyameṣāṁ svabhāvaudārikatā prathame tu dhyāne |
This is their grossness in essential nature relative to the first dhyāna;
sbh2.0:35.12
tathā tena te śāntatarāḥ praṇītatarā ityucyante |
and so those [of the first dhyāna] are said to be calmer and more excellent.
sbh2.0:35.13
tatra saṁkhyaudārikatā katamā |
What is grossness in quantity?
sbh2.0:35.14
kāmāvacaro rūpaskandhaḥ prabhūtataraḥ parijñeyaḥ |
The form-aggregate pertaining to the realm of desire is more abundant, to be fully known
sbh2.0:35.15
prahātavya evaṁ yāvadvijñānaskandha iyamucyate skandhaudārikatā |
and abandoned; and likewise up to the consciousness-aggregate. This is called grossness [Conjectural: in quantity].
Printed skandhaudārikatā; read saṁkhyaudārikatā (35.6; Xuanzang 數麁性).
sbh2.0:36.1
evamuparimābhūmiṣu svabhāvo vau dārikatā saṁkhyaudārikatā ca |
Likewise in the upper planes, grossness in essential nature and grossness in quantity
sbh2.0:36.2
yathāyogaṁ veditavyāḥ : |
are to be understood as appropriate to each case.
sbh2.0:36.3
iyaṁ tūparimāsu bhūmiṣu yāvadākiṁcanyāyatanā t tadaudārikata rā śca veditavyāḥ sarvā adharimā bhūmayaḥ |
But in the upper planes, [going] up to the sphere of nothingness, all the lower planes should be understood as more gross relative to those above,
sbh2.0:36.4
duḥkhavihārata rā śca alpāyuṣkatarāśca, naivasaṁjñānāsaṁjñāyatanaṁ punaḥ śāntameva upari śreṣṭhatayā bhūmerabhāvāt |
more painful in abiding, and shorter-lasting; the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception is simply calm, since there is no plane above it that is more excellent.
sbh2.0:36.5
yatra samāsata ādīnavārthaḥ |
Here the meaning of disadvantage, in brief,
sbh2.0:36.6
audārikatārthaḥ |
is the meaning of grossness:
sbh2.0:36.7
yasyāṁ yasyāṁ bhūmau prabhūtataramā ā dīnavaṁ vo bhavati |
whichever plane has greater disadvantage,
sbh2.0:36.8
sā ādīnavataḥ |
that, in respect of disadvantage,
sbh2.0:36.9
audāriketyucyate |
is called gross;
sbh2.0:36.10
yasyāṁ laukikānāṁ bhūmāvalpatara mā ā dīnavaṁ vo bhavati |
[and] the plane on which there is less disadvantage
sbh2.0:36.11
sā ādīnavataḥ śāntetyucyate |
is called, in respect of disadvantage, calm.
sbh2.0:36.12
idaṁ laukikānāṁ laukikena mārgeṇa kleśaviśodhanamālambanaṁ tathāpi tasyādharimāṁ bhūmimādīnavataḥ paśyataḥ |
This is the defilement-purifying object of worldlings, by the worldly path: for one who sees the lower plane as disadvantage,
sbh2.0:36.13
rogataḥ, ayogakṣemataḥ, uparimāṁca bhūmiṁ śāntataḥ |
as disease, as without security, and the upper plane as calm.
sbh2.0:36.14
ye adhobhūmikāḥ kleśā yāvadākiṁcanyāyatanabhūmikāḥ, kāmadhātumupādāya te prahīyante |
[Thus] the defilements of the lower planes, [going] up through the sphere of nothingness, beginning with the realm of desire, are abandoned,
sbh2.0:36.15
na tvatyantataḥ prahīyante |
but not abandoned absolutely;
sbh2.0:36.16
te punareva te pratisandhikā bhavanti |
they are subject to re-arising.
sbh2.0:36.17
lokottareṇa vā punarmārgeṇa kleśaviśodhanamālambanaṁ caturvidhaṁ, tadyathā |
By the supramundane path, on the other hand, the defilement-purifying object is fourfold, namely:
sbh2.0:36.18
duḥkhasatyaṁ, samudayasatyaṁ, nirodhasatyaṁ, mārgasatyañca |
the truth of suffering, the truth of origin, the truth of cessation, and the truth of the path.
sbh2.0:37.1
tatra duḥkhasatyaṁ katamat |
Now, what is the truth of suffering?
sbh2.0:37.2
tadyathā jātirduḥkhaṁ jarāpi duḥkhaṁ vyādhirmaraṇamapriyasaṁyogaḥ priyavinābhāva icchāvighātaśca |
Namely: birth is suffering, aging too is suffering, illness [is suffering], death [is suffering], union with the unloved [is suffering], separation from the loved [is suffering], and frustration of desire [is suffering] —
sbh2.0:37.3
saṁkṣepataḥ pañcopādānaskandhā duḥkhaṁ |
in short, the five aggregates of clinging are suffering.
sbh2.0:37.4
tatra samudaya āryasatyaṁ tṛṣṇā paunarbhavikī nandīrāgasahagatā tatra tatrābhinandinī |
The noble truth of origin is craving — productive of further existence, bound up with delight and lust, taking delight here and there.
sbh2.0:38.1
tatra niro dha ā ryasatyaṁ yadasyā eva tṛṣṇāyā aśeṣaprahāṇaṁ |
The noble truth of cessation is the complete abandonment of that very craving.
sbh2.0:39.1
mārgasatyaṁ āryāṣṭāṁgo mārgaḥ |
The truth of the path is the noble eightfold path.
sbh2.0:39.2
tatra kṛṣṇapakṣaṁ śuklapakṣaṁ copādāya hetuphalavyavasthānena catuḥsatyavyavasthānaṁ |
The fourfold delineation of [these] truths is made by reference to the dark side and the bright side, by the structure of cause and effect:
sbh2.0:40.1
tatra duḥkhasatyaṁ phalaṁ |
the truth of suffering is the effect,
sbh2.0:40.2
samudayasatyaṁ hetuḥ |
the truth of origin is the cause [on the dark side];
sbh2.0:40.3
nirodhasatyaṁ phalaṁ |
the truth of cessation is the effect,
sbh2.0:40.4
mārgasatyaṁ hetuḥ prāptaye, sparśanāyai |
the truth of the path is the cause [on the bright side] — for the attainment, for the direct touching [of cessation].
sbh2.0:40.5
tatra duḥkhasatyaṁ vyādhisthānīyaṁ tatprathamataḥ parijñeyaṁ |
The truth of suffering is like the illness: it is to be fully known first.
sbh2.0:40.6
samudayasatyaṁ vyādhinidānasthānīyaṁ |
The truth of origin is like the cause of the illness:
sbh2.0:40.7
taccāntaramparivarjayitavyaṁ |
it is to be discarded next.
sbh2.0:40.8
nirodhasatyamārogyasthānīyaṁ |
The truth of cessation is like health:
sbh2.0:40.9
tacca sparśayitavyaṁ spraṣṭavyam , sākṣātkartavyaṁ |
it is to be touched, to be realized directly.
sbh2.0:40.10
mārgasatyaṁ bhaiṣajyasthānīyaṁ |
The truth of the path is like the medicine:
sbh2.0:40.11
taccāsevitavyaṁ, bhāvayitavyaṁ |
it is to be resorted to, to be cultivated,
sbh2.0:40.12
bahulīkartavyaṁ |
to be made much of.
sbh2.0:40.13
bhūtaṁ caitattathā avitathā -thama viparītamaviparyastaṁ duḥkhaṁ duḥkhārthena, yāvanmārgo mārgārthena tasmātsatyamityucyate |
And [each] is real, just so, not otherwise, not contradicted, not perverted: suffering by way of being suffering, [and so on] up to the path by way of being the path — therefore [each] is called a truth.
sbh2.0:40.14
svalakṣaṇaṁ ca na visamvadati |
And its specific characteristic does not deceive,
sbh2.0:40.15
taddarśanāccāviparītā buddhayaḥ pravarttante |
and from seeing it unperverted cognitions arise.
sbh2.0:40.16
tena satyamityucyate |
For that reason it is called truth.
sbh2.0:40.17
kasmātpunaretānyāryāṇāmeva satyāni bhavanti |
Why, again, are these truths only of the noble ones (ārya)?
sbh2.0:40.18
āryā etāni paśyantyeva samānāni, satyatā to jānanti paśyanti |
The noble ones alike regard them [as truths]; they know and see them as truths,
sbh2.0:40.19
yathābhūtaṁ, bālāstu na jānanti, na paśyanti |
as they actually are. The foolish, on the other hand, do not know, [do not] see [them]
sbh2.0:40.20
yathābhūtaṁ tasmādāryasatyānītyucyante |
as they actually are. Therefore they are called noble truths.
sbh2.0:40.21
bālānāmetaddharmatayā satyaṁ nāvabodheta budhyate |
For the foolish, this is truth by the nature of things, not by being understood;
sbh2.0:40.22
āryāṇāṁ tūbhayathā tatra jānāti jñāyate |
for the noble ones, in both ways.
sbh2.0:40.23
duḥkhamiti jāyamānasya duḥkhā vedanotpadyeta |
‘[Birth is] suffering’: for one being born, painful feeling arises,
sbh2.0:40.24
kāyika caitasikī, na tu ja ā tireva duḥkhaṁ, duḥkhanidānaṁ sā, evaṁ yāvadicchāvighāto duḥkhamiti |
bodily and mental; but birth itself is not the suffering — it is the cause of suffering; and so on up to ‘frustration of desire is suffering’:
sbh2.0:41.1
icchāvighātanidānaṁ duḥkhamutpadyeta |
Suffering arises caused by frustration of desire,
sbh2.0:41.2
kāyikacaitasikaṁ, na tvicchāvighāta eva |
[suffering both] bodily and mental; but the frustration of desire itself is not the
sbh2.0:41.3
duḥkhaṁ, duḥkhanidānaṁ punaḥ sa iti peyālam |
[felt] suffering — it is rather the cause of suffering, and [the same holds] for the rest.
sbh2.0:41.4
saṁkṣepataḥ paṁcopādānaskandhāḥ duḥkhamityebhirjātyādibhiḥ paryāyai ḥ |
‘In brief, the five aggregates of clinging are suffering’: by these modes beginning with birth
sbh2.0:41.5
duḥkhaduḥkhataiva paridīpitā |
only the suffering-of-suffering (duḥkha-duḥkhatā) is elucidated;
sbh2.0:41.6
tatra vipaṇāmaduḥkhatā saṁskāraduḥkhatā cāvaśiṣṭā |
the suffering of change and the suffering of conditioning (saṁskāra-duḥkhatā) remain.
sbh2.0:41.7
sā punaḥ pañcaskandhaduḥkhatayā paridīpitā bhavati |
But these are elucidated by the suffering of the five aggregates,
sbh2.0:41.8
tathā hi paṁcopādānaskandhāstrivedanāparigatāste tathoktāyāḥ dukhaduḥkhatāyā bhājanabhūtā ḥ |
for the five aggregates of clinging are pervaded by the three feelings, and are the vessel of the suffering-of-suffering just described,
sbh2.0:41.9
yā ca noktā vipariṇāmaduḥkhatā |
and [also of] the suffering of change, not [explicitly] stated,
sbh2.0:41.10
saṁskāraduḥkhatā ca |
and [of] the suffering of conditioning —
sbh2.0:41.11
sāpyeṣveva draṣṭavyā |
these too are to be seen in just these aggregates.
sbh2.0:41.12
kena punaḥ kāraṇena bhagavatā duḥkhaduḥkhataiva parikīrttitā |
For what reason, then, did the Blessed One proclaim the suffering-of-suffering alone
sbh2.0:41.13
svaśabdena vipariṇāmaduḥkhatā, saṁskāraduḥkhatā punaḥ paryāyeṇa |
by its own term, but the suffering of change and the suffering of conditioning [only] by another mode?
sbh2.0:41.14
tathā hi duḥkhaduḥkhatāyā māryāṇāṁ bālānāñca tulyā duḥkhatābuddhiḥ pravarttate |
Because regarding the suffering-of-suffering, for the noble ones and the foolish alike, the cognition of suffering arises equally.
sbh2.0:41.15
saṁvejikātyarthaṁ duḥkhaduḥkhatā pūrvamakṛtaprajñānāmevaṁ ca deśyamāne sukhamavatāro bhavati |
And the suffering-of-suffering is exceedingly sobering; and for those who have not cultivated wisdom before, when it is taught so, there is easy entry
sbh2.0:41.16
satyeṣu vineyānāṁ |
into the truths for those to be trained.
sbh2.0:41.17
tatra trividhāyā duḥkhatāyāḥ kathaṁ vyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
How, then, is the threefold suffering delineated?
sbh2.0:41.18
yattāvadduḥkhaṁ jātirduḥkhaṁ yāvadicchāvighāto duḥkhamityanena sādhiṣṭhānā duḥkhā vedanā ākhyātā |
Now, what was said — ‘suffering is birth, [and so on] through to frustration of desire is suffering’ — by this the painful feeling with its basis is declared.
sbh2.0:42.1
sā ca duḥkhaduḥkhatā |
And that is the suffering-of-suffering.
sbh2.0:42.2
idaṁ duḥkhaduḥkhatāyā vyavasthānaṁ |
This is the delineation of the suffering-of-suffering.
sbh2.0:42.3
ye vā punaretadvipakṣā dharmāstathā yauvanaṁ jarāyā, vyādherārogyaṁ, jīvitaṁ maraṇasya, priyasaṁprayogo ’priyasaṁprayogasya |
[Now] those dharmas which are its opposite — namely youth [as opposed] to aging, health to illness, life to death, union with the loved to union with the unloved,
sbh2.0:42.4
apriyavinābhāvaḥ priyavinābhāvasya, icchāsampattiricchāvighātasya, ye ca duḥkhāyāṁ vedanāyāṁ pravṛttāḥ kleśāḥ sādhiṣṭhānā, ye cārogyādiṣu sukhasthānīyeṣu dharmeṣu tannirjātāyāṁ ca vedanāyāṁ ye pravṛttāḥ kleśā iyamucyate vipariṇāma duḥkhatā |
separation from the unloved [as opposed] to separation from the loved, [and] gratification of desire to its frustration — and the defilements, with their basis, that proceed in painful feeling, and the defilements that proceed in regard to dharmas that are occasions of pleasure, such as health, and in regard to the feeling born of them — this is called the suffering of change.
sbh2.0:43.1
tatra sukhā vedanā sādhiṣṭhānā anityatayā pa ri ṇamantī |
A pleasant feeling with its basis, [being] impermanent, undergoes change,
sbh2.0:43.2
atyarthībhāvādhipateyaṁ duḥkhaṁ vidadhāti |
[and] because [Conjectural: becoming otherwise] predominates, it produces suffering.
Printed atyarthībhāva-; read anyathībhāva- (Xuanzang 若變若異).
sbh2.0:43.3
kleśāḥ punaḥ sarvatra pravṛttāḥ paryavasthānaṣa śa eva duḥkhā bhavanti |
The defilements, again, wherever they arise, are painful by the very force of obsession,
sbh2.0:43.4
vipariṇāmaśca sa cetasaḥ tasmādvipariṇāmaduḥkhatetyucyate |
and this is [the] changed-state of mind. Hence it is called the suffering of change.
sbh2.0:44.1
yathoktaṁ bhagavatā- avatīrṇavipariṇatena cittena mātṛgrāmasya hastigrahaṇaṁ ceti vistaraḥ |
As was said by the Blessed One: ‘with a mind overcome and changed, the taking of a woman by the hand’ — and so on at length.
Printed hastigrahaṇaṁ; read hastagrahaṇaṁ (Xuanzang 執母邑手).
sbh2.0:45.1
yathā coktaṁ- kāma c chandaparyavasthitaḥ |
And as was said: ‘Gripped by the obsessive desire of sense-lust
sbh2.0:45.2
kāma c chandaparyavasthānapratyayaṁ tajjaṁ catasikaṁ duḥkhadaurmanasyaṁ pratisaṁvedayate |
and conditioned by that sense-desire obsession, he experiences mental pain and dejection born thereof.’
sbh2.0:45.3
evaṁ vyāpādastyānamiddhāmauddha middhauddha tyakaukṛtyavicikitsāparyavasthitaḥ |
Likewise [for one] gripped by ill-will, sloth-and-torpor, restlessness-and-regret, [and] doubt.
sbh2.0:45.4
tadanenāgamenāptena paramāptena kleśeṣu duḥkhārtopi lambate |
Hence, by this scriptural tradition, supremely attested, the suffering-character is found in defilements,
sbh2.0:45.5
vipariṇāmārtho rtto pi |
and so is the change-character.
sbh2.0:45.6
tenocyate kleśavipariṇāma duḥkhate ti |
Therefore one speaks of defilement-as-change-suffering.
sbh2.0:45.7
idaṁ vipariṇāma duḥkhatāyā vyavasthānaṁ |
This is the delineation of the suffering of change.
sbh2.0:45.8
saṁskāraduḥkhatā punaḥ |
The suffering of conditioning, again, is
sbh2.0:45.9
sarvatragā upādānaskandheṣu saṁkṣepata āryā ca dukhaduḥkhatā, yā ca kleśasaṁgṛhītā vipariṇāmadharmatā, ye yā ca sādhiṣṭhānā sukhā vedanā tāṁ sthāpayitvā, ye tadanye skandhāśca duḥkhāḥ duḥkha sahagatāstannirjātāstadutpattipratyayā stasya cotpannasya sthitibhājanā iyamucyate saṁskāraduḥkhatā |
all-pervasive in the aggregates of clinging: in brief, setting aside [Conjectural: whatever] suffering-of-suffering [there is], the change-nature comprised under defilement, and the pleasant feeling with its basis, the remaining aggregates — [Conjectural: accompanied by neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling], born from it, conditions for its arising, and vessels for its abiding once arisen — this is called the suffering of conditioning.
Printed āryā ca; read yā ca (Xuanzang 除苦苦性 has no ‘noble’). Printed duḥkhāḥ (duḥkha)sahagatāḥ, the second word queried by Shukla; the sense follows Xuanzang 不苦不樂受俱行.
sbh2.0:46.1
ye skandhā anityā udayavyayayuto tāḥ sopādānāstrivedanābhiranuṣaktā ḥ |
The aggregates [are] impermanent, marked by arising and vanishing, accompanied by clinging, attached to the three feelings,
sbh2.0:46.2
dauṣṭhulyopagatā ayogakṣemapatitā avinirmuktāḥ |
afflicted by encumbrance (dauṣṭhulya), fallen into insecurity, not released
sbh2.0:46.3
duḥkhaduḥkhatāyā vipariṇāmaduḥkhatāyā asvavaśavartinaśca |
from the suffering-of-suffering and the suffering of change, and not proceeding under their own control —
sbh2.0:46.4
iyamucyate saṁskāraduḥkhatayā duḥkhatā |
this is called suffering by way of the suffering of conditioning.
sbh2.0:46.5
idaṁ saṁskāraduḥkhatāyā vyavasthānaṁ |
This is the delineation of the suffering of conditioning.
sbh2.0:46.6
tabha tra tṛṣṇā prārthanābhilāṣobhinandaneti paryāyāḥ |
Here ‘craving’ (tṛṣṇā), ‘longing,’ ‘yearning,’ [and] ‘delighting’ are synonyms.
sbh2.0:46.7
sā punaḥ prārthanā tribhirmukhaiḥ pravṛttā s tadyathā punarbhavaprārthanā, viṣayaprārthanā ca |
That yearning, again, proceeds along three avenues: yearning for further existence, and yearning for sense-objects.
sbh2.0:46.8
tatra yā punarbhavaprārthanā mā paunarbhavikī tṛṣṇā |
The yearning for further existence is the craving that is productive of re-becoming.
sbh2.0:46.9
viṣayaprārthanā punardvividhā |
Yearning for sense-objects is twofold:
sbh2.0:46.10
prāpteṣu viṣayeṣu saumanasyādhyavasānasahagatā, aprāpteṣu ca viṣayeṣu saṁyogābhilāṣasahagatā |
[a] with respect to sense-objects already attained, accompanied by joy and clinging to them; and [b] with respect to sense-objects not yet attained, accompanied by longing for union with them.
sbh2.0:46.11
tatra yā prāpteṣu viṣayeṣu saumanasyādhyavasānasahagatā nandīrāgasahagatetyucyate |
Of these, the one with respect to objects attained, accompanied by joy and clinging, is what is called ‘accompanied by delight and lust.’
sbh2.0:46.12
yā punaraprāpteṣu viṣayeṣu saṁyogābhilāṣasahagatā tatra tatrābhinandinītyucyate |
The one with respect to objects not yet attained, accompanied by longing for union, is what is called ‘delighting here and there.’
sbh2.0:46.13
nirodhopidvividhaḥ |
Cessation, too, is twofold:
sbh2.0:46.14
kleśanirodhaḥ, upakleśanirodhaśca |
cessation of defilement (kleśa), and cessation of secondary defilement (upakleśa).
sbh2.0:46.15
mārgopi dvividhaḥ |
The path, too, is twofold:
sbh2.0:46.16
śaikṣaścāśaikṣaśca |
that of the trainee (śaikṣa) and that of the non-trainee (aśaikṣa).
sbh2.0:46.17
idamālambanaṁ kleśaviśodhanaṁ lokottareṇa mārgeṇa veditavyaṁ |
This defilement-purifying object is to be understood as belonging to the supramundane path.
sbh2.0:46.18
tenāha caturvidhamālambanaṁ |
Thus he speaks of a fourfold object:
sbh2.0:46.19
vyāpyālambanaṁ, caritaviśodhanaṁ, kauśalyālambanaṁ, kleśaviśodhanaṁ ceti |
the pervasive object (vyāpyālambana), the conduct-purifying object (caritaviśodhana), the skill object (kauśalyālambana), and the defilement-purifying object (kleśaviśodhana).
sbh2.0:47.1
tatrāvavādaḥ katamaḥ |
Now, what is instruction (avavāda)?
sbh2.0:47.2
caturvidho a vavādaḥ |
Fourfold instruction:
sbh2.0:47.3
aviparītāvavādaḥ |
unperverted instruction,
sbh2.0:47.4
anupūrvāvavādaḥ |
graduated instruction,
sbh2.0:47.5
āgamāvavādaḥ |
instruction from tradition,
sbh2.0:47.6
adhigamāvavādaśca |
and instruction from direct realization.
sbh2.0:48.1
tatrāviparītāvavādaḥ katamaḥ |
What is unperverted instruction?
sbh2.0:48.2
yadaviparītaṁ dharmasattvaṁ ca deśayati |
That by which one teaches [Conjectural: the Dharma and its meaning] in an unperverted way,
Printed dharmasattvaṁ, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 法義.
sbh2.0:48.3
grāhayati |
[and] brings the hearer to grasp it —
sbh2.0:48.4
bhūtaṁ yadasya niryāti |
[teaching what is] real, which leads him out,
sbh2.0:48.5
samyagduḥkhakṣayāya |
for the right destruction of suffering,
sbh2.0:48.6
duḥkhasyāntakriyāyai |
for the making of an end to suffering —
sbh2.0:48.7
ayamucyate ’viparītāvavādaḥ |
this is called unperverted instruction.
sbh2.0:48.8
anupūrvāvavādaḥ katamaḥ |
What is graduated instruction?
sbh2.0:48.9
yatkālena dharmaṁ deśayati |
When in due time one teaches the Dharma,
sbh2.0:48.10
uttānottānāni sthānāni tatprathamato grāhayati |
[and] points out first of all the more accessible matters, has them learnt,
sbh2.0:48.11
vācaya ti |
has them recited,
sbh2.0:48.12
tataḥ paścādgaṁbhīrāṇi prathamasya vā satyasyābhisamayāya tatprathamato ’vavadate |
and afterwards the deep ones — first, for the realization of the first truth, one instructs concerning that,
sbh2.0:48.13
tataḥ paścātsamudayanirodhamārgasatyasya, prathamasya dhyānasya samāpattaye tatprathamatovavadate |
[and] afterwards concerning the truths of origin, cessation, and path; [or] for the attainment of the first dhyāna, one instructs first concerning that,
sbh2.0:48.14
tataḥ paścādanyāsāṁ dhyānasamāpattīnāmayamevaṁbhāgīyonupūrvāvavādo veditavyaḥ |
[and] afterwards concerning the other dhyānas and attainments. This kind of instruction is to be known as graduated instruction.
sbh2.0:48.15
tatrāgamāvavādo yathā tena gurūṇāmantikādāgamitaṁ bhavati |
Instruction from tradition is what one has received from teachers or those in the place of teacher —
sbh2.0:48.16
gurusthānīyānāṁ, yogajñānāṁ, ācāryāṇāmupādhyāyasya vā, tathāgatasya vā, tathāgataśrāvakasya vā |
from preceptors and instructors who know yoga, or from the Tathāgata, or from a disciple of the Tathāgata —
sbh2.0:48.17
tathaivānenānyūnamadhikaṁ kṛtvā parānavavadate |
and [transmits], neither subtracting nor adding, instructing others.
sbh2.0:48.18
ayamucyate āgamāvavādaḥ |
This is called instruction from tradition.
sbh2.0:48.19
tatrādhigamāvavādaḥ |
Instruction from direct realization:
sbh2.0:48.20
yathānena tedharmā adhigatā bhavanti |
[the case in which] one to whom these dharmas have become known,
sbh2.0:48.21
sparśitāḥ spṛṣṭāḥ sākṣātkṛtā, ekākinā vyavakṛṣṭavihāriṇā |
touched, contacted, [and] made manifest — having dwelt alone, secluded —
sbh2.0:48.22
tañcaiva pareṣāṁ prāptaye |
[then,] for the attainment of [the same] by others,
sbh2.0:48.23
sparśanāyai sākṣātkriyāyai |
for their touching, for their making-manifest,
sbh2.0:48.24
avavadate |
instructs [them].
sbh2.0:48.25
ayamucyate adhigamāvavādaḥ |
This is called instruction from direct realization.
sbh2.0:48.26
asti punaḥ sarvākāraparipūrṇovavādaḥ |
There is, moreover, a wholly complete instruction, of every aspect.
sbh2.0:48.27
sa punaḥ katamaḥ |
What is it?
sbh2.0:48.28
yastribhiḥ yat tribhiḥ prātihāryairavavadati |
That by which one instructs through the three wonders (prātihārya):
sbh2.0:48.29
ṛddhiprātihāryeṇa, ādeśanāprātihāryeṇa |
the wonder of psychic power, the wonder of mind-reading,
sbh2.0:48.30
anuśāstiprātihāryeṇa |
and the wonder of admonition.
sbh2.0:48.31
ṛddhiprātihāryeṇa, anekavidhamṛddhiviṣayamupadarśayatyātmani ca bahumānaṁ ja na yati |
By the wonder of psychic power, one displays manifold ranges of psychic power and so engenders respect [for oneself]
sbh2.0:48.32
pareṣāṁ yathā tena bahumānajātāḥ |
in others, such that, born of respect,
sbh2.0:48.33
śrotrāvadhānayoge manasikāre ādarajātā bhavanti |
they become eager in applying the ear and in attention.
sbh2.0:48.34
tatra |
Then,
sbh2.0:48.35
deśanā prātihāryeṇa cittacaritaṁ samanveṣya anuśāstiprātihāryeṇa yathendriyaṁ, yathācaritaṁ, yathāvatāraṁdharmadeśanāṁ deśayati |
by the wonder of mind-reading, one investigates the operations of their mind; by the wonder of admonition, one teaches the Dharma to suit their faculties, their conduct, [and] their access [to teaching],
sbh2.0:48.36
pratipakṣe samanuśāsti |
and admonishes [them] by appropriate antidotes.
sbh2.0:48.37
tenāyaṁ prātihāryatrayasaṁgṛhītaḥ paripūrṇāvavādo bhavati |
Thereby this instruction, subsumed under the three wonders, is the complete instruction.
sbh2.0:49.1
tatra śikṣā katamā |
Now, what is training (śikṣā)?
sbh2.0:49.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:49.3
tisraḥ śikṣāḥ |
the three trainings —
sbh2.0:49.4
adhiśīlaṁ śikṣā, adhicittamadhiprajñaṁ śikṣā |
training in higher virtue (adhiśīla), training in higher mind (adhicitta), [and] training in higher wisdom (adhiprajñā).
sbh2.0:50.1
tatrādhiśīlaṁ śikṣā katamā |
What is the training in higher virtue?
sbh2.0:50.2
yathāpi tacchīlaṁ vā tacchīlavān viharatīti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
Just as ‘… he dwells possessed of virtue,’ and so on in detail, as above.
sbh2.0:51.1
tatrādhiśīlaṁ cittaṁ śikṣā viviktaṁ kāmairviviktaṁ pāpakairakuśalairdharmaiḥ savitarkaṁ, savicāraṁ, vivekajaṁ, prītisukhaṁ, cittaikāgratā ñ ca, prathamaṁ dhyānaṁ yāvaccaturthaṁ dhyānamupasampadya viharati |
The training in higher mind: secluded from sense-desires, secluded from evil unwholesome dharmas, with applied and sustained thought, born of seclusion, accompanied by joy and pleasure, and with one-pointedness of mind — he enters and abides in the first dhyāna, [and so on] up to the fourth dhyāna.
sbh2.0:51.2
iyamadhicittaṁ, śikṣā |
This is the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:51.3
api khalu sarve ārūpyāstadanyāśca samādhisamāpattayaḥ |
Moreover, all the formless attainments and other concentration-attainments
sbh2.0:51.4
adhicittaṁ śikṣetyucyate |
are called training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:51.5
api tu dhyānāni niśritya tatprathamataḥ satyābhisamayomā vakrāntirbhavati |
But it is in reliance on the dhyānas that the realization of the truths, the entry [into the fixed course], first comes about,
sbh2.0:51.6
na tu sarveṇa sarvaṁ vinā dhyānaiḥ tasmātpradhānāni dhyānāni kṛtvā adhicittaṁ śikṣetyuktāni |
not entirely without the dhyānas. Therefore the dhyānas, taken as primary, are called the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:51.7
tatrādhiprajñaṁ śikṣā yā caturṣvāryasatyeṣu yathābhūtaṁ jñānaṁ |
The training in higher wisdom is the knowing of the four noble truths as they actually are.
sbh2.0:51.8
kena kāraṇena tisra eva śikṣā na tadūrdhvaṁ |
For what reason are there just three trainings, no more?
sbh2.0:51.9
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:51.10
samādhipratiṣṭhārthena |
in the sense of ‘establishment of concentration’;
sbh2.0:51.11
jñānasaṁniśrayārthena |
in the sense of ‘support of knowledge’;
sbh2.0:51.12
kṛtyakaraṇārthena ca |
and in the sense of ‘doing the task’.
sbh2.0:51.13
tatra samādhipratiṣṭhārthenādhiśīlaṁ |
[That is,] in the sense of ‘establishment of concentration’, [there is] the training in higher virtue —
sbh2.0:51.14
śikṣā |
[that] training;
sbh2.0:51.15
tathā hi śīlaṁ pratiṣṭhāya cittaikāgratāṁ spṛśati |
for, having been established in virtue, one touches the one-pointedness of mind,
sbh2.0:52.1
cittasamādhiṁ |
the concentration of mind.
sbh2.0:53.1
tatra jñānasanniśrayārthena adhicittaṁ śikṣā |
In the sense of ‘support of knowledge’, [there is] the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:53.2
tathā hi samāhitacittasyaikāgratā |
For when the mind is concentrated, one-pointed,
sbh2.0:53.3
smṛtyā jñeye vastuni yathābhūtaṁ jñānadarśanaṁ pravarttate |
knowing and seeing of the knowable as it actually is proceeds by mindfulness.
sbh2.0:53.4
tatra kṛtyakaraṇārthena adhiprajñaṁ śikṣā |
In the sense of ‘doing the task’, [there is] the training in higher wisdom;
sbh2.0:53.5
tathā hi suviśuddhena jñānadarśanena kleśaprahāṇaṁ sākṣātkaroti |
for by perfectly purified knowing-and-seeing one directly realizes the abandonment of defilements.
sbh2.0:53.6
eṣa hi svārtha e tat paramaṁ kṛtyaṁ yaduta kleśaprahāṇaṁ |
For this is one’s own good, this is the supreme task — namely, the abandonment of defilements;
sbh2.0:53.7
tata uttarikaraṇīyaṁ punarnāsti | tena etāstisra eva śikṣāḥ || kāḥ punarāsāṁ śikṣāṇāmānupūrvīṣu viśuddhaśīlasya vipratisāraḥ |
Beyond that there is nothing further to be done; therefore there are just these three trainings. What, moreover, is the sequence of these trainings? In one of well-purified virtue there is [Conjectural: no] regret;
Printed ānupūrvīṣu viśuddhaśīlasya vipratisāraḥ; read ānupūrvī suviśuddhaśīlasyāvipratisāraḥ (53.8; Xuanzang 善清淨 … 無憂悔).
sbh2.0:53.8
avipratisāriṇaḥ prāmodyaṁ, prītiḥ, praśrabdhiḥ, sukhaṁ, sukhitasya cittasamādhiḥ |
in the regret-free, gladness; in gladness, joy; in joy, pliancy; in pliancy, pleasure; in the pleased, mental concentration.
sbh2.0:53.9
samāhitacitto yathābhūtaṁ prajānāti |
The concentrated mind knows things as they actually are,
sbh2.0:53.10
yathābhūtaṁ paśyati |
sees [them] as they actually are;
sbh2.0:53.11
yathābhūtaṁ jānanpaśyannirvidyate |
knowing and seeing [them] as they actually are, one grows weary;
sbh2.0:53.12
nirviṇṇo virajyate |
wearied, one becomes detached;
sbh2.0:53.13
virakto vimucyate |
detached, one is liberated;
sbh2.0:53.14
vimukto a nupādāya parinirvāti |
liberated, without clinging, one attains final nirvāṇa.
sbh2.0:53.15
evamimāni śīlāni bhāvitāni agratāyāmupanayanti |
Thus these virtues, when cultivated, lead on to the peak —
sbh2.0:53.16
yadutānupādāya parinirvāṇamiyamāsāṁ śikṣāṇāmānupūrvī |
namely, to nirvāṇa without clinging. This is the sequence of these trainings.
sbh2.0:53.17
tatra kena kāraṇenādhiśīlaṁ śikṣā adhiśīlamityucyate |
For what reason is the training in higher virtue called ‘higher virtue’?
sbh2.0:53.18
evamadhicittamadhiprajñamadhikārārthenā dhikārārthena ca |
Similarly higher mind [and] higher wisdom — [each is so called] by reason of referent and by reason of [Conjectural: pre-eminence].
Printed adhikārārthenādhikārārthena (Shukla: a repetition); read adhikārārthenādhikārthena (53.26; Xuanzang 所趣義故，最勝義故).
sbh2.0:53.19
tatra kathamadhikārārthenā nā a dhicittamadhikṛtya yacchīlaṁ sā adhiśīlaṁ śikṣā |
How [is it so called] by reason of referent? With higher mind as referent, virtue is the training in higher virtue.
sbh2.0:53.20
adhiprajñamadhikṛtya yaścittasamādhiḥ |
With higher wisdom as referent, mental concentration
sbh2.0:53.21
sā adhicittaṁ śikṣā |
is the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:53.22
kleśaprahāṇamadhikṛtya yajjñānaṁ darśanaṁ |
With reference to abandoning defilement, that knowing-and-seeing
sbh2.0:53.23
sā adhiprajñaṁ śikṣā |
is the training in higher wisdom.
sbh2.0:53.24
yā cādhicittaṁ |
[Conjectural: How by reason of pre-eminence?] [Lacuna] And whichever is the training in higher mind,
Printed text omits the question and the first member, yā cādhiśīlaṁ śikṣā; Xuanzang 云何最勝義？謂若增上戒學.
sbh2.0:53.25
yā cādhiprajñaṁ śikṣā |
and whichever is the training in higher wisdom —
sbh2.0:53.26
etāḥ śikṣāḥ asminneva śāsane asādhāraṇā ito bāhyairevamadhikārthena |
these trainings are unique to this teaching, not shared with those outside it. Thus [they are called ‘higher’] by reason of pre-eminence.
sbh2.0:53.27
asti punaradhicittaṁ śikṣā yā adhiprajñaṁ śikṣāyā āvāhikā, astyadhiprajñaṁ śikṣā yā adhicittaṁ śikṣāyā āvāhikā |
There is also a training in higher mind that brings on the training in higher wisdom, and a training in higher wisdom that brings on the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:53.28
tadyathā |
For example:
sbh2.0:53.29
āryaśrāvakaḥ |
the noble disciple,
sbh2.0:53.30
alābhī mauladhyānānāṁ, śaikṣo, dṛṣṭapadaḥ, tataḥ paścādbhāvanāprahātavyānāṁ kleśānāṁ prahāṇāya prayujyamāna ḥ smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁgaṁ bhāvayati |
not yet possessed of the basic dhyānas, a trainee, one who has seen the track (dṛṣṭapada), afterwards engaging for the abandonment of the defilements to be abandoned by cultivation, cultivates the awakening-factor of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:53.31
iyamadhiprajñaṁ śikṣā |
This is the training in higher wisdom
sbh2.0:53.32
adhicittaṁ śikṣāyā āvāhikā |
that brings on the training in higher mind.
sbh2.0:53.33
adhicittaṁ punaḥ śikṣā adhiprajñāyā āvāhikā pūrvamevoktā |
The training in higher mind that brings on the training in higher wisdom was stated earlier.
sbh2.0:54.1
tatra astyadhiśīlaṁ śikṣā |
Here: there is training in higher virtue
sbh2.0:54.2
nādhicittaṁ, nādhiprajñaṁ |
without higher mind or higher wisdom;
sbh2.0:54.3
astyadhiśīlamadhicittaṁ, nādhiprajñaṁ |
there is higher-virtue-with-higher-mind without higher wisdom;
sbh2.0:54.4
na tvastyadhiprajñaṁ śikṣā yā vinādhiśīlenādhicittena ca |
but there is no training in higher wisdom without both higher virtue and higher mind.
sbh2.0:54.5
ato yatrādhiprajñaṁ |
Therefore where higher wisdom is [being]
sbh2.0:54.6
śikṣā |
trained,
sbh2.0:54.7
tatra tisraḥ śikṣā veditavyā ḥ |
there all three trainings are to be understood [as present].
sbh2.0:55.1
idaṁ tāvacchikṣāvyavasthānaṁ |
This much is the delineation of the trainings.
sbh2.0:55.2
tatra yoginā yogaprayuktena śikṣitavyaṁ |
Here a yogin engaged in yoga should train.
sbh2.0:55.3
tatra trayaḥ pudgalāḥ satyānyabhisamāgacchanti |
Three [kinds of] person attain the truths:
sbh2.0:55.4
katame trayastadyathā |
which three? Namely,
sbh2.0:55.5
avītarāgaḥ yadbhūyo vītarāgaḥ, vītarāgaśca |
one [wholly] not free from lust, one largely free from lust, and one wholly free from lust.
sbh2.0:55.6
tatra sarveṇasarvamavītarāgaḥ satyānyabhisamāgacchan saha satyābhisamayātsrota -yena strota āpanno bhavati |
The one wholly not free from lust, attaining the truths, with the realization of the truths becomes a stream-enterer.
sbh2.0:55.7
yadbhūyo vītarāgaḥ punaḥ sakṛdāgāmī bhavati |
The one largely free from lust becomes a once-returner.
sbh2.0:55.8
vītarāgaḥ saha satyābhisamayād yenā nāgāmī bhavati |
The one wholly free from lust, with the realization of the truths, becomes a non-returner.
sbh2.0:55.9
trīṇīndriyāṇi |
[There are] three faculties:
sbh2.0:55.10
anājñātamājñāsyāmīndriyaṁ |
the faculty ‘I shall come to know what is not yet known’ (anājñātam-ājñāsyāmi-indriya),
sbh2.0:55.11
ājñendriyamājñātavata indriyaṁ |
the faculty of knowing (ājñendriya), [and] the faculty of one who has known.
sbh2.0:55.12
eṣāmindriyāṇāṁ kathaṁ vyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
How is the delineation of these faculties made?
sbh2.0:55.13
anabhisamitānāṁ satyānāṁ abhisamayāya prayuktasya anājñātamājñāsyāmīndriyavyavasthānaṁ |
For one engaged in the realization of truths not yet realized, [there is] the delineation of the faculty ‘I shall come to know the unknown.’
sbh2.0:55.14
abhisamitavataḥ śaikṣasyājñendriyavyavasthānaṁ |
For the trainee who has realized [them], [there is] the delineation of the faculty of knowing.
sbh2.0:55.15
kṛtakṛtyasyāśaikṣasyārhataḥ |
For the non-trainee, the arhat, whose task is done,
sbh2.0:55.16
ājñā tāvīndriya vyavasthānaṁ |
[there is] the delineation of the faculty of one who has known.
sbh2.0:55.17
trīṇi vimokṣamukhāni |
[There are] three doors of liberation (vimokṣa-mukha),
sbh2.0:55.18
