tadyathā śūnyatā a praṇihitamānimittemeṣāṁ trayāṇāṁ vimokṣamukhānāṁ kathaṁ vyavasthānaṁ bhavati | āha | dvayamidaṁ saṁskṛtamasaṁskṛtaṁ ca | tatra saṁskṛtaṁ traidhātukapratisaṁyuktāḥ pañcaskandhāḥ, asaṁskṛtaṁ punarnirvāṇaṁ | idamubhayaṁ yacca saṁskṛtaṁ, yaccā saṁskṛtamityucyate |
namely: emptiness (śūnyatā), wishlessness (apraṇihita), [and] signlessness (animitta). How is the delineation of these three doors of liberation made? Answer: there are these two, the conditioned and the unconditioned. Of them, the conditioned is the five aggregates bound up with the three realms; the unconditioned is nirvāṇa. This pair, what is conditioned and what is unconditioned, is what is spoken of [as real].
sbh2.0:55.19
yatpunaridamucyate |
But what is spoken of as
sbh2.0:55.20
ātmā vā, sattvo vā, jīvo vā, janturvā, idamasat |
self, being, soul, or person — that is unreal.
sbh2.0:55.21
tatra saṁskṛte doṣadarśanādādīnavadarśanādapraṇidhānaṁ bhavati |
Now, from seeing fault and disadvantage in the conditioned, no wish toward it arises;
sbh2.0:55.22
apraṇidhānāccāpraṇihitaṁ vimokṣamukhaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
from this absence of wishing, the door of liberation of wishlessness is delineated.
sbh2.0:55.23
nirvāṇe punaḥ tatra praṇidhānavataḥ praṇidhānaṁ bhavati |
In nirvāṇa, on the other hand, for one possessed of the wish, there is wishing —
sbh2.0:55.24
śāntadarśanaṁ |
seeing it as calm,
sbh2.0:55.25
praṇītadarśanaṁ |
as sublime,
sbh2.0:55.26
niḥsaraṇadarśanaṁ ca |
and as the way out (niḥsaraṇa);
sbh2.0:55.27
niḥsaraṇadarśanācca punarānimittaṁ vimokṣamukhaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
and from this seeing-the-way-out, the door of liberation of signlessness is delineated.
sbh2.0:55.28
tatrāsatyasamvidyamāne naiva praṇidhānaṁ bhavati |
Now, in what is unreal, non-existent, there is no wishing at all.
sbh2.0:55.29
tadyathaivāsattathaivāsaditi |
‘Just as it is unreal, so it is unreal’ —
sbh2.0:55.30
jānataḥ paśyataḥ śūnyatāvimokṣamukhaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
for one who knows and sees it [thus], the door of liberation of emptiness is delineated.
sbh2.0:55.31
evaṁ trayāṇāṁ vimokśamukhānāṁ vyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
Thus the delineation of the three doors of liberation is made.
sbh2.0:56.1
tatra katame śikṣānulomikā dharmāḥ |
Now, what are the dharmas conducive to training (śikṣānulomika-dharma)?
sbh2.0:56.2
āha daśa śikṣāvilomā dharmāḥ |
Answer: there are ten dharmas adverse to training,
sbh2.0:56.3
teṣāṁ pratipakṣeṇa daśa śikṣānulomikā dharmā veditavyāḥ |
[and] as antidotes to them, ten dharmas conducive to training are to be understood.
sbh2.0:56.4
tatra katame śikṣāvilomādharmāstadyathā |
What are the dharmas adverse to training? Namely:
sbh2.0:56.5
mātṛgrāmaḥ |
a woman —
sbh2.0:56.6
śiśurudāravarṇo raṁjanīyaḥ |
young, of stately complexion, captivating —
sbh2.0:56.7
śikṣāprayuktasya kulaputrasyādhimātramantarāyakaraḥ paripanthakaḥ, satkāyaparyāpanneṣu saṁskāreṣu niyantirālasyaṁ, kausīdyaṁ |
for a son of good family engaged in training, an extreme obstacle, a waylayer; [Conjectural: craving] for the formations comprised in the personality (satkāya); laziness, indolence;
Printed niyantir; read nikāntir (Xuanzang 愛著; so too 56.26).
sbh2.0:56.8

sbh2.0:56.9

sbh2.0:56.10

sbh2.0:56.11

sbh2.0:56.13

sbh2.0:56.17

sbh2.0:56.18

sbh2.0:56.19

sbh2.0:56.20

Syllables lost by pin-hole after amātrayā (Shukla’s note); Xuanzang 於諸言說 ‘in speech’.
sbh2.0:56.21

excessive talking,
sbh2.0:56.22

sbh2.0:56.23

in objects previously seen, heard, or experienced — manifold and of many kinds — scattering of mind, distraction of mind,
sbh2.0:56.24

sbh2.0:56.25

sbh2.0:56.26

sbh2.0:56.27
, jīvitāśā tadāśānugatasya śocanā, klāmyanā, paridevanā iti |

sbh2.0:56.28
ime daśa śikṣāvilomā dharmāḥ |
These are the ten dharmas adverse to training.
sbh2.0:56.29
katame daśa śikṣāpadānāṁ vilomānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ pratipakṣeṇa śikṣānulomikā bhavanti |
What ten dharmas, as antidotes to these ten dharmas adverse to the bases of training, are conducive to training?
sbh2.0:56.30
tadyathā-aśubhasaṁjñā, anitye duḥkhasaṁjñā |

sbh2.0:56.31

sbh2.0:56.32

sbh2.0:56.33

sbh2.0:56.37

sbh2.0:56.38
itīmā daśa saṁjñā ḥ |
These ten perceptions,
sbh2.0:56.39
āsevitā bhāvitā bahulīkṛtā daśavidhasya śikṣāparipanthakasya daśānāṁ śikṣāvilomānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ prahāṇāya samvartante |

sbh2.0:56.40

Now: the light of Dharma,

sbh2.0:57.1
tatrāpare daśa śikṣānulomikā dharmā veditavyāḥ |
Now, another ten dharmas conducive to training are to be understood.
sbh2.0:57.2
katame daśa |
Which ten?
sbh2.0:57.3
tadyathā pūrvako hetuḥ |
Namely: prior cause,
sbh2.0:57.4

sbh2.0:57.5

sbh2.0:57.6

sbh2.0:57.7

That which is the prior ripening of the faculties,
sbh2.0:57.8
indriyasamudāgamaśca |

sbh2.0:57.9
tatrānulomika upadeśaḥ katamaḥ ya upadeśo ’viparītaścāna nu pūrvikasya śca |
What is appropriate instruction? That instruction which is unperverted and graduated.
sbh2.0:57.10
tatra yoniśaḥ prayogaḥ |
What is thorough application?
sbh2.0:57.11
yathaivā va vaditaḥ voditaḥ |
Just as one has been instructed,
sbh2.0:57.12

so does one apply oneself;
sbh2.0:57.13

and applying himself thus, he produces right view.
sbh2.0:58.1
tatra sātatyasatkṛtyakāritā |
What is continual respectful exertion?
sbh2.0:58.2
ṣaḍrūpeṇa prayogeṇa abandhyañca kālaṁ karoti |
By sixfold application he does not die in vain
sbh2.0:58.3

sbh2.0:58.4

sbh2.0:58.5
yathāpi taduttare vimokṣe spṛhāmutpādayati |

sbh2.0:58.6

sbh2.0:58.8

sbh2.0:58.9

By two causes one comes to possess the power of yoga:
sbh2.0:58.10

sbh2.0:58.11

The [Conjectural: pacification] of bodily and mental encumbrance: as when, for one whose body is wearied, fatigued,
Printed dauṣṭhu[lyaṁ, prītiḥ] praśrabdhir, letters lost by pin-hole and supplied by Shukla; read dauṣṭhulyapratipraśrabdhiḥ (57.6; Xuanzang 止息身心麁重).

sbh2.0:58.16

sbh2.0:58.19

sbh2.0:58.20
sadānuṣaktaṁ tatsamyaṅmārgabhāvanayā pratipraśrambhayati |
continues to follow, he pacifies it by cultivation of the right path.
sbh2.0:58.21
tatrābhīkṣṇapratyavekṣā |
What is repeated reflection?
sbh2.0:58.22
abhīkṣṇaṁ śīlānyārabhya kukṛtaṁ pratyavekṣate |
With respect to the virtues, he repeatedly reflects on what was done badly
sbh2.0:58.23
sukṛtañca |

[and] reviews what was left undone, and what done;
sbh2.0:58.25

sbh2.0:58.26

sbh2.0:58.27

sbh2.0:58.28

sbh2.0:58.29
tatrāparitamanāya nayā tatkālāntareṇa jñātavyaṁ |
What is non-distress? [As to] what is in time to be known,
sbh2.0:58.30
draṣṭavyaṁ, prāptavyaṁ |
to be seen, to be attained,

mental affliction;

sbh2.0:58.35

[but] abandons it.
sbh2.0:58.36
nirabhimānatā |
What is freedom from conceit?
sbh2.0:58.37
adhigame prāptau |
With respect to direct realization and attainment,
sbh2.0:58.38
sparśanāyāṁ nirabhimāno bhavati |
with respect to touching, he is free from conceit.
sbh2.0:58.39
aviparītagrāhī, prāpte prāpta saṁjñī, adhigate adhigatasaṁjñī |
He grasps without distortion: with respect to what is attained, he has the perception ‘attained’; with respect to what is realized, the perception ‘realized.’
sbh2.0:58.40
itīme daśa dharmāḥ śikṣākāmasya yoginaḥ |
Thus these ten dharmas, for the yogin who desires training,
sbh2.0:59.1
ādimadhyaparyavasānamupādāya śikṣāmanulomayati nti , na vilomayanti |
from beginning to middle to end, conform to training; they do not run counter to it.
sbh2.0:59.2
tenocyante śikṣānulomikā iti |
Therefore they are called dharmas conducive to training.
sbh2.0:59.3
tatra katamo yograbhaṁśaḥ |
What, there, is loss of yoga?
sbh2.0:59.4
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:59.5

four kinds of loss of yoga.
sbh2.0:59.6
katame catvāraḥ |
Which four?
sbh2.0:59.7
asti yogabhraṁśa ātyantikaḥ |
There is final loss of yoga;
sbh2.0:59.8

there is temporary;
sbh2.0:59.9
asti pārihāṇikaḥ |
there is loss-by-falling-away;
sbh2.0:59.10
asti mithyāpratipattikṛtaḥ |
there is loss caused by wrong practice.
sbh2.0:59.11
tatrātyantiko yogabhraṁśaḥ |
There, final loss of yoga
sbh2.0:59.12
ayogasthānāṁ pudgalānāṁ veditavyaḥ |
is to be understood with reference to persons [Conjectural: not established in the lineage].
Printed ayogasthānāṁ; read agotrasthānāṁ (59.15; Xuanzang 無種姓).
sbh2.0:59.13
tasyāparinirvāṇadharmakatvādatyantaparibhraṣṭā eva yogādbhavanti |
Because they are of a nature not to attain nirvāṇa, they are absolutely fallen away from yoga.
sbh2.0:59.14
tatra tāvatkālikaḥ |
There, the temporary:
sbh2.0:59.15
tadyathā gotrasthānāṁ parinirvāṇa dharmakāṇāṁ pratyayavikalānāṁ, te hi dūramapi, paramapi gatvā avaśyameva pratyayānāsādayiṣyanti |
namely, those who, established in the lineage, of a nature to attain nirvāṇa, but lacking the conditions — for they, even after going far and very far, will surely come upon the conditions
sbh2.0:59.16
yogaṁ ca saṁmukhīkṛtya bhāvayitvā parinirvāsyanti |
and, bringing forth yoga, will cultivate and reach final nirvāṇa.
sbh2.0:59.17
tenaiva teṣāṁ tāvatkālika eva bhraṁśo bhavati |
For them, by that very fact, the loss is only temporary.
sbh2.0:59.18

There, loss of yoga by falling away from what has been attained:
sbh2.0:59.19

as when someone here falls away from the knowledge, vision, contact and dwelling that he had attained and gained.
sbh2.0:59.20

sbh2.0:59.21

as when someone here

in yoga; he does not succeed
sbh2.0:59.24

in the wholesome dharma worthy of being meditated upon.
sbh2.0:59.25
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ bahukleśo bhavati |
As when someone here is full of defilements,
sbh2.0:59.26
prabhūtarajaskajātīyaḥ |
of much-dust nature,
sbh2.0:59.27
mahāvijñānaśca bhavati |
and is also of great discernment,
sbh2.0:59.28
mahābuddhiḥ |
great intellect,
sbh2.0:59.29

endowed with great intelligence.

sa śrutamudgṛhṇāti |
He takes up the scriptures,
sbh2.0:59.31
śrutaṁ paryavāpnoti |
masters the scriptures,
sbh2.0:59.32

little or much; and dwelling in the forest, by his preaching of Dharma he gratifies the minds of those who come — householders and renouncers, straight persons, straightforward by nature.

sbh2.0:59.33
kuhanānucaritayā ca ceṣṭayā kāyavākyapratisaṁyuktayā, tasya tena hetubhāvena, tena pratyayenotpadyate |

sbh2.0:59.34

sbh2.0:59.35

sbh2.0:59.36

of robes, alms, dwelling-place, medicine for the sick, requisites, and he is honored, respected by kings, royal ministers, even by caravan leaders, and [Conjectural: reputed to be an arhat].
Printed arhatsampata(da)ḥ; read arhatsaṁmataḥ (59.40; Xuanzang 謂之是阿羅漢).
sbh2.0:59.37
tasyāntā varttante śrāvakāḥ, gṛhiṇaḥ, pravrajitāḥ |
Around him gather disciples, householders, renouncers.
sbh2.0:59.38

[Conjectural: they are attached to him and follow him about, and to draw in more,] — whence there occurs to him:
Printed api adhvādenteṣu gredhaṁ nigamayaṁ vā varttate vā …, corrupt, letters lost (Shukla conjectures grāmo nigamo vā); the sense follows Xuanzang 戀著親愛，隨順而轉。為多招引.
sbh2.0:59.39
santi ye śrāvakā, gṛhipravrajitā, ye mayi saṁbhāvanājātā yeṣāṁ mahatsaṁsa ma taḥ te cedyā nmā mupasaṁkramya yoge manasikāre śamathavipaśyanāyā ṁ praśnaṁ pṛccheyuḥ |
‘There are disciples, householders and renouncers, who hold me in high regard, who hold me as great. If they were to come to me and ask a question concerning yoga, attention, śamatha and vipaśyanā,
sbh2.0:59.40
teṣāṁ cāhaṁ pṛṣṭo vyākuryāṁ na jānāmītyevaṁ sati yā saṁbhāvanā sā ca hīyenna hīyeta, na ca syāmarhatsammataḥ, yannvahaṁ svayameva cintayitvā, tulayitvopaparīkṣya yogaṁ vyavasthāpayeyaṁ |
and I, asked, were to answer, ‘I do not know,’ — then the regard they have would diminish, would lessen, and I would no longer be reputed as an arhat. Better that I myself think it out, weigh it, examine it, and so establish a yoga.’
sbh2.0:59.41
sa etamevārthamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā lābhasatkārābhigṛddha ekākī rahogataḥ svayameva cintayitvā, tulayitvā, tulayitvopaparīkṣya yogaṁvyavasthāpayati |
Making this very thing predominant, greedy for gain and honor, alone and in seclusion, he himself thinks it out, weighs it, examines it, and so establishes a yoga.
sbh2.0:59.42
sa cāsya yogo na sūtre bhavati |
And that yoga of his is not in the discourses,
sbh2.0:59.43
na vinaye saṁdṛśyate |
is not seen in the vinaya,
sbh2.0:59.44
dharmatāṁ ca vilomayati |
and runs counter to the nature of things.
sbh2.0:59.45
mayete ya ete bhikṣavaḥ |
Those monks
sbh2.0:59.46
sūtradharā, vinayadharā, mātṛkādharāsteṣāṁ tadyogasthānaṁ vinigūhati |
who are sūtra-bearers, vinaya-bearers, mātṛkā-bearers — from them he conceals that yoga-topic
sbh2.0:59.47
na prakāśayati |
and does not reveal it.
sbh2.0:59.48
yepyasya śrāvakā bhavanti |
Those who are his disciples,
sbh2.0:59.49
gṛhiṇaḥ, pravrajitāśca, tānapi yogapratiyuktaye ājñāpayati |
householders and renouncers — them too he instructs in the practice of that yoga.
sbh2.0:59.50
tatkasya hetormā, maiva te sūtradharā, vinayadharā, mātṛkādharā, etadyogasthānaṁ śrutvā sūtrevatārayeyuḥ |
‘Why? Lest those sūtra-bearers, vinaya-bearers, mātṛkā-bearers, hearing of this yoga-topic, should bring it to the sūtras,
sbh2.0:59.51
tacca nāvatāraye tare dvinaye, saṁdarśayeyuḥ |
and it would not fit; should display it in the vinaya,
sbh2.0:59.52
tacca na saṁdṛśyate |
and it is not seen [there];
sbh2.0:59.53
dharmatayā upaparīkṣyeta |
should examine it by the nature of things,
sbh2.0:59.54
tacca dharmatāṁ virodhayet |
and it would contradict the nature of things.
sbh2.0:59.55
te ca tato nidānam pratītā bhaveyurapratītavacanaiśca sāṁ codayeyuḥ |
And they, on that account, would not be convinced, and would challenge me with words of disbelief,
sbh2.0:59.56
adhikaraṇāni cotpādayeyuḥ |
and would raise disputes.
sbh2.0:59.57
evamahaṁ punarapi na satkṛtaḥ |
Thus I would once more not be honored,
sbh2.0:59.58
syānna gurukṛto, rājñāṁ rājāmātrāṇāṁ yāvaddhanināṁ śreṣṭhināṁ sārthavāhānāṁ, na ca punarlābhī syāṁ cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārāṇāmiti |
not held as great by kings, royal ministers, the wealthy, the chiefs, the caravan leaders, and would no longer be a recipient of robes, alms, dwelling, medicine for the sick, and requisites.’
sbh2.0:59.59
sa tāmeva lābhasatkārakāmatāmadhipatiṁ kṛtvā adharme dharmasaṁjñī, vinidhāya saṁjñāṁ rūpi ṇī madharmaṁ dharmato dīpayati |
Making that very desire for gain and honor predominant, perceiving as Dharma what is not Dharma, having set up a distorted perception, he elucidates non-Dharma as Dharma,
sbh2.0:59.60
saṁprakāśayati |
proclaims it.
sbh2.0:59.61
tatra yesya dṛṣṭyanumatamāpadyante |
And those who come into his view’s persuasion,
sbh2.0:59.62
tepyadharme |
they too in non-dharma
sbh2.0:59.63
dharmasaṁjñino bhavanti |
have the perception of dharma.
sbh2.0:59.64
mandatvānmohatvātte adharme dharmasaṁjñino yathānuśiṣṭā api, pratipadyamānā mithyāpratipannā eva te veditavyāḥ |
Through dullness and delusion, they have the perception of dharma in non-dharma; even when instructed accordingly and practicing, they are practicing wrongly — so it should be known.
sbh2.0:59.65
ayamevaṁ rūpo mithyāpratipattikṛto yogabhraṁśassaddharmapratirūpako hyasaddharmaḥ saddharmasyāntardhānāya |
This kind of loss of yoga produced by wrong practice is a counterfeit of the true dharma, a false dharma that tends to the disappearance of the true dharma.
sbh2.0:59.66
itīme catvāro yogabhraṁśā dhyāyinā bhikṣuṇā yogācāreṇa parijñeyā varjayitavyāḥ |
These four kinds of loss of yoga are to be recognized and avoided by the meditating monk, the practitioner of yoga.
sbh2.0:59.67
tatra yogaḥ katamaḥ |
What, then, is yoga?
sbh2.0:59.68
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:59.69
caturvidho yogaḥ |
yoga is fourfold,
sbh2.0:59.70
tadyathā śraddhā, chando, vīryaṁ, upāyaśca |
namely faith, desire, energy, and means.
sbh2.0:59.71
tatra śraddhā dvividhādhiṣṭhānā-abhisaṁpratyayākārā prasādākārā ca |
Of these, faith is twofold in basis, and has the aspect of conviction and the aspect of clarity;
sbh2.0:59.72
dharmayuktivicāraṇādhiṣṭhānā pudgalānubhāvādhimuktyadhiṣṭhānā ca |
it is based on examining the reasoning of the dharmas, and based on trust in the power of the person.
sbh2.0:59.73
chandopi caturvidhaḥ |
Desire too is fourfold,
sbh2.0:59.74
tadyathā prāptaye |
namely for attainment,
sbh2.0:59.75
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ uttare vimokṣaspṛhāmutpādayati |
as when someone here engenders eagerness for the higher liberation,
sbh2.0:59.76
vistareṇa pūrvavat |
in detail as before.
sbh2.0:59.77
paripṛcchāyai |
For inquiry,
sbh2.0:59.78
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ spṛhāmutpādayati vistareṇa pūrvavatparipṛcchāyai |
as when someone here engenders eagerness, in detail as before, for inquiry,
sbh2.0:59.79
ārāmaṁ gamanāya, vijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāṁ yogajñānāmantikamaśrutasya śravaṇāya, śrutasya ca paryavadānāya |
for going to the monastery, into the presence of discerning fellow brahma-farers who know yoga, for the hearing of what has not been heard, for the clarification of what has been heard.
sbh2.0:59.80
saṁbhārasamudāgamācchandaḥ |
Desire for the gathering of the requisites;
sbh2.0:59.81
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ śīlasamvarapāriśuddhaye, indriyaṁ saṁvarapāriśuddhaye, bhojane mātrajñatāyāṁ, jāgarikānuyoge, saṁprajānadvihāritāyāmuttarottarāṁ spṛhāmutpādayati |
as when someone here generates progressively higher eagerness for the purification of the restraint of virtue, of the restraint of the senses, knowing the right measure in food, application to wakefulness, dwelling with full awareness.
sbh2.0:59.82
anuyogācchandaḥ |
Desire for application;
sbh2.0:59.83
yā yaḥ sātatyaprayogatāyāṁ satkṛtyaprayo gatā yāṁ ca mārgabhāvanāyāṁ spṛhāmutpādayatyabhilāṣaṁ kartukāmatāmityayaṁ caturvidhaśchandaḥ |
as when one engenders longing, yearning, willingness, in continual application and in respectful application to cultivation of the path — this is the fourfold desire.
sbh2.0:59.84
ityayaṁ caturvidhaśchandaḥ yaduta prāptaye |
namely: for attainment,
Printed text repeats ity ayaṁ caturvidhaś chandaḥ from 59.83 (Shukla’s note).
sbh2.0:59.85

for inquiry,
sbh2.0:59.86

for the gathering of the requisites,

There energy too is fourfold, namely: for hearing, for reflection, for cultivation,
sbh2.0:59.89

sbh2.0:59.95

sbh2.0:59.96

likewise for one who, having entered seclusion, from time to time cultivates śamatha and vipaśyanā; likewise for one who, applied night and day, purifies the mind of the hindrances by walking and sitting —
sbh2.0:59.97

sbh2.0:59.100
upāyopi caturvidhastadyathā śīlasamvaramindriyasaṁvaramadhipatiṁ kṛtvā sūpasthitasmṛtitā |
Means too is fourfold, namely: making the restraint of virtue and restraint of senses predominant, well-established mindfulness;
sbh2.0:59.101

sbh2.0:59.102

sbh2.0:59.104
adhiprajñañca dharmavipaśyanā |

sbh2.0:59.105
sa cāyaṁ yogaścaturvidhaḥ |
This yoga is fourfold
sbh2.0:59.106
ṣoḍaśākāro bhavati |
and is of sixteen aspects.
sbh2.0:59.107

sbh2.0:59.108

sbh2.0:59.115

to the direct realization of the not-yet-realized —
sbh2.0:59.116

sbh2.0:59.117

Therefore these four dharmas are called yoga.
sbh2.0:59.118

sbh2.0:59.127
tatra balavāhano manaskāraḥ katamaḥ |
What is forceful attention?
sbh2.0:59.128
tadyathā ādikarmikasyādhyātmameva cittaṁ sthāpayataḥ |
Namely, for the beginner steadying the mind inwardly
sbh2.0:59.129
saṁsthāpayataśca |
and stabilizing it,

discerning dharmas,
sbh2.0:59.131

until he attains [stable] attention,
sbh2.0:59.132
tāvadasya balavāhano manaskāro bhavati |
his is forceful attention:
sbh2.0:59.133

by force he holds the mind down and establishes it in one-pointedness;
sbh2.0:59.134
tenocyate balavāhana iti |
hence it is called forceful.
sbh2.0:59.135
sa c chidravāhano manaskāraḥ katamaḥ |
What is interrupted attention?
sbh2.0:59.136

[Interrupted attention is] the characteristic-knowing attention of one who has gained attention and proceeds by the worldly or the supramundane path.
sbh2.0:59.137
tathā hi samādhis tatra cintayā vyavasthīyate |

sbh2.0:59.138

not exclusively by the cultivation-mode.
sbh2.0:59.139

sbh2.0:59.140

from beyond the characteristic-knowing attention up to the practice-culminating attention.
sbh2.0:59.141
tathānābhogavāhano manaskāraḥ |
The effortless attention:
sbh2.0:59.142

the fruit-of-practice-culmination attention.
sbh2.0:59.143

[There are] four other attentions, namely: conforming (ānulomika), antidotal (prātipakṣika), gladdening (prasadanīya), and reviewing (pratyavekṣaṇīya).
sbh2.0:59.144
sbh2.0:59.145
tatrānulo miko manaskāraḥ |

sbh2.0:59.146

sbh2.0:59.148

sbh2.0:60.1

sbh2.0:60.2

making which predominant, one reviews the abandoned and not-yet-abandoned state of [the] defilements.

sbh2.0:61.8

Likewise, with vipaśyanā as forerunner,
sbh2.0:61.9

is the cause-sign for a subsequently more intense vipaśyanā; likewise, vipaśyanā with śamatha as forerunner is the cause-sign for a subsequently more intense śamatha.
sbh2.0:61.11

sbh2.0:61.14

sbh2.0:61.16

sbh2.0:62.1
saṁrajyate |
is impassioned,
sbh2.0:62.2
saṁkliśyate |
is defiled.
sbh2.0:62.3
tatra vikṣepanimittaṁ |
The sign of distraction:
sbh2.0:62.4
yenālambananimittena nidānanimittena cittambahirdhā vikṣipyate |
that by which, through the object-sign and the cause-sign, the mind is thrown outward and distracted.
sbh2.0:62.5
tāni punarnimittāni yathā samāhitāyāṁ bhūmau ebhirmanaskārairālambanamadhimucyate |
These signs, moreover, are as in the concentrated plane. When one resolves upon the object by these attentions,
sbh2.0:62.6
katyadhimokṣā bhavanti |
how many resolves (adhimokṣa) are there?
sbh2.0:62.7
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:62.8
navādhimokṣā stadyathā |
nine resolves, namely:
sbh2.0:62.9
prabhāsvaraścāprabhāsvaraśca |
luminous and not-luminous,
sbh2.0:62.10
jaḍḥ, paṭuḥ, parītto, mahadgataḥ |
dull, sharp, limited, great,
sbh2.0:62.11
apramāṇaḥ |
boundless,
sbh2.0:62.12
pariśuddhaḥ |
pure,
sbh2.0:62.13
apariśuddhaśceti |
and not-pure.
sbh2.0:63.1
tatra prabhāsvarodhimokṣo ya ālokanimitte sūdgṛhīte ālokasahagataḥ |
The luminous resolve: when the light-sign is well grasped, [the resolve] is accompanied by light.
sbh2.0:63.2
tatrāprabhāsvarodhimokṣaḥ |
The not-luminous resolve:
sbh2.0:63.3
tadyathā ālokanimitte sūdgṛhīte andhakārasahagataḥ |
namely, when the light-sign is [Conjectural: not well grasped], [the resolve is] accompanied by darkness.
Printed sūdgṛhīte; read asūdgṛhīte (Xuanzang 不能善取).
sbh2.0:63.4
tatra jaḍo ’dhimokṣaḥ |
The dull resolve:
sbh2.0:63.5
yo mṛdvindriyasantānapatitaḥ |
the one falling within the continuum of mild faculties.
sbh2.0:63.6
tatra paṭuradhimokṣo yastīkṣṇendriyasantānapatitaḥ |
The sharp resolve: that falling within the continuum of sharp faculties.
sbh2.0:63.7
tatra parīttodhimokṣaḥ |
The limited resolve:
sbh2.0:63.8
yaḥ parīttaśraddhācha ccha ndasamādhiḥ parīttālambanaśca |
[the one made] with limited faith, desire, concentration, and a limited object.
sbh2.0:63.9
iti manaskāraparīttatayā cālambanaparīttatayā ca parīttodhimokṣaḥ |
Thus, from the limited-ness of the attention and of the object — [there is the] limited resolve.
sbh2.0:64.1
tatra mahadgatodhimokṣaḥ |
The great resolve:
sbh2.0:64.2
tatra yo mahadgataḥ śraddhācchandasahagato mahadgatamvā ālambanamadhimucyate |
the resolve that is accompanied by great faith and desire, or that resolves upon a great object —
sbh2.0:64.3
yodhimokṣa iti |
such a resolve is,
sbh2.0:64.4
manaskāramahadgata ta yā cālambanamahadgatatayā mahadgatodhimokṣaḥ |
from the greatness of attention and the greatness of the object, the great resolve.
sbh2.0:64.5
tatrāpramāṇodhimokṣaḥ |
The boundless resolve:
sbh2.0:64.6
apramāṇa ḥ śraddhācha ccha ndasahagataḥ |
[the one] accompanied by boundless faith and desire,
sbh2.0:64.7
anantamvā aparyanta mālamba namadhimucyate |
or that resolves upon an endless, boundless object.
sbh2.0:64.8
yo ’dhimokṣa iti |
This resolve is,
sbh2.0:64.9
manaskārāpramāṇatayā cālambanapramāṇatayā cāpramāṇādhimokṣaḥ |
from the boundlessness of attention and of the object, the boundless resolve.
Printed -ālambanapramāṇatayā; read -ālambanāpramāṇatayā (64.4; Xuanzang 所緣無量).
sbh2.0:64.10
tatra pariśuddho ’dhimokṣaḥ yaḥ prabhāvitaḥ, pariniṣpannaḥ paryavasānagataḥ |
The pure resolve: [the one] well cultivated, completed, arrived at its conclusion.
Printed prabhāvitaḥ; read subhāvitaḥ (64.11; Xuanzang 已善修).
sbh2.0:64.11
apariśuddho vā punaryo na subhāvito, na pariniṣpanno na paryavasānagataḥ |
The not-pure: [the one] not well cultivated, not completed, not arrived at its conclusion.
sbh2.0:64.12
tatra kati yogasya yogakaraṇīyāni |
How many are the tasks (karaṇīya) of yoga?
sbh2.0:64.13
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:64.14
catvāri |
four.
sbh2.0:64.15
katamāni catvāri |
Which four?
sbh2.0:64.16
tadyathā |
Namely:
sbh2.0:64.17
āśrayanirodhaḥ |
cessation of the basis (āśraya-nirodha),
sbh2.0:64.18
āśrayaparivarttaḥ |
transformation of the basis (āśrayaparivarta),
sbh2.0:64.19
ālambanaparijñānaṁ |
full knowledge of the object,
sbh2.0:64.20
ālambanābhiratiśca |
and delight in the object.
sbh2.0:64.21
tatratrāśrayanirodhaḥ prayogamanasikārabhāvanānuyuktasya yo dauṣṭhulyasahagata āśrayaḥ |
Cessation of the basis: for one engaged in the cultivation of attention in practice, the basis attended by encumbrance
sbh2.0:64.22
so ’nupūrveṇa nirudhyate |
is gradually brought to cessation,
sbh2.0:64.23
prasrabdhisahagataścāśrayaḥ parivarttate |
and the basis transforms into one accompanied by pliancy.
sbh2.0:64.24
ayamāśrayanirodhoyamāśraya parivartaḥ |
This is the cessation of the basis, this is the transformation of the basis —
sbh2.0:64.25
yogakaraṇīyaṁ |
[this is] a task of yoga.
sbh2.0:64.26
tatrālambanaparijñānamālambanābhiratiśca |
[As for] the full knowledge of the object and delight in the object:
sbh2.0:64.27
astyālambanaparijñānamālambanābhiratiḥ |
there is [a] full knowledge of the object and delight in the object
sbh2.0:64.28
āśrayanirodhaparivarttapūrvaṁgamaṁ |
led by cessation and transformation of the basis;
sbh2.0:64.29
yadā cālambanaparijñānamālambanābhiratimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā āśrayo nirudhyate |
[and] when making full knowledge of the object and delight in the object predominant, the basis ceases
sbh2.0:64.30
parivarttate ca |
and is transformed.
sbh2.0:64.31
astyālambanaparijñānamālambanābhiratiḥ |
[Also,] there is [a] full knowledge of the object and delight in the object
sbh2.0:64.32
āśrayaviśuddhipūrvaṁgamaḥ |
led by the purity of the basis:
sbh2.0:64.33
āśrayaviśuddhimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā suviśuddhamālambanajñānaṁ |
making the purity of the basis predominant, [a] perfectly pure knowledge of the object
sbh2.0:64.34
kāryapariniṣpattikāle pravartate |
arises at the time of the completion of the task,
sbh2.0:64.35
abhiratiśca |
and [a] delight [in the object likewise arises].
sbh2.0:64.36
tenocyate catvāri yogasya karaṇīyānīti |
Hence one says: ‘four are the tasks of yoga.’
sbh2.0:65.1
tatra kati yogācārāḥ |
How many practitioners of yoga (yogācāra) are there?
sbh2.0:65.2
āha trayastadyathā |
Answer: three, namely:
sbh2.0:65.3
ādikarmikaḥ, kṛtaparicayaḥ |
the beginner (ādikarmika), the experienced one (kṛta-paricaya),
sbh2.0:65.4
atikrāntamanaskāraśca |
and one who has gone beyond attention (atikrānta-manaskāra).
sbh2.0:66.1
tatrādikarmiko yogācāraḥ |
The beginner practitioner of yoga [is twofold]:
sbh2.0:66.2
manaskārādikarmikaḥ |
beginner with respect to attention,
sbh2.0:66.3
kleśaviśuddhyādikarmikaśca |
and beginner with respect to purification of defilements.
sbh2.0:67.1
tatra manaskārādikarmikaḥ |
The beginner with respect to attention:
sbh2.0:67.2
tatra prathamakarmika ekāgratāyāṁ yāvanmanaskāraṁ na prāpnoti |
the first-time practitioner, [applied] to one-pointedness, so long as he has not attained attention,
sbh2.0:67.3
cittaikāgratāṁ na spṛśati |
[who] has not [yet] touched mental one-pointedness.
sbh2.0:67.4
tatra kleśaviśuddhyādikarmikaḥ |
The beginner with respect to purification of defilements:
sbh2.0:67.5
adhigatepi manaskāre kleśasya cittaṁ vimocayitukāmasya yallakṣaṇapratisaṁvedino manaskārasyārambhaḥ pratigrahaścābhyāsaḥ |
though he has attained attention, [he is one] who, wishing to liberate his mind from defilement, [is engaged in] the undertaking, the holding, [and] the practicing of the characteristic-knowing attention (lakṣaṇa-pratisaṁvedī).
sbh2.0:67.6
ayaṁ kleśaviśuddhyādikarmikaḥ |
This is the beginner with respect to purification of defilements.
sbh2.0:67.7
tatra kṛtaparicayaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the experienced one?
sbh2.0:67.8
lakṣaṇapratisaṁvedinaṁ manaskāraṁ sthāpayitvā tadanyeṣu ṣaṭsu manaskāreṣu prayoganiṣṭhāpanneṣu kṛtaparicayo bhavati |
Apart from the characteristic-knowing attention, when he has become practiced in the other six attentions, up to the practice-culminating attention, he is the experienced one.
sbh2.0:67.9
tatrātikrāntamanaskārāḥ raḥ prayoganiṣṭhāphalamanaskāre veditavyaḥ |
‘One who has gone beyond attention’ is to be known with respect to the fruit-of-practice-culmination attention:
sbh2.0:67.10
atikrānto ’sau bhavati |
he has gone beyond
sbh2.0:67.11
pramo yo gabhāvanāmanaskāraṁ |
the attention of practice-cultivation,
sbh2.0:67.12
sthito bhavati |
[and] is established
sbh2.0:67.13
bhāvanāphale |
in the fruit of cultivation.
sbh2.0:67.14
tasmādatikrāntamanaskāra ityucyate |
Therefore he is called ‘one who has gone beyond attention.’
sbh2.0:67.15
api ca kuśalaṁ dharma c chandamupādāya |
Moreover, from [his taking up] desire for the wholesome dharma onward,
sbh2.0:67.16
prayujyamāno yāvannirvedhabhāgīyāni kuśalamūlāni notpādayati |
while engaged and not yet producing the wholesome roots conducive to penetration (nirvedha-bhāgīya),
sbh2.0:67.17
tāvadādikarmiko bhavati |
for that long he is a beginner.
sbh2.0:67.18
yadā punarnirvedhabhāgīyānyutpādayati |
When, however, he produces the penetration-conducive ones —
sbh2.0:67.19
tadyathā ūṣmagatāni |
namely the warmth (ūṣmagata),
sbh2.0:67.20
mūrdhānaḥ mūrdhnaḥ , satyānulomāḥ |
the summits (mūrdhan),
sbh2.0:67.21
kṣāntayo ntīḥ laukikānagradharmān |
truth-conforming patiences, [and] the supreme worldly dharmas —
sbh2.0:67.22
tadā kṛtaparicayo bhavati |
then he is the experienced one.
sbh2.0:67.23
yadā punaḥ samyaktvaṁ nyāmamavatarati |
When, however, he enters the fixed course of rightness (samyaktva-nyāma),
sbh2.0:67.24
satyānyabhisamāgacchati |
and realizes the truths,
sbh2.0:67.25
aparapratyayo bhavatyananyaneyaḥ |
he becomes one who does not depend on another, who is not led by another
sbh2.0:67.26
śāstuḥ śāsane tadātikrāntamanaskāro bhavati |
in the Teacher’s dispensation — then he is one who has gone beyond attention.
sbh2.0:67.27
parapratyayaṁ manaskāramatikramyāparapratyaye sthitaḥ | tasmādatikrāntamanaskāra ityucyate || tatra yogabhāvanā katamā |
Having passed beyond attention dependent on another, he stands in [attention] not dependent on another; therefore he is called ‘one who has gone beyond attention.’ There, what is the cultivation of yoga?
sbh2.0:67.28
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:67.29
dvividhā |
twofold:
sbh2.0:67.30
saṁjñābhāvanā bodhipakṣyā bhāvanā ca |
cultivation of perception, and cultivation of the [dharmas] on the side of awakening.
sbh2.0:67.31
tatra saṁjñābhāvanā katamā |
There, what is cultivation of perception?
sbh2.0:67.32
tadyathā laukikamārgaprayuktaḥ |
Namely: one engaged on the worldly path
sbh2.0:67.33
sarvāsvadharimāsu bhūmiṣvādīnavasaṁjñā ṁ bhāvayati |
cultivates the perception of disadvantage in all the lower planes.
sbh2.0:67.34
prahā ṇāya vā punaḥ prayuktaḥ |
Or, engaged for [Conjectural: nirvāṇa],
Printed prahāṇāya, Shukla’s filling of a pin-hole; read nirvāṇāya (Xuanzang 涅槃道).
sbh2.0:67.35
prahāṇadhātau, virāgadhātau nirodhadhātau, śāntadarśī prahāṇasaṁjñāṁ, virāgasaṁjñāṁ, nirodhasaṁjñāñca bhāvayati |
in the realm of abandonment, in the realm of dispassion, in the realm of cessation, seeing [them] as calm, he cultivates the perception of abandonment, the perception of dispassion, the perception of cessation.
sbh2.0:67.36
śamathāya vā punaḥ prayuktaḥ |
Or, engaged for śamatha,
sbh2.0:67.37
ūrdhvamadhaḥ saṁjñāṁ śamathapakṣyāṁ bhāvayati |
he cultivates the perception of up-and-down on the śamatha side.
sbh2.0:67.38
vipaśyanāyāṁ prayuktaḥ |
Engaged in vipaśyanā,
sbh2.0:67.39
paścātpunaḥ saṁjñāṁ vipaśyanāpakṣyāṁ bhāvayati |
he cultivates the perception of [Conjectural: before-and-behind] on the vipaśyanā side.
Printed paścātpunaḥ saṁjñāṁ; read puraḥpaścātsaṁjñāṁ (Xuanzang 前後想).
sbh2.0:67.40
vipaśyanāyāṁ prayuktaḥ |
[Conjectural: Of these, the up-and-down perception:]
Printed vipaśyanāyāṁ prayuktaḥ paścātpura, corrupt; the heading follows Xuanzang 上下想者.
sbh2.0:67.41
paścātpura imameva kāyaṁ yathāsthitaṁ yathāpraṇihitaṁ ūrdhvaṁ pādatalādadhaḥ keśamastakātpūrṇaṁ nānāvidhasyāśuceḥ pratyavekṣate |
he reviews this body, just as it is, just as it is set up, upward from the soles of the feet, downward from the hair of the head, full of various impurities.
sbh2.0:67.42
santyasminkāye keśā romāṇīti pūrvavat |
‘In this body there are head-hairs, body-hairs…’ as before.
sbh2.0:67.43
tatra paścātpunaḥ |
[Conjectural: Of these, the before-and-behind perception:]
Printed paścātpunaḥ saṁjñī; read puraḥpaścātsaṁjñā (Xuanzang 前後想者).
sbh2.0:67.44
saṁjñī tathā tadekatyena pratyavekṣaṇānimittameva |
as when, by someone, just the sign of the review
sbh2.0:67.45
sādhu ca, suṣṭhu ca, sūdgṛhītaṁ bhavati |
is well and thoroughly grasped,
sbh2.0:67.46
sumanasīkṛtaṁ, sūtkṛṣṭaṁ |
well attended to, well drawn out,
sbh2.0:67.47
supratividdhaṁ |
well penetrated.
sbh2.0:67.48
tadyathā sthito niṣaṇṇaṁ pratyavekṣate |
Namely: standing, he reviews someone seated;
sbh2.0:67.49
niṣaṇṇo vā nipannaṁ |
seated, one lying down;
sbh2.0:67.50
purato vā gacchantaṁ, pṛṣṭhato gacchanpratyavekṣate |
or, going behind, he reviews one going in front.
sbh2.0:67.51
sā khalveṣā traiyadhvikānāṁ saṁskārāṇāṁ pratītyasamutpannānāṁ vipaśyanākārā pratyavekṣāparidīpitā |
This very thing illustrates vipaśyanā-mode review of the formations of the three times, dependently arisen.
sbh2.0:67.52
tatra yattāvadāha- sthito niṣaṇṇaṁ pratyavekṣate |
There, when it says ‘standing, he reviews someone seated,’
sbh2.0:67.53
anena vartamānena manaskāreṇa anāgatajñeyaṁ pratyavekṣate |
by this present attention, future knowable is reviewed.
sbh2.0:67.54
vartamānāpi manaskārāvasthā utpannā sthitetyucyate |
The present state of attention, having arisen, is called ‘standing.’
sbh2.0:67.55
anāgatā punaḥ jñeyāvasthā |
Future knowable state,
sbh2.0:67.56
anutpannatvādutpādābhimukhatvācca niṣaṇṇetyucyate |
from non-arising and from being on the verge of arising, is called ‘seated.’
sbh2.0:67.57
yatpunarāha |
When it further says,
sbh2.0:67.58
niṣaṇṇo vā nipannaṁ pratyavekṣata ityanena pratyutpannena manaskāreṇātītasya jñeyasya pratyavekṣaṇā paridīpitā |
‘seated, he reviews someone lying down,’ by this, by a present attention the review of the past knowable is illustrated.
sbh2.0:67.59
pratyutpannā hi manaskārāvasthā |
For the present state of attention,
sbh2.0:67.60
nirodhābhimukhā niṣaṇṇetyucyate |
facing cessation, is called ‘seated.’
sbh2.0:67.61
atītā punaḥ niruddhatvājjñeyāvasthā nipannetyucyate |
The past, from being ceased, knowable state, is called ‘lying down.’
sbh2.0:67.62
yatpunarāha |
When further it says,
sbh2.0:67.63
purato vā gacchantaṁ pratyavekṣata ityanena pratyutpannena manaskāreṇa |
‘one going in front he reviews,’ by this, by present attention,
sbh2.0:67.64
anantaraniruddhasya manaskārasya pratyavekṣā paridīpitā |
the review of immediately-preceding-ceased attention is illustrated.
sbh2.0:67.65
tatra ya utpannotpanno manasikāro a nantaraniruddhaḥ sa purato yāyī |
There, the attention which has arisen and immediately ceased: that ‘goes in front.’
sbh2.0:67.66
tatra anantarotpannaḥ |
Printed text repeats 67.67.
sbh2.0:67.67
anantarotpanno manaskāraḥ |
There, the immediately-arisen attention,
sbh2.0:67.68
navanavo ’nantaraniruddhasyānantaraniruddhasya grāhakaḥ |
newly arising, grasping each immediately-ceased one in turn,
sbh2.0:67.69
sa pṛṣṭhato yāyī |
‘goes behind.’
sbh2.0:67.70
tatra śamathaṁ ca vipaśyanāṁ ca bhāvayanstadubhayapakṣyāmālokasaṁjñāṁ bhāvayati |
There, cultivating śamatha and vipaśyanā, he cultivates the perception of light on both sides.
sbh2.0:67.71
iyaṁ saṁjñābhāvanā |
This is the cultivation of perception.
sbh2.0:67.72
tatra bodhipakṣyabhāvanā katamā |
There, what is the cultivation of the [dharmas] on the side of awakening?
sbh2.0:67.73
yaḥ ṣaṭ sapta triṁśatāṁ bodhipakṣyāṇāṁ dharmāṇāmabhyāsaḥ |
The practice of the thirty-seven dharmas on the side of awakening,
sbh2.0:67.74
paricayaḥ |
the familiarization,
sbh2.0:67.75
āsevanā bahulīkāra iyamucyate bodhipakṣyabhāvanā |
the resort, the making-much-of — this is called the cultivation of the [dharmas] on the side of awakening.
sbh2.0:67.76
tadyathā caturṇāṁ smṛtyupasthānānāṁ, caturṇāṁ samyakprahāṇānāṁ, caturṇā ṛddhipādānāṁ |
Namely: the four foundations of mindfulness, the four right exertions, the four bases of psychic power,
sbh2.0:67.77
pañcānāmindriyāṇāṁ, paṁcānāṁ balānāṁ, saptānāṁ bodhyaṁgānāmāmāryāṣṭāṅgasya mārgasya |
the five faculties, the five powers, the seven awakening-factors, and the noble eightfold path.
sbh2.0:67.78
kāyasmṛtyupasthānasya vedanācittadharmasmṛtyupasthānasya |
Foundation of mindfulness in the body, in feelings, in mind, in dharmas.
sbh2.0:67.79
anutpannānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanutpādāya c chandaṁ janayati |
For unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas, that they may not arise, he generates desire,
sbh2.0:67.80
vyāyacchate, vīryamārabhate |
exerts himself, arouses energy.
sbh2.0:67.81
cittaṁ pragṛhṇāti |
He uplifts the mind,
sbh2.0:67.82
pradadhāti |
settles it.
sbh2.0:67.83
samyakprahāṇasya ṇṇāya |
For the right exertion.
sbh2.0:67.84
utpannānāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ prahāṇāya |
For the abandonment of arisen evil unwholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:67.85
anutpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmutpādāya |
For the arising of unarisen wholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:67.86
utpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ sthitaye |
For the stability of arisen wholesome dharmas,
sbh2.0:67.87
asaṁmoṣāya bhāvanāparipūraye ya yo bhāvavṛddhivipulatāyai chandaṁ vyāyacchate |
for non-forgetting, for fullness of cultivation, for augmentation, growth, and expansion, he generates desire and exerts himself.
sbh2.0:67.88
vīryamārabhate |
He arouses energy.
sbh2.0:67.89
cittaṁ pragṛhṇāti, pradadhātīti samyakprahāṇasya chandasamādhiprahāṇasaṁskārasamanvāgatasya |
He uplifts the mind, settles it — this is right exertion.
sbh2.0:67.90
ṛddhipādasya, śraddhāvīryacittamīmānsā māṁsā samādhiprahāṇasaṁskārasamanvāgatasya ṛddhipādasya, vīryacittamīmānsā māṁsā samādhiprahāṇasaṁskārasamanvāgatasya ṛddhipādasya, śraddhāvīryasmṛtisamādhiprajñendriyaśca śraddhāsmṛtisamādhiprajñābalānāṁ smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁgasya |
The bases of psychic power are those endowed with desire-concentration, energy-concentration, mind-concentration, and investigation-concentration, together with the formations of abandonment; then come the faculties of faith, energy, mindfulness, concentration, wisdom; the powers of faith, [Lacuna] mindfulness, concentration, wisdom; and the awakening-factor of mindfulness.
Printed list of the bases corrupt (śraddhā- twice); the four follow Xuanzang 欲勤心觀三摩地. The printed powers lack vīrya (Xuanzang 精進力).
sbh2.0:67.91
samyagdṛṣṭeḥ |
Right view.
sbh2.0:67.92
samyaksaṁkalpasya, samyagvākkarmāntājīvānāṁ, samyagvyāyāmasya, samyaksmṛteḥ samyaksamādheśca |
Right intention, right speech, action, livelihood, right effort, right mindfulness, right concentration.
sbh2.0:68.1
tatra katamaḥ kāyaḥ |
Now, what is the body?
sbh2.0:68.2
katamā kāye kāyānupaśyanā |
What is the contemplation of body in body (kāye kāyānupaśyanā)?
sbh2.0:68.3
katamā smṛtiḥ |
What is mindfulness (smṛti)?
sbh2.0:68.4
katamāni smṛterupasthānāni |
What are the foundations of mindfulness?
sbh2.0:68.5
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:68.6
kāyaḥ pañcatriṁśadvidhaḥ |
the body is of thirty-five kinds —
sbh2.0:68.7
tadyathā ādhyātmiko, bāhyaśca |
namely: internal and external;
sbh2.0:68.8
indriyasaṁgṛhītaḥ |
comprised by a sense-faculty,
sbh2.0:68.9
anindriyasaṁgṛhītaśca |
and not comprised by a sense-faculty;
sbh2.0:68.10
sattvasaṁkhyātā to ’sattvasaṁkhyātā ta śca |
reckoned as a sentient being, and reckoned as not a sentient being;
sbh2.0:68.11
dauṣṭhulyasahagataḥ, praśrabdhisahagataśca |
accompanied by encumbrance (dauṣṭhulya), and accompanied by pliancy (praśrabdhi);
sbh2.0:68.12
bhūtakāyaḥ, bhautikakāyaśca |
elemental body (bhūta) and derived body (bhautika);
sbh2.0:68.13
nāmakāyo, rūpakāyaśca |
name-body (nāma-kāya) and form-body (rūpa-kāya);
sbh2.0:68.14
nārakastairyagyonikaḥ |
the body of a hell-being, of an animal,
sbh2.0:68.15
paitṛviṣayikaḥ |
of a hungry ghost;
sbh2.0:68.16
mānuṣyo, divyaśca |
of a human, of a deva;
sbh2.0:68.17
savijñānakaḥ |
endowed with consciousness,
sbh2.0:68.18
avijñānakaṁ ko vā |
and without consciousness;
sbh2.0:68.19
antaḥkāyo, bahiḥkāyaśca |
inner body and outer body;
sbh2.0:68.20
vipariṇato a vipariṇataśca |
transformed and not transformed.
sbh2.0:69.1
strīkāyaḥ, puruṣakāyaḥ, ṣaṇḍa ḍha kakāyaśca |
Female body, male body, eunuch body;
sbh2.0:69.2
mitrakāyaḥ, amitrakāyaḥ |
friend’s body, foe’s body,
sbh2.0:69.3
udāsīnakāyaśca |
indifferent body.
sbh2.0:69.4
hīnakāyo, madhyakāyaḥ, praṇītakāyaśca, dahnakāyaḥ, yūna yuva kāyo, vṛddhakāyaśca |
Inferior body, middle body, superior body; child body, young body, old body.
sbh2.0:69.5
ayaṁ tāvatkāyaśca prabhedaḥ |
This much is the division of body.
sbh2.0:69.6
tatrānupaśyanā trividhā |
Contemplation (anupaśyanā), in turn, is threefold:
sbh2.0:69.7
yā kāyamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā- śrutamayī vā prajñābhāvanāmayī vā |
with body as predominant, wisdom born of hearing, [Lacuna] or born of cultivation.
Printed śrutamayī vā prajñā bhāvanāmayī vā, two of the three (69.6, 77.68); cintāmayī is lost (Xuanzang 思所成).
sbh2.0:69.8
yayā prajñayā sarvaṁkāyaṁ sarvākāraṁ samyagevopaparīkṣate |
By that wisdom one fully examines the whole body in every aspect rightly,
sbh2.0:69.9
saṁtīrayatyanupraviśati |
ascertains, penetrates,
sbh2.0:69.10
anuvupyate |
[Conjectural: comprehends it].
Printed anuvupyate; read anubudhyate (Xuanzang 隨覺).
sbh2.0:69.11
tatra smṛtipadasya kāyamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā |
Of these, mindfulness: with body as predominant,
sbh2.0:70.1
ye dharmā udgṛhītāsteṣāmeva ca dharmāṇāṁ yorthaḥ |
of the dharmas grasped [by hearing], their meaning
sbh2.0:70.2
cintito, ye ca bhāvanayā sākṣātkṛtā ḥ |
[reflected upon], and what is realized [by cultivation]:
sbh2.0:70.3
tatra vyaṁjane, cārthe ca, sākṣākriyāyāṁ ca yaścetasaḥ asaṁmoṣaḥ sūdgṛhītā vā me te ete dharmā na veti |
with respect to the letter, the meaning, and direct realization, that non-forgetfulness of mind which asks, ‘are these dharmas of mine well-grasped or not,’
sbh2.0:71.1
sūpalakṣitā vā tatra tatra prajñayā na veti |
‘well-marked or not by wisdom in each case,’
sbh2.0:71.2
susaṁsparśitā ḥ susaṁspṛṣṭāḥ tatra tatra vimuktyā na veti rupasthitā bhavatīdaṁ smṛterupasthānaṁ |
‘well-touched by liberation in each case, or not’ — the establishing of mindfulness in these matters is the foundation of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:71.3
api ca smṛtyā rakṣāyai smṛterupasthānaṁ viṣayāsaṁkleśāyālambanopanibaddhāya nibandhanāya ca |
Moreover, the foundation of mindfulness serves for the guarding [done] by mindfulness, for non-defilement in regard to sense-objects, and for binding [it] to the object.
sbh2.0:71.4
tatra smṛtyā rakṣā yathoktaṁ pūrvamevārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati |
Of these, the guarding by mindfulness is as said before: ‘there is established guarded mindfulness,
sbh2.0:71.5
nipakasmṛtiriti |
mindfulness alert.’
sbh2.0:71.6
tatra viṣayāsaṁkleśāya |
Non-defilement in regard to sense-objects is,
sbh2.0:71.7
yathoktaṁ smṛtyā rakṣitamānasaḥ |
as said, ‘with mind guarded by mindfulness,
sbh2.0:71.8
samāvasthācārako, na nimittagrāhī |
proceeding in a state of evenness, he does not seize signs,
sbh2.0:71.9
nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī |
does not seize secondary features,’
sbh2.0:71.10
yāvadvistareṇa rakṣati |
and so on at length, he guards
sbh2.0:71.11
mana indriyaṁ mana indriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
the mind-faculty; with the mind-faculty he undertakes restraint.
sbh2.0:71.12
tatrālambanopanibandhāya |
The binding to the object is,
sbh2.0:71.13
yathoktaṁ caturvidhe ālambane smṛtimupanibadhnataḥ |
as said, the binding of mindfulness to the four objects (ālambana).
sbh2.0:71.14
tadyathā vyāpyālambane, caritaviśodhane, kauśalyālambane, kleśaviśodhane vā ebhistribhirākārairyā sūpasthitasmṛtitā idamucyate smṛterupasthānaṁ |
Namely: pervasive object (vyāpī), conduct-purifying (carita-viśodhana), skill object (kauśalya), defilement-purifying (kleśa-viśodhana) — mindfulness well-established by these three modes is called the foundation of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:72.1
tatra vedanā katamā |
Now, what is feeling (vedanā)?
sbh2.0:72.2
tadyathā sukhā, duḥkhā, aduḥkhāsukhā ca vedanā |
Namely: pleasant, painful, and neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling.
sbh2.0:72.3
tatra sukhāpi kāyikī |
The pleasant is bodily,
sbh2.0:72.4
duḥkhāpyaduḥkhāsukhāpi |
and so too the painful and the neither-painful-nor-pleasant.
sbh2.0:72.5
yathā kāyikī |
As they are bodily,
sbh2.0:72.6
evaṁ caitasikī |
so they are mental.
sbh2.0:72.7
sukhāpi sāmiṣā, duḥkhāpyaduḥkhāsukhāpi |
Pleasant with worldly attachments (sāmiṣa), and likewise the painful and the neither-painful-nor-pleasant.
sbh2.0:72.8
evaṁ nirāmiṣāpi, evaṁ gardha vā śrite tā , naiṣkramyāśritā vedanā, sukhāpi duḥkhāpyaduḥkhāpyaduḥkhāsukhāpi |
So too without worldly attachments (nirāmiṣa); so too dependent on greed (gardhāśrita) and dependent on renunciation (naiṣkramyāśrita) — whether the feeling is pleasant, painful, or neither-painful-nor-pleasant.
sbh2.0:72.9
saiṣā ekaviṁśatividhā vedanā bhavati |
Thus feeling is of twenty-one kinds,
sbh2.0:72.10
navavidhā vā |
or of nine kinds.
sbh2.0:73.1
tatra cittaṁ katamat |
Now, what is mind (citta)?
sbh2.0:73.2
tadyathā- sarāgaṁ cittaṁ, sadveṣaṁ, vigatadveṣaṁ, samohaṁ, vigatamohaṁ, saṁkṣiptaṁ, vikṣiptaṁ, līnaṁ, pragṛhītaṁ |
Namely: mind with lust, [Lacuna] with hatred, without hatred; with delusion, without delusion; contracted, distracted; slack, uplifted;
Printed list omits vigatarāgaṁ cittaṁ (75.3; Xuanzang 離貪心).
sbh2.0:73.3
uddhatamanuddhataṁ, vyupaśāntamavyupaśāntaṁ |
agitated, not-agitated; calmed, not-calmed;
sbh2.0:73.4
samāhitamasamāhitaṁ, subhāvitamasubhāvitaṁ |
concentrated, not-concentrated; well-cultivated, not-well-cultivated;
sbh2.0:73.5
suvimuktaṁ cittamasuvimuktaṁ cittaṁ |
well-released, not-well-released.
sbh2.0:73.6
tadetadabhisamasya viṁśatividhaṁ cittaṁ bhavati |
Thus, in summary, the mind is of twenty kinds.
sbh2.0:73.7
tatra dharmāḥ katame |
Now, what are dharmas?
sbh2.0:73.8
rāgo, rāgavinayaśca |
Lust and the subduing of lust;
sbh2.0:73.9
dveṣo, dveṣavinayaśca |
hatred and the subduing of hatred;
sbh2.0:73.10
moho mohavinayaśca |
delusion and the subduing of delusion;
sbh2.0:73.11
saṁkṣepo, vikṣepaḥ |
contraction and distraction;
sbh2.0:73.12
layaḥ, pragraha, auddhatyamanauddhatyaṁ |
slackness and uplift; agitation and non-agitation;
sbh2.0:73.13
vyupaśamaḥ |
calming
sbh2.0:73.14
avyupaśamassusamāhi ta tā, na susamāhitatā |
and non-calming; well-concentrated and not-well-concentrated;
sbh2.0:73.15
subhāvitamārgatā, na subhāvitamārgatā |
well-cultivated path and not-well-cultivated path;
sbh2.0:73.16
subhāvitamuktatā, na subhāvitamuktatā ca |
being [Conjectural: well-released] and not-well-released.
Printed subhāvitamuktatā; read suvimuktatā (73.5; Xuanzang 善解脫).
sbh2.0:73.17
itīme kṛṣṇaśukla pakṣa vyavasthitā viṁśatidharmā veditavyāḥ |
These twenty dharmas are to be understood as established on the dark and bright sides,
sbh2.0:73.18
saṁkleśavyavadānapakṣye kṣyāḥ |
on the side of defilement and on the side of purification.
sbh2.0:73.19
tatra sukhāvedanā yatsukhavedanīyaṁ sparśaṁ pratītyotpadyate |
Of these, pleasant feeling is that which arises in dependence upon a contact to-be-felt-as-pleasant —
sbh2.0:73.20
sātaṁ, veditaṁ, vedanāgataṁ |
the agreeable, the felt, what belongs to feeling.
sbh2.0:73.21
sā punaryā pañcavijñānasaṁprayuktā |
That which is associated with the five [sense-]consciousnesses
sbh2.0:73.22
sā kāyikī |
is bodily.
sbh2.0:73.23
yā manovijñānasaṁprayuktā sā caitasikī |
That which is associated with mind-consciousness is mental.
sbh2.0:73.24
yathā sukhavedanīyamevaṁ duḥkhavedanīyamaduḥkhāsukhavedanīyaṁ sparśaṁ pratītyotpadyate asātaṁ, naivasātaṁ nāsātaṁ veditaṁ vigatarāgaṁ |
As with ‘to-be-felt-as-pleasant,’ so too with ‘to-be-felt-as-painful’ and ‘to-be-felt-as-neither-painful-nor-pleasant’ — that which arises in dependence on contact: the disagreeable, the neither-agreeable-nor-disagreeable, the felt,
Printed vigatarāgaṁ, bracketed by Shukla as a later hand’s addition; not in Xuanzang.
sbh2.0:73.25
vedanāgatamidamucyate duḥkhā aduḥkhāsukhā vedanā |
what belongs to feeling — this is called painful or neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling.
sbh2.0:73.26
sā punaryā pañcavijñānakāyasaṁprayuktā |
That which is associated with the five [sense-]consciousnesses
sbh2.0:73.27
sā kāyikī |
is bodily.
sbh2.0:73.28
yā manovijñānasaṁprayuktā |
That which is associated with mind-consciousness
sbh2.0:73.29
sā caitasikī |
is mental.
sbh2.0:73.30
yā nirvāṇānukūlā sā nairvedhikī |
That which is favorable to nirvāṇa, penetrative,
sbh2.0:73.31
atyantaniṣṭhatāyai atyantavimalatāyai |
which, to ultimate completion, to ultimate purity,
sbh2.0:73.32
atyantabrahmacaryaparyavasānāyai ya |
to the ultimate consummation of the holy life,
sbh2.0:73.33
samvarttate |
leads —
sbh2.0:73.34
sā nirāmiṣā |
that is without worldly attachments.
sbh2.0:74.1
yā punardhātupatitā, bhavapatitā sā sāmiṣā |
That which has fallen into the realms, fallen into becoming — that is with worldly attachments.
sbh2.0:74.2
yā puna nā rūpārūpya pratisaṁyuktā, vairāgyānukūlā vā, sā naiṣkramyāśritā |
That which is connected with form and the formless [realms], or favorable to dispassion — that is dependent on renunciation.
sbh2.0:74.3
yā punaḥ kāyapratisaṁyuktā, na ca vairāgyānukūlā, sā gardhāśritā |
That which is connected with [Conjectural: the desire realm] and not favorable to dispassion — that is dependent on greed.
Printed kāya-; read kāma- (Xuanzang 欲界繫).
sbh2.0:75.1
tatra sarāgaṁ cittaṁ |
Mind with lust is
sbh2.0:75.2
yadraṁjanīye vastuni rāgaparyavasthitaṁ |
the mind obsessed by lust with respect to a lust-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:75.3
vigatarāṁgaṁ yadrāgaparyavasthānāpagataṁ |
Mind without lust is free from lust-obsession.
sbh2.0:75.4
sadveṣe ṣaṁ ya d dveṣaṇīye vastuni dveṣaparyavasthitaṁ |
Mind with hatred is the mind obsessed by hatred with respect to a hatred-arousing thing.
sbh2.0:75.5
vigatadveṣaṁ yaddveṣaparyavasthānāpagataṁ |
Mind without hatred is free from hatred-obsession.
sbh2.0:75.6
tatra sammo mo haṁ |
Mind with delusion is
sbh2.0:75.7
yanmohanīye vastuni .... tānyetāni ṣaṭcittāni cārasahagatāni veditavyāni |
what, in regard to a delusion-arousing thing, [Lacuna] — these six minds are to be understood as belonging to activity.
After vastuni Shukla marks a gap: the close of the delusion definition and the definition of mind without delusion are lost; Xuanzang has both (癡纏所纏 … 離癡心).
sbh2.0:75.8
tatra trīṇi saṁkleśapakṣyāṇi |
Three are on the side of defilement.
sbh2.0:75.9
trīṇi saṁkleśaprātipakṣikāṇi |
Three are antidotal to defilement.
sbh2.0:75.10
tatra saṁkṣiptacittaṁ yacchamathākāreṇādhyātmamātmanopanibaddhaṁ |
A contracted mind is one bound inwardly, by oneself, by way of śamatha.
sbh2.0:75.11
vikṣiptaṁ |
A distracted mind is
sbh2.0:75.12
yadbahirdhā pañcasu kāmaguṇeṣvanuvisṛtaṁ |
one scattered outwardly upon the five strands of sense-pleasure.
sbh2.0:75.13
tatra līnaṁ cittaṁ |
A slack mind is
sbh2.0:75.14
yatstyānamiddhasahagataṁ, pragrahītaṁ yat prasadanīyenālambanena saṁpratiṣṭhitaṁ |
the one accompanied by sloth-and-torpor; an uplifted mind is one well-established on a gladdening object.
sbh2.0:75.15
uddhataṁ cittaṁ yadati saṁpragrahādauddhatyaparyavasthitamanubaddhacittaṁ yatpragrahakāle cābhisaṁkṣepakāle copekṣā prāptaṁ |
An agitated mind is one obsessed by agitation through excessive uplifting; a [Conjectural: not-agitated] mind is one which, when in the time of uplifting or the time of contraction, attains equanimity.
Printed anubaddha-; read anuddhataṁ (73.3; Xuanzang 不掉心).
sbh2.0:75.16
tatra praśāntaṁcittaṁ yannivaraṇebhyo vimuktamavyupaśāntaṁ punaryadavimuktaṁ |
A calmed mind is one liberated from the hindrances; not-calmed, one not yet liberated.
sbh2.0:75.17
tatra samāhitaṁ cittaṁ yannivaraṇavimokṣānmauladhyānapraviṣṭaṁ, na susamāhitaṁ yadapraviṣṭaṁ |
A concentrated mind is one liberated from the hindrances and entered into the basic dhyāna; not-well-concentrated, one not yet entered.
sbh2.0:75.18
tatra subhāvitaṁ cittaṁ yadasyaiva samādherdīrghakālaparicayānnikāmalābhī bhavatyakṛcchralābhī |
A well-cultivated mind is that of one who, by long practice of this very concentration, attains [it] at will, attains [it] without difficulty,
sbh2.0:75.19
āśusamāpattā |
attains [it] quickly.
sbh2.0:75.20
tatra na subhāvitaṁ cittametadpiryayeṇa veditavyam |
A not-well-cultivated mind is to be understood by the opposite.
sbh2.0:75.21
tatra suvimuktaṁ cittaṁ yatsarvataścātyantataśca vimuktaṁ |
A well-released mind is one completely and entirely released.
sbh2.0:75.22
na suvimuktaṁ |
A not-well-released
sbh2.0:75.23
cittaṁ yanna sarvato nā nā pyatyantato vimuktamitīmāni caturdaśa cittāni |
mind is one not completely or entirely released — thus these fourteen [further] minds
sbh2.0:75.24
vihāragatāni veditavyāni |
are to be understood as belonging to dwelling.
sbh2.0:75.25
tatra nivaraṇabhūviśuddhā ddhi bhūmimārabhya vihāragatānyaṣṭau cittāni veditavyāni |
Beginning from the plane of purification from hindrances, eight minds are to be known as belonging to dwelling:
sbh2.0:75.26
vikṣiptaṁ saṁkṣiptaṁ yāvad vyupaśāntamavyupaśāntamiti |
distracted, contracted, on up to calmed, not-calmed.
sbh2.0:75.27
kleśaviśuddhiṁ punarārabhya vihāragatāni ṣaṭ cittāni yāvatsuvimuktaṁ cittaṁ na suvimuktamiti |
Beginning from purification of defilement, six minds belonging to dwelling: on up to well-released mind, not-well-released.
sbh2.0:75.28
yatpunaḥ satyadhyātmaṁ nivaraṇe asti me nivaraṇamiti jānāti |
When, with hindrance present inwardly, he knows ‘I have a hindrance’;
sbh2.0:75.29
asati nivaraṇe nāsti me nivaraṇamiti jānāti |
when not present, he knows ‘I have no hindrance’;
sbh2.0:75.30
yathā cānutpannasya vivaraṇasyotpādo bhavati |
and how the arising of an unarisen hindrance occurs,
sbh2.0:75.31
tadapi yathā yotpannasya vigamo bhavati |
that too; how, having arisen, it departs,
sbh2.0:75.32
tadapi prajānāti |
that too he knows.
sbh2.0:75.33
yatra sati cakṣuḥsaṁyojane yāvatpunaḥ nmana saṁyojane asti me yāvatpunaḥ manaḥ saṁyojanamiti |
When an eye-fetter is present, on up to a mind-fetter, ‘I have an eye-fetter,’ on up to, ‘I have a mind-fetter’;
sbh2.0:75.34
asati yāvatpunaḥ nmanaḥ saṁyojane nāsti me manaḥ saṁyojanamiti prajānāti |
when none is present, ‘I have no eye-fetter,’ on up to, ‘I have no mind-fetter’ — thus he knows.
sbh2.0:75.35
yathā cānutpannasya yāvanmanaḥ saṁyojanasyotpādo bhavati |
And how the arising of an unarisen mind-fetter occurs,
sbh2.0:75.36
tadapi prajānāti |
that too he knows.
sbh2.0:75.37
yathā cotpannasya nirodho bhavati |
And how the cessation of an arisen one occurs,
sbh2.0:75.38
tadapi prajānāti |
that too he knows.
sbh2.0:75.39
satyadhyātmaṁ smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁge asti me smṛti saṁbodhyaṁgamiti prajānāti |
When the mindfulness-awakening-factor (smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁga) is inwardly present, he knows, ‘I have the mindfulness-awakening-factor.’
sbh2.0:75.40
asati nāsti me prajānāti |
When absent, he knows, ‘I have it not.’
sbh2.0:75.41
yathā cānutpannasya smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁgasyotpādo bhavati |
And how the arising of an unarisen mindfulness-awakening-factor occurs,
sbh2.0:75.42
tadapi prajānāti |
that too he knows.
sbh2.0:75.43
yathā cotpannasya sthitirbhavati |
And how the stability of an arisen one occurs —
sbh2.0:75.44
asaṁmoṣo bhāvanā |
its non-forgetting, its fullness of cultivation,
sbh2.0:75.45
paripūrirbhūyo bhāvavṛddhirvipulatā tadapi prajānāti |
its augmentation, growth, and expansion — that too he knows.
sbh2.0:75.46
satyadhyātmaṁ smṛtisaṁbodhyaṁgamevaṁ dharmavinayavīryapraśrabdhisamādhyupekṣāsaṁbodhyaṁgaṁ veditavyamiti yadevaṁ svabhāvādīnavapratipakṣākāraiḥ saṁkliṣṭadharmaparijñānamidaṁ śarīraṁ dharmasmṛtyupasthānasya, yathā kāye kāyānupaśyanā smṛtyupasthānapakṣame kṣae vaṁ vedanā yāṁ yaccitte yāvaccite dharmeṣu yathāyogaṁ veditavyam |
As with the mindfulness-awakening-factor when present inwardly, so likewise the [remaining six] awakening-factors — [Conjectural: dharma-discrimination], energy (vīrya), [Lacuna] pliancy (praśrabdhi), concentration (samādhi), and equanimity (upekṣā) — are to be understood. The full knowledge of defiled dharmas by way of own-nature, disadvantage, and antidote — this is the body of the foundation of mindfulness in dharmas. As it is for the body — the contemplation of body in body, together with the mindfulness-and-foundation side — so for feelings, up to mind, and dharmas, it is to be understood as appropriate.
Printed dharmavinaya- (also 83.13); read dharmavicaya- (Xuanzang 擇法覺支). prīti is lost from the list (83.14; Xuanzang 喜).
sbh2.0:75.47
tatra kathamadhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
How, then, does he dwell contemplating the body in the body internally?
sbh2.0:75.48
kathaṁ bahirdhā kathamadhyātmabahirdhā |
How externally? How internally-and-externally?
sbh2.0:75.49
yadā adhyātmaṁ pratyātmaṁ satva ttva saṁkhyāte kāye kāyānupaśyī darśī viharati |
When inwardly, in himself, he sees the body in the body reckoned as a sentient being —
sbh2.0:75.50
evamadhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
thus inwardly he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:75.51
kathaṁ bahirdhā kathamadhyātmabahirdhā |
How externally? How internally-and-externally?
sbh2.0:75.52
yadā adhyātmaṁ pratyātmaṁ, yadā bahirdhā asattvasaṁkhyātaṁ rūpamālambanīkarotyevaṁ bahirdhākāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
When externally he takes as object form reckoned as non-sentient — thus he dwells contemplating body in body externally.
sbh2.0:75.53
yadā bahirdhā rdho paraktaṁ sattvasaṁkhyātaṁ rūpamālambanīkarotyevamadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
When externally he takes as object [Conjectural: another’s] form reckoned as sentient — thus he dwells contemplating body in body both internally-and-externally.
Printed paraktaṁ, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 他有情數身色.
sbh2.0:75.54
tatrādhyātmaṁ rūpamupādāya |
Now, when inwardly he takes up subtle form
sbh2.0:75.55
sūkṣmaṁ sattvasaṁkhyātaṁ |
reckoned as a sentient being,
sbh2.0:75.56
yā utpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstānālambanīkurvan |
and takes as object the feelings, mind, and dharmas that have arisen [in dependence on it],
sbh2.0:75.57
adhyātmaṁ vedanā yāṁ , citte, dharmeṣu dharmānudarśī viharati |
internally — in feelings, in mind, in dharmas — he dwells contemplating dharmas in dharmas.
sbh2.0:75.58
bāhyamasattvasaṁkhyātaṁ rūpamupādāya |
When he takes up external form reckoned as non-sentient
sbh2.0:75.59
yā utpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstānālambanīkurvan |
and takes as object the feelings, mind, and dharmas arising thereon,
sbh2.0:75.60
bahirdhā vedanāyāṁ, citte, dharmeṣu dharmānudarśī viharati |
externally — in feelings, mind, dharmas — he dwells contemplating dharmas in dharmas.
sbh2.0:75.61
bahirdhā bāhyaṁ rūpasattvamupādāya |
When externally he takes up outer sentient form
sbh2.0:75.62
yā utpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstānālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā vedanāyāṁ, citte dharmeṣu dharmānudarśī viharati |
and takes as object the feelings, mind, dharmas arising thereon, he dwells contemplating dharmas in dharmas both internally-and-externally.
sbh2.0:76.1
aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode:
sbh2.0:76.2
indriyasaṁgṛhītaṁ rūpamālambanīkurvan, adhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānupaśyī darśī viharati |
when he takes form comprised by a sense-faculty as object, internally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.3
anindriyasaṁgṛhītaṁ |
When, not comprised by a sense-faculty,
sbh2.0:76.4
rūpagatamanupādattamālambanīkurvan bahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
what belongs to form and is unappropriated is taken as object — externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.5
anindriyasaṁgṛhītameva |
When, not comprised by a sense-faculty,
sbh2.0:76.6
rūpamadhyātmamupagatamupādattaṁ rūpamālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānupaśyī darśī viharati |
form, internal and appropriated, is taken as object — internally-and-externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.7
anindriyasaṁgṛhītaṁ rūpagatamanupādattamālambanīkurvan bahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
Printed text repeats 76.3–6 (bracketed by Shukla as a separate hand’s addition).
sbh2.0:76.8
anindriyasaṁgṛhītameva rūpamadhyātmamupagatamupādattaṁ rūpamālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
sbh2.0:76.9
evaṁ pūrvaṁ ṁ trividhaṁ rūpamupādāya |
Thus, taking up the threefold form just described
sbh2.0:76.10
yadu yo tpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstānyathāyogamālambanīkurvan tathādarśī viharatīti veditavyaṁ |
as [the basis for] feelings, mind, and dharmas that have arisen — and taking them as object, he dwells contemplating accordingly. So one should understand.
sbh2.0:76.11
aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode:
sbh2.0:76.12
yatsamāhitabhūmikaṁ praśrabdhisahagataṁ rūpamālambanīkarotyevamadhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
when he takes as object form belonging to the concentrated plane and accompanied by pliancy — thus inwardly he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.13
yatsūkṣmamevādhyātmaṁ samāhitabhūmikaṁ dauṣṭhulyasahagataṁ rūpamālambanīkaroti |
When he takes as object subtle form, inwardly, of the [Conjectural: unconcentrated] plane, accompanied by encumbrance —
Printed samāhita-; read asamāhita- (Xuanzang 不定地).
sbh2.0:76.14
evaṁ bahirdhā kāye kāyānupaśyī darśī viharati |
thus he dwells externally contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.15

When he takes as object another’s form, accompanied by encumbrance or by pliancy —
sbh2.0:76.16
kāyānudarśī viharati |
internally-and-externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.17
evaṁ tadupādāyotpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmā yathāyogaṁ veditavyāḥ | aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
So too the feelings, mind, dharmas arising in dependence on it are to be understood as appropriate. Another mode:
sbh2.0:76.18
adhyātmaṁ bhūtarūpamālambanīkurvannadhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
when he takes inward elemental form as object, inwardly he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.19
bāhyaṁ bhūtarūpamālambanī kurvan bahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
When outer elemental form is taken as object — externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.20
tacca bhūtarūpamupādāya |
And the derived form
sbh2.0:76.21
yadutpannamindriyaviṣayasaṁgṛhītamupādāya |
that has arisen in dependence on that elemental form, comprised in faculties and sense-objects —
sbh2.0:76.22
rūpaṁ cālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
taking [that] form as object, internally-and-externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.23
evaṁ tadupādāya yā utpannā vedanā cittaṁ dharmāstepi yathāyogaṁ veditavyāḥ |
So too with the feelings, mind, dharmas arising in dependence — these are to be understood as appropriate.
sbh2.0:76.24
aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode:
sbh2.0:76.25
yadā savijñānakaṁ kāyamadhyātmamālambananīkaroti |
when inwardly he takes the body endowed with consciousness as object,
sbh2.0:76.26
evamadhyātmaṁ kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
thus inwardly he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.27
avijñānakaṁ rūpaṁ sattvasaṁkhyātaṁ |
When consciousness-less form reckoned as a sentient being
sbh2.0:76.28
vinīlakādiṣvavasthāsvālambanī kurvanbahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
is taken as object — in states such as the blue-livid corpse and so on — externally he dwells contemplating body in body.
sbh2.0:76.29
a sa vijñānakasya ca rūpasyātīte kāle savijñānatāṁ |
And when he takes as object the past conscious state of form without consciousness,
sbh2.0:76.30
avijñāna ka sya ca rūpasyānāgate kāle avijñānatāṁ, tulyadharmatāṁ, samadharmatāṁ ālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharatyevaṁ tadupādāya, yā utpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstepi yathāyogaṁ veditavyāḥ |
and the future unconscious state of form [Conjectural: endowed with consciousness], their like nature and sameness of nature — internally-and-externally he dwells contemplating body in body. So too the feelings, mind, dharmas arising in dependence are to be understood appropriately.
Printed avijñānakasya twice; the second read savijñānakasya (Xuanzang 有識身色於未來時無識性).
sbh2.0:76.31
aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode:
sbh2.0:76.32
ātmanaḥ anta ḥ kāyaṁ keśaromanakhādibhiḥ ākārairālambanīkurvannadhyātmabahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
when one takes one’s own inner body — by way of head-hair, body-hair, nails, and so on — as object, [Conjectural: internally] one dwells contemplating body in body.
Printed adhyātmabahirdhā; read adhyātmaṁ (Xuanzang 於內身).
sbh2.0:77.1
pareṣāmantaḥ kāyaṁ keśaromanakhādibhirākārairālambanīkurvanbahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharatyadhyātmaṁ cittaṁ ca bahiḥkāyavipariṇataṁ vinīlakādibhirākāraiḥ |
When one takes others’ inner body — by way of head-hair, body-hair, nails, and so on — as object, externally one dwells contemplating body in body; and when, [Conjectural: inwardly, the outer body, changed], by way of aspects such as blue-livid,
Printed adhyātmaṁ cittaṁ ca bahiḥkāyavipariṇataṁ; Xuanzang 內表身變異 has no ‘mind’.
sbh2.0:77.2
bahirdhā ca bahiḥkāyaṁ vipariṇatamavipariṇataṁ ca |
and, externally, the outer body, changed and unchanged,
sbh2.0:77.3
vinīlakādibhirākāraistulyadharmatayā ālambanīkurvan bahirdhā kāye kāyānudarśī viharati |
are taken as object by way of aspects such as blue-livid, on the basis of like-naturedness — [Conjectural: internally-and-externally] one dwells contemplating body in body.
Printed bahirdhā; read adhyātmabahirdhā (76.22; Xuanzang 內外身).
sbh2.0:77.4
tadupādāya yā utpannā vedanā, cittaṁ, dharmāstepi yathāyogaṁ veditavyāḥ |
The feelings, mind, and dharmas arising in dependence on that are likewise to be understood as appropriate.
sbh2.0:77.5
ityevaṁbhāgīyā kāye vedanācittadharmaprabhedenabahavaḥ paryāyā veditavyāḥ |
Thus modes of this sort, by way of the distinction into body, feelings, mind, and dharmas, are to be understood as many.
sbh2.0:77.6
ime tu katipayāḥ ye paryāyāḥ |
Here only a few modes
sbh2.0:77.7
saṁprakāśitāḥ |
have been elucidated.
sbh2.0:77.8
tatra caturṇāmviparyāsānāṁ pratipakṣeṇa bhagavatā catvāri smṛtyupasthānāni vyavasthāpitāni |
Now, as antidote to the four perversions (viparyāsa), the Blessed One established the four foundations of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:77.9
tatrāśucau śucīti viparyāse pratipakṣeṇa kāyasmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
As antidote to the perversion ‘[seeing] the pure in the unclean,’ the foundation of mindfulness on the body was established.
sbh2.0:77.10
tathā hi bhagavatā kāyasmṛtyupasthānabhāvanāyāṁ |
Thus by the Blessed One, in the cultivation of the body-foundation-of-mindfulness,
sbh2.0:77.11
aśubhāpratisaṁyuktāścatasraḥ śivapathikā deśitāḥ |
four charnel-ground reflections (śivapathikā) connected with the unattractive are taught,
sbh2.0:77.12
yā asya bahulaṁ kurvan manasikurvataḥ |
which, frequently engaged with and attended to,
sbh2.0:77.13
aśucau śucīti viparyāsaḥ prahīyate |
lead to the abandonment of the perversion ‘the pure in the unclean.’
sbh2.0:77.14
tatra sukhe sukhamiti |
As antidote to the perversion ‘[seeing] the pleasant in [Conjectural: the painful],’
Printed sukhe sukham; read duḥkhe sukham (77.17; Xuanzang 苦中計樂).
sbh2.0:77.15
viparyāsapratipakṣeṇa vedanāsmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
the foundation of mindfulness on feeling is established.
sbh2.0:77.16
vedanānudarśī viharan |
Dwelling contemplating feeling,
sbh2.0:77.17
yatkiṁcidveditamidamatra duḥkhasyeti yathābhūtaṁ prajānātyevamasya yo duḥkhasukhe sukhamiti |
he knows, as it actually is, ‘whatever is felt, that counts as suffering’; and thus, for him, the perversion
sbh2.0:77.18
viparyāsaḥ |
‘pleasant in the painful’
sbh2.0:77.19
sa prahāyate prahīyate |
is abandoned.
sbh2.0:77.20
anitye nityamiti viparyāsaḥ |
As antidote to the ‘permanent in the impermanent’ perversion,
sbh2.0:77.21
pratipakṣeṇa smṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
the foundation of mindfulness [on mind] is established.
sbh2.0:77.22
tasya sarāgādicittaprabhedena teṣāṁ teṣāṁ rātriṁdivasānāmatyayātkṣaṇalavamuhūrttānā ṇā manekavidhānāṁ bahunānāprakāratāṁ cittasyopalabhya yaḥ anitye nityamiti viparyāsaḥ sa prahīyate |
For him, perceiving, through the passing of those nights and days, moments, instants, and hours, the mind’s manifold variety of many kinds — by way of the distinction into minds with lust and so on — the ‘permanent in the impermanent’ perversion is abandoned.
sbh2.0:77.23
yatrā nā tmanyātmeti viparyāsapratipakṣeṇa dharmasmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
And as antidote to the ‘self in the not-self’ perversion, the foundation of mindfulness on dharmas is established.
sbh2.0:77.24
tasya yeṣāṁ ātmadṛṣṭyādikā nāṁ saṁkleśānāṁ sadbhāvādyeṣāṁ nānātmadṛṣṭyādikānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmasadbhāvātskandheṣvātma darśanaṁ bhavati |
For him, on account of the existence of defilements such as self-view and so on, and on account of the non-existence of wholesome dharmas such as the view of non-self, there is the seeing of self in the aggregates
sbh2.0:77.25
nānyasya, svalakṣaṇataḥ |
and not for another. By specific characteristic
sbh2.0:77.26
sāmānyalakṣaṇataśca dharmādharmānudarśino yathābhūtaṁ paśyataḥ |
and general characteristic, for one who dwells contemplating [Conjectural: dharmas in dharmas], seeing as it actually is,
Printed dharmādharmānudarśino, from a blurred photograph (Shukla’s note); read dharmeṣu dharmānudarśino (Xuanzang 於諸法住循法觀).
sbh2.0:77.27
yonātmanyātmeti viparyāsaḥ |
the perversion ‘self in non-self’
sbh2.0:77.28
sa prahīyate |
is abandoned.
sbh2.0:77.29
aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode:
sbh2.0:77.30
prāyeṇa hi loka evaṁ pravṛttaḥ |
for in the world this is how things commonly run.
sbh2.0:77.31
skandheṣu skandhamātraṁ, dharmamātraṁ, yathābhūtamaprajānan yathā kāye āśritaḥ |
In the aggregates, not knowing the mere aggregates, the mere dharmas, as they actually are, [imagines a self] as resting on the body;
sbh2.0:77.32

and the one resting there [Conjectural: experiences] pleasure and pain and is defiled and purified by dharma and non-dharma —
Printed sukhaduḥkha ja, letters indistinct (Shukla’s note); the sense follows Xuanzang 受用苦樂.
sbh2.0:77.33
tatrātmana āśrayavastusaṁmohāpanayanārthaṁ |
for the removal of confusion about the [supposed] self’s basis-of-resort,
sbh2.0:77.34
kāyasmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
the foundation of mindfulness on the body is established.
sbh2.0:77.35
tasyaivātmanaḥ anubhavanavastusaṁmohāpanayanārthaṁ vedanāsmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
For the removal of confusion about that same self’s basis-of-experiencing, the foundation of mindfulness on feeling is established.
sbh2.0:77.36
yatraiva ca te citte, manasi, vijñāne, ātmagrāheṇa saṁmūḍhā, ātmavastusaṁmohāpanayanārthaṁ dharmasmṛtyupasthānaṁ vyavasthāpitam | aparaḥ paryāyaḥ |
And where they are confused by self-grasping in mind, mentality, and consciousness — for the removal of confusion about the self-thing, [Lacuna] the foundation of mindfulness on dharmas is established. Another mode:
After -ārthaṁ the close of the mind-foundation clause and the opening of the dharma-foundation clause are lost; Xuanzang has both (立心念住 … 立法念住).

Through the feelings the mind is defiled by dharmas,
sbh2.0:77.55

and purified.
sbh2.0:77.56
tatra smṛtyupasthānamiti ko ’rtha āha |
Now, what is the meaning of ‘foundation of mindfulness’? Answer:
sbh2.0:77.57
yatra ca smṛtimupasthāpayati |
where one establishes mindfulness,
sbh2.0:77.58
yena ca smṛtimupasthāpayati |
and by what one establishes it,
sbh2.0:77.59
taducyate smṛtyupasthānaṁ |
that is called the foundation of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:77.60
yatra smṛtimupasthāpayati |
Where one establishes mindfulness —
sbh2.0:77.61
tadālambanasmṛtyupasthānaṁ yena smṛtimupasthāpayati |
that is the object-foundation-of-mindfulness (ālambana-smṛtyupasthāna); by what one establishes mindfulness,
sbh2.0:77.62
tatra yā prajñā smṛtiśca samādhisaṁgrāhikā tatsvabhāvasmṛtyupasthānaṁ |
that — the wisdom and mindfulness that uphold concentration — is the own-nature foundation of mindfulness (svabhāva-smṛtyupasthāna).
sbh2.0:77.63
tadanye tatsaṁprayuktāścittacaitasikā dharmāḥ |
And other mental and mental-factor dharmas associated with them
sbh2.0:77.64
saṁsargasmṛtyupasthānaṁ |
are the conjunction-foundation-of-mindfulness (saṁsarga-smṛtyupasthāna).
sbh2.0:77.65
api ca kāyavedanādhipateyo mārgaḥ samutpannaḥ kuśalaḥ sāsravaḥ |
Moreover, the wholesome path arisen with body and feeling [Lacuna] as predominant, with flux
Printed kāyavedanādhipateyo; Xuanzang 身受心法增上 names all four foundations.
sbh2.0:77.66
anāsravaśca |
or flux-less —
sbh2.0:77.67
tatsmṛtyupasthānaṁ |
that is the foundation of mindfulness.
sbh2.0:77.68
sa punaḥ śrutamayaścintāmayo bhāvanāmayaśca |
Further, it is born of hearing, born of reflection, or born of cultivation.
sbh2.0:77.69
tatra śrutacintāmayaḥ |
Of these, the hearing-born and reflection-born
sbh2.0:77.70
sāsrava eva |
are only with-flux.
sbh2.0:77.71
bhāvanāmayaḥ syātsāsravaḥ syādanāsravaḥ |
The cultivation-born may be either with flux or fluxless.
sbh2.0:78.1
sa evaṁ caturṣu smṛtyupasthāneṣu kṛtaparicaya audāriko kau dārikaṁ viparyāsamapīnaya kuśalākuśaladharmābhijñaḥ |
Thus, gaining experience in the four foundations of mindfulness, taking away the gross perversion, knowing both wholesome and unwholesome dharmas,
sbh2.0:78.2
tadanantaramanutpannānāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanutpādāya |
immediately afterwards [he undertakes the four right exertions]: for the non-arising of unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas,
sbh2.0:78.3
utpannānāṁ prahāṇāya |
for the abandonment of arisen ones,
sbh2.0:78.4
anutpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmutpādāya |
for the arising of unarisen wholesome dharmas,
sbh2.0:78.5
utpannānāṁ sthitaya iti vistareṇa pūrvavadyāvaccittaṁ pragṛhṇāti |
for the stability of arisen ones — in detail as before, on up to ‘he uplifts the mind,
sbh2.0:78.6
pradadhāti |
settles it.’
sbh2.0:79.1
tatra katame pāpakā akuśalā dharmā ḥ |
Now, what are evil unwholesome dharmas?
sbh2.0:79.2
yatkāmāvacaraṁ kliṣṭaṁ kāyakarma, vākkarma, manaskarma, kāyavāṅmanoduścaritasaṁgṛhītaṁ |
Whatever, in the realm of desire, is the defiled bodily action, verbal action, or mental action — comprised in the wrong-conduct of body, speech, and mind.
sbh2.0:79.3
yena tatsamutthāpakāḥ kleśāste punarye asamavahitā, asaṁmukhībhūtāste utpannā, ye samavahitāḥ saṁmukhībhūtāste utpannāḥ |
And the defilements which engender these: those not yet assembled, not yet manifest, are the unarisen; those that have assembled and are manifest are the arisen.
sbh2.0:79.4
tatra kuśalā dharmā ye tatprātipakṣikā dharmāduścaritaprātipakṣikā, nivaraṇaprātipakṣikāḥ, saṁyojanaprātipakṣikā vā tepyanutpannāstathaiva veditavyāḥ |
The wholesome dharmas — those that are antidotal to them, antidotal to misconduct, antidotal to hindrances, or antidotal to fetters — these too, unarisen and arisen, are to be understood in the same way as the evil unwholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:79.5
utpannāśca pāpakā akuśalā dharmāstatra yadā anutpannānāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanutpādāya spṛhāmutpādayati |
When, for the non-arising of unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas, he generates eagerness,
sbh2.0:79.6
praṇidhatte, sarveṇa sarvaṁ sarvathā notpādayiṣyāmītyevaṁ chandaṁ janayati |
he sets his mind [on it]: ‘I will not, completely, entirely, in any way, let them arise.’ Thus he generates desire.
sbh2.0:79.7
utpannānvā punaḥ samavahitānsarveṇasarvaṁ nādhivāsayiṣyāmi prahāsyāmi |
Or of those that have arisen, assembled: ‘I will not tolerate them at all, I shall abandon them,
sbh2.0:79.8
prativinodayiṣyāmi ya da nutpanneṣu pāpakeṣvakuśaleṣu pūrva mevotpāda |
shall dispel them.’ And when evil unwholesome [dharmas formerly] unarisen [Conjectural: have already arisen],
Printed yad anutpanneṣu … pūrvam evotpāda, the last word without ending; the sense follows Xuanzang 已生 … 先已和合.
sbh2.0:79.9
spṛhāmutpādayati |
he generates eagerness,
sbh2.0:79.10
praṇidhatte |
sets his mind.
sbh2.0:79.11
nādhivāsaya yi tukāmo bhavati |
He becomes one who wishes not to tolerate [them].
sbh2.0:79.12
ayamutpannānāṁ prahāṇāya c chandaḥ |
This is the desire for the abandonment of arisen ones.
sbh2.0:79.13
te punaḥ pāpakā akuśalā dharmā atītavastvālambanā vā, anāgatavastvālambanā vā, vartamānaviṣayālambanā vā utpadyante, bhavanti |
These evil unwholesome dharmas arise taking past, future, or present objects.
sbh2.0:79.14
yenoktaviṣayālambanā ḥ , pratyakṣaviṣayālambanāśca ye atītānāgatāvasthālambanāste, ye coktaviṣayālambanā, ye vartamānaviṣayālambanāste pratyakṣaviṣayālambanā ḥ |
They have [Conjectural: remote] objects or perceptible objects: those with past or future states as objects have [Conjectural: remote] objects; those with present objects have perceptible objects.
Printed yenokta- (twice); read parokṣa- (79.15; Xuanzang 不現見境).
sbh2.0:79.15
tatra parokṣālambanānāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanutpādāya |
Now, for the non-arising of unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas with remote objects,
sbh2.0:79.16
utpannānāṁ ca prahāṇāya |
and for the abandonment of arisen ones,
sbh2.0:79.17
vyāyacchate |
he exerts himself.
sbh2.0:79.18
pratyakṣaviṣayālambananānāṁ punaḥ |
And as for those with perceptible objects —
sbh2.0:79.19
anutpannānāmanutpādāyotpannānāṁ ca prahāṇāya vīryamārabhate |
for the non-arising of unarisen and the abandonment of arisen, he arouses energy.
sbh2.0:79.20
tathā hi teṣāṁ dṛḍhatareṇa vīryāraṁbheṇānutpattiḥ |
For only by firm rousing of energy is there non-arising of these,
sbh2.0:79.21
prahāṇaṁ vā bhavati |

sbh2.0:79.22
api ca mṛdumadhyānāṁ samavasthānāmanutpannānāmanutpādāya |
Moreover, for the non-arising of unarisen mild and middling [Conjectural: obsessions],
Printed samavasthāna- here and at 79.24, 80.13, 80.17, 80.21, 80.36, 80.37; read paryavasthāna- (Xuanzang 纏; cf. 79.132).
sbh2.0:79.23
utpannānāṁ prahāṇāya vyāyacchate |
and for the abandonment of arisen ones, he exerts himself.
sbh2.0:79.24
adhimātrāṇāṁ samavasthānāṁ anutpannānāmanutpādāya |
For the non-arising of unarisen strong [Conjectural: obsessions],
sbh2.0:79.25
utpannānāṁ ca prahāṇāya vīryamārabhate |
and for the abandonment of arisen ones, he arouses energy.
sbh2.0:79.26
sa cedatīte ālambane carati |
If he moves in a past object,
sbh2.0:79.27

he moves in such a way
sbh2.0:79.28

sbh2.0:79.30

sbh2.0:79.31

sbh2.0:79.32

As with past objects, so future is to be understood.
sbh2.0:79.33

Thus it is said: for the non-arising of unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas and for the abandonment of arisen ones, he exerts himself.
sbh2.0:79.34

If he moves in a present object, he moves in such a way
sbh2.0:79.35
yathā tenālambanena kleśo notpadyate |
that no defilement arises through that object.
sbh2.0:79.36

sbh2.0:79.38

he abandons,
sbh2.0:79.39

dispels,
sbh2.0:79.40

puts an end to it.
sbh2.0:79.41
evamanutpannānāṁ pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanutpādāya |
Thus it is said: for the non-arising of unarisen evil unwholesome dharmas,
sbh2.0:79.42
utpannānāñca prahāṇāya vīryamārabhata ityucyate |
and for the abandonment of arisen ones, he arouses energy.
sbh2.0:79.43
santi pāpakā akuśalā dharmā ye saṁkalpavaśe bale notpadyante |
Some evil unwholesome dharmas arise through the power of thought,
sbh2.0:79.44

not through the power of the object;
sbh2.0:79.45

some arise through both —
sbh2.0:79.46

through the power of thought and the power of the object.
sbh2.0:79.47
tatra saṁkalpabalenotpadyante |
Of these, the ones that arise through the power of thought are,
sbh2.0:79.48
tadyathā viharataḥ |
as when dwelling,
sbh2.0:79.49

sbh2.0:79.50
tatra saṁkleśavaśe le na ca viṣayabalena cotpadyate nte |
And the ones that arise through the power of [Conjectural: thought] and the power of the object are,
Printed saṁkleśa-; read saṁkalpa- (79.45; Xuanzang 思惟力).
sbh2.0:79.51
tadyathā carato vartamānenālambanenotpadyante |

sbh2.0:79.52
avaśyaṁ tatrāyoniśaḥ saṁkalpo bhavati |
In that case too there is necessarily improper thought (ayoniśaḥ saṁkalpa).
sbh2.0:79.53

he exerts himself.

sbh2.0:79.57
