for the non-arising of unarisen ones and the abandonment of arisen ones, he arouses energy.
sbh2.0:79.60
tatrānutpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmanu mu tpādāya chandaṁ janayatīti |
Now, ‘for the arising of unarisen wholesome dharmas, he generates desire’ means:
sbh2.0:79.61
ye kuśalādharmā apratilabdhā a saṁmukhībhūtasya tāḥ teṣāṁ pratilambhāya saṁmukhībhāvāya ca smṛti mutpādayati |
for those wholesome dharmas not yet acquired, not yet manifest, he generates [Conjectural: eagerness] for their acquisition and manifestation.
Printed smṛtim; read spṛhām (79.71; Xuanzang 希願).
sbh2.0:79.62
cittaṁ praṇidhatte |
He sets his mind [on them].
sbh2.0:79.63

Intense desire to obtain [them],
sbh2.0:79.64
saṁmukhīkartukāmatā cāsya pratyupasthitā bhavati |
intense desire to bring [them] to manifestation, is present in him.
sbh2.0:79.65
ayamanutpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmutpattaye |
This is [what is meant by ‘desire’] for the arising of unarisen wholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:79.66
kṛ ya ttu utpannānāṁ ca kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ sthitaye, asaṁmoṣāya, bhāvanāparipūraye chandaṁ janayatīti |
‘For the stability, non-forgetting, fullness of cultivation of arisen wholesome dharmas, he generates desire’ means:
sbh2.0:79.67
utpannāḥ kuśalā dharmā ye pratilabdhāssaṁmukhībhūtāśca, tatra pratilaṁbhāvigamaṁ pratilabdhāṁ pārihāṇimadhikṛtyāha |
those wholesome dharmas which, having arisen, are acquired and manifest — with respect to non-loss of what has been acquired, with respect to non-falling-away from what has been acquired, [the text] says ‘for the stability’;
sbh2.0:79.68
sthitaya iti saṁmukhībhāvādadhandhāyitatvamadhikṛtyāhāsaṁmoṣāyeti |
with respect to its being clearly manifest, without dullness, [the text] says ‘for the non-forgetting’;
sbh2.0:79.69
teṣāmeva ca kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmpratilabdhānāṁ sammukhībhūtānāmāsevanānvayātpariniṣpattiṁ niṣṭhāgamanamadhikṛtyāha |
with respect to the bringing of those very acquired and manifest wholesome dharmas to fullness, to completion by resort, [the text] says
sbh2.0:79.70

‘for the fullness of cultivation.’
sbh2.0:79.71
tatra ca spṛhāmutpādayati |
He generates eagerness,
sbh2.0:79.72
cittaṁ praṇidhatte |
sets his mind.
sbh2.0:79.73
tīvrā cāsya sthitikāmatā asammoṣakāmatā |

sbh2.0:79.74
bhāvanāparipūrikāmatā pratyupasthitā bhavati |
and desire-for-fullness-of-cultivation are present.
sbh2.0:79.75
ayamucyate |
This is called
sbh2.0:79.76

the desire for stability, non-forgetting, and fullness of cultivation of arisen wholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:79.77
tatra vyāyacchata iti |
Now, ‘he exerts himself’ means:
sbh2.0:79.78

for making present what has been acquired. ‘He arouses energy’:
sbh2.0:79.79
apratilabdhānāṁ pratilambhāya |

sbh2.0:79.80

sbh2.0:79.81

sbh2.0:79.82

sbh2.0:79.83

sbh2.0:79.84

sbh2.0:79.85
tatra cittaṁ pragṛhṇāti |
He uplifts the mind:
sbh2.0:79.86

sbh2.0:79.88

and, for the arisen, on up to the fullness of cultivation, arouses energy,

Printed text repeats 79.85–89.
sbh2.0:79.91
yadā taccittaṁ śamathabhāvanāyāmekāgratāyāṁ prayuktaṁ bhavati |
sbh2.0:79.92

sbh2.0:79.94
asaṁmoṣāya bhāvanāparipūraye |
sbh2.0:79.95

sbh2.0:79.96

sbh2.0:79.97

sbh2.0:79.109

in detail; the [other] two right exertions are to be understood.
sbh2.0:79.110
tadyathā kṛṣṇapakṣyāṇāṁ tatraikaṁ samvaraṇaprahāṇaṁ yadutpannānāmpāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ prahāṇāya c chandaṁ janayatīti vistareṇa |
Namely: of the dark side, the first is restraint-abandonment (samvaraṇa-prahāṇa), which is ‘for the abandonment of arisen evil unwholesome dharmas, he generates desire,’ in detail.
sbh2.0:79.111
dvitīyaṁ prahāṇaprahāṇaṁ yadanutpannānāmanutpādāya c chandaṁ janayatīti vistareṇa, utpannaṁ hi saṁvarayitavyaṁ |
The second is abandonment-of-abandonment (prahāṇa-prahāṇa): ‘for the non-arising of unarisen ones, he generates desire,’ in detail. For the arisen is to be restrained —
sbh2.0:79.112
pāpakaṁ ca vastu |
the evil thing —
sbh2.0:79.113

and the unarisen, since it is abandoned by its not occurring, is thus to be abandoned by its not coming to manifest; on that account,
sbh2.0:79.114
prahīṇasya prahāṇaṁ prahāṇaprahāṇa ṁ |
the abandonment of what is abandoned is abandonment-of-abandonment.
sbh2.0:79.115
tatra bhāvanāprahāṇamekaṁ yadāha |
Then there is one cultivation-abandonment (bhāvanā-prahāṇa), as it says:
sbh2.0:79.116
anutpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmutpādāyeti vistareṇa yāvaccittaṁ pragṛhṇāti, pradadhātīti |
‘For the arising of unarisen wholesome dharmas,’ in detail on up to ‘he uplifts the mind, settles it.’
sbh2.0:79.117
tathā hi kuśalā dharmā āsevyamānā, bhāvyamānā, apratilabdhāśca pratilabhyante |
For wholesome dharmas, resorted to and cultivated, both come to be acquired when not previously acquired,
sbh2.0:79.118
pratilabdhāśca sammukhīkriyante |
and are made manifest when acquired.
sbh2.0:79.119
tatrānurakṣaṇāprahāṇamekaṁ |
And there is one protection-abandonment (anurakṣaṇā-prahāṇa),
sbh2.0:79.120
yadāha |
as it is said:
sbh2.0:79.121
utpannānāṁ kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ sthitaye |
‘for the stability of arisen wholesome dharmas,’
sbh2.0:79.122
vistareṇa yāvaccittaṁ pragṛhṇāti |
in detail on up to ‘he uplifts the mind,
sbh2.0:79.123
pradadhāti |
settles it.’
sbh2.0:79.124
tathā hi pratilabdheṣu saṁmukhīkṛteṣu ca |
For wholesome dharmas once acquired and manifest,
sbh2.0:79.125
kuśaleṣu dharmeṣu yāvatpramādavarjanā apramādaniṣevaṇā ca |
the avoidance of negligence and resort to heedfulness
sbh2.0:79.126
sā kuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ sthitaye, asammoṣāya, bhāvanāparipūraye |
is for their stability, non-forgetting, and fullness of cultivation.
sbh2.0:79.127
evamanutpannāḥ kuśalā dharmā anurakṣitā bhavantyayaṁ tāvatsamyakprahāṇānāṁ vistaravibhāgaḥ |
Thus the [Conjectural: arisen] wholesome dharmas are protected — this is the detailed division of right exertions.
Printed anutpannāḥ; read utpannāḥ (79.125–126; Xuanzang 已生).
sbh2.0:79.128
samāsata sārtha ḥ punaḥ katamaḥ |
What, then, is their summary meaning?
sbh2.0:79.129
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:79.130
kṛṣṇaśuklapākṣikasya tyāgātpunarvastunaḥ |
for the relinquishing of the dark-side object
sbh2.0:79.131
prāptaye pūrvameva spṛhāyuktena bhavitavyaṁ |
and for the acquisition of the bright-side object, eagerness must be present beforehand.
sbh2.0:79.132
paryavasthānaprahāṇāya ca |
And for the abandonment of obsession (paryavasthāna) [it must be present].
sbh2.0:79.133
astyāśayasampatprayogasampacca |
there is excellence of intention and excellence of application —
sbh2.0:79.134
paridīpitā bhavati |
[this] is elucidated.
sbh2.0:79.135
tatrāsyā styā śayasampat |
Excellence of intention
sbh2.0:79.136
chandajananatayā, prayogasampatpunaḥ vyāyāmavīryārambhacittapragrahāpramādadhanaiḥ |
[is achieved] by generation of desire; excellence of application, by striving, arousing energy, uplifting the mind, and [Conjectural: settling it].
Printed -apramādadhanaiḥ; read -pradadhanaiḥ (79.108; Xuanzang 持心).
sbh2.0:79.137
etāvacca yoginā karaṇīyaṁ |
This much is the yogin’s task:
sbh2.0:79.138
yatprahātavyasya vustanaḥ prahāṇāya, prāptavyasya vastunaḥ prāptaye pūrvameva spṛhājātena bhavitavyaṁ, paryavasthānaprahāṇāya, vīryamārabdhavyamanuśayaprahāṇāya ca, kālena kālaṁ śamathapragrahopekṣānimittāni bhāvayitavyāni |
for the abandonment of what is to be abandoned and for the attainment of what is to be attained, eagerness must arise beforehand; for abandoning obsession, and for abandoning latent tendencies (anuśaya), he must arouse energy; from time to time he should cultivate the signs of śamatha, uplifting, and equanimity.
sbh2.0:79.139
paryavasthānaprahāṇānuśayaprahāṇāya ca ye prātipakṣikā dharmāḥ kuśalāste samudānayitavyāḥ |
And for the abandonment of obsession and of the latent tendency, the wholesome dharmas antidotal to them are to be accumulated.
sbh2.0:79.140
taccaitatsarvaṁ caturbhiḥ samyakprahāṇaiḥ paridīpitaṁ bhavatyayaṁ samāsārthaḥ |
All this is elucidated by the four right exertions — this is the summary meaning.
sbh2.0:80.1
tatra catvāraḥ samādhayaḥ, tadyathā chandasamādhiḥ |
Now, the four concentrations [of the bases of psychic power], namely: concentration of desire (chanda-samādhi),
sbh2.0:80.2
vīryasamādhiḥ, cittasamādhiḥ, mīmāṁsāsamādhiśca |
concentration of energy (vīrya-samādhi), concentration of mind (citta-samādhi), and concentration of investigation (mīmāṁsā-samādhi).
sbh2.0:80.3
tatra chanda samādhi madhipatiṁ kṛtvā yaḥ pratilabhyate samādhirayaṁ chandasamādhiḥ |
The concentration acquired by making desire predominant is the concentration-of-desire.
sbh2.0:80.4
vīryaṁ, cittaṁ, mīmāṁsāmadhipati kṛtvā pratilabhyate pratilabhyate samādhirayaṁ ........... mīmānsā māṁsā samādhiḥ |
The concentration acquired by making energy, mind, or investigation predominant is [Lacuna] the concentration of investigation.
Printed ayaṁ …… mīmāṁsāsamādhiḥ, the gap marked by Shukla; the energy- and mind-concentrations are lost (Xuanzang 勤三摩地、心三摩地).
sbh2.0:80.5
yadā tāvadayaṁ chandameva kevalaṁ janayati |
When he generates desire alone,
sbh2.0:80.6
chandajātaśca tānpāpakānakuśalāndharmān svabhāvato, nidānata, ādīnavataḥ, pratipakṣataśca |
and, with desire arisen, with respect to those evil unwholesome dharmas in their own-nature, cause, disadvantage, and antidote,
sbh2.0:80.7
samyagevopanidadhyāti |
rightly contemplates them,
sbh2.0:80.8
ekāgrāṁ smṛtiṁ pravarttayati |
he establishes one-pointed mindfulness.
sbh2.0:80.9
evaṁ kuśalā n dharmāssva dharmānsva bhāvato nidānataśca |
Likewise, with respect to the wholesome dharmas — in their own-nature, cause,
sbh2.0:80.10
akuśalato niḥsaraṇataḥ samyagevopanidadhyā dhā ti |
[Conjectural: benefit], and way-out — he rightly contemplates.
Printed akuśalato; read anuśaṁsato (80.28; Xuanzang 功德).
sbh2.0:80.11
ekāgrāṁ smṛtimavasthāpayati |
He establishes one-pointed mindfulness.
sbh2.0:80.12
tadbahulākārāmekāgratāṁ spṛśati |
He touches one-pointedness that abounds in that mode,
sbh2.0:80.13
samavasthānasamudācāradūrīkaraṇayogena |
by the method of distancing the manifestation of [Conjectural: obsession],
sbh2.0:80.14
na tvasyāpyanuśayamutpādayati |
but he does not [Conjectural: eradicate] even the latent tendency (anuśaya)
Printed utpādayati; read udghātayati (80.21; Xuanzang 永害).
sbh2.0:80.15
pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmayamapyucyate |
of evil unwholesome dharmas. This too is called
sbh2.0:80.16
chandādhipateyaḥ |
the concentration with desire as predominant.
sbh2.0:80.17
sa sā atītā te vā, anāgatapratyutpanne vā punarālambane pāpakākuśalādharmāsthānīye ladharmasthānīye mṛdumadhyādhimātrakleśasamavasthānīye, anutpannasya vā anutpādāya, utpannasya vā prahāṇāya, vyāyacchamāno vīryamāramamāṇaḥ |
When, in a past, future, or present object — serving as a basis for evil unwholesome dharmas and for mild, middling, or strong defilement-[Conjectural: obsession] — for the non-arising of unarisen or the abandonment of arisen [dharmas], he exerts himself and arouses energy,
sbh2.0:80.18
tatrālambane vicaratyantasya vālambanasya svabhāvato nidānataḥ pratipakṣataśca |
moving in that object, and with respect to that object, by way of own-nature, cause, and antidote,
sbh2.0:80.19
samyagupanidhyāyataḥ |
rightly contemplating,
sbh2.0:80.20
ekāgrāṁ smṛtimupasthāpayato yattadbahulavihāriṇaścittaikāgratā utpadyate |
establishing one-pointed mindfulness — then by the frequent dwelling-mode the one-pointedness of mind that arises
sbh2.0:80.21
samavasthānadūrīkaraṇayogena tvasyāpyanuśayamudghātayati |
by the method of distancing [Conjectural: obsession], but [Conjectural: does not] eradicate the latent tendency either
Printed tv asyāpy; read na tv asyāpy (80.14; Xuanzang 而未永害煩惱隨眠).
sbh2.0:80.22
pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāmayaṁ vīryādhipateyaḥ |
of evil unwholesome dharmas. This is the energy-predominant
sbh2.0:80.23
samādhi ḥ |
concentration.
sbh2.0:80.24
līnamvā punaścittaṁ pragṛhṇataḥ |
Again, for one uplifting a slack mind,
sbh2.0:80.25
pragṛhītaṁ cittaṁ samādadhataḥ |
concentrating the uplifted mind,
sbh2.0:80.26
kālena ca kālamadhyupekṣitaḥ |
and from time to time regarding it with equanimity,
sbh2.0:80.27
yatpāpakānāmakuśalānāndharmāṇāṁ pāpakākuśalā la dharmasthānīyānvayāt kuśala ta ḥ |
[for one] rightly contemplating the evil unwholesome dharmas [Conjectural: and] the dharmas that serve as bases for evil unwholesome [dharmas],
Printed -sthānīyānvayāt kuśalataḥ, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 若能隨順惡不善法，及諸善法.
sbh2.0:80.28
kuśalāndharmān kuśalākuśalasthānīyāṁśca dharmān svabhāvato, nidānata, ādīnavataḥ, anuśaṁsataḥ, pratipakṣato, niḥsaraṇataḥ, samyagupani da dhyāyataḥ |
and the wholesome dharmas and the dharmas that serve as bases for wholesome and unwholesome [dharmas], in own-nature, cause, disadvantage, benefit, antidote, and way-out;
sbh2.0:80.29
ekāgratāṁ smṛtimupasthāpayataḥ |
establishing one-pointed mindfulness
sbh2.0:80.30
tadbahulavihāriṇo yā utpadyante cittasyaikāgratāḥ vistareṇa yāvadayaṁ |
by frequent dwelling, the one-pointedness of mind that arises, in detail on up to: this is
sbh2.0:80.31
cittādhipateyaḥ samādhiḥ |
the mind-predominant concentration.
sbh2.0:80.32
tatra ye pāpakākuśalā la dharmasthānīyā dharmā bhavanti |
Now, the dharmas that serve as bases for evil unwholesome dharmas
sbh2.0:80.33
ayoniśo manasikurvataḥ |
for one of unwise attention
sbh2.0:80.34
ta eva kuśaladharmasthānīyā bhavanti |
become the very same dharmas that serve as bases for wholesome [dharmas]
sbh2.0:80.35
yoniśo manasikurvataḥ |
for one of wise attention.
sbh2.0:80.36
tasyaivaṁ samavasthāneṣu dūrīkṛteṣu |
When, in him, the [Conjectural: obsessions] have been distanced,
sbh2.0:80.37
samavasthānapratipakṣe ca |
and when, as the antidote to [Conjectural: obsession],
sbh2.0:80.38
samādhipramukheṣu dharmeṣvanutpanneṣu te pāpakā akuśalā dharmā dharma mudā haranti |
the dharmas headed by concentration have [Conjectural: arisen], those evil unwholesome dharmas [Conjectural: do not occur].
Printed dharmeṣv anutpanneṣu … dharmā dharma mudā haranti, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 能生起 … 皆不現行 (the antidotes arisen, the evil dharmas do not occur).
sbh2.0:80.39
tasyaivaṁ bhavati |
He thinks thus:
sbh2.0:80.40
kiṁ sataḥ samvidyamānānpāpakānakuśalāndharmān na pratisaṁvedayāmyāhosvidasataḥ |
‘Are there evil unwholesome dharmas present that I do not perceive, or are there none?
sbh2.0:80.41
asaṁvidyamānānyanva nnva haṁ parimīmānse māṁsaye yaṁ |
If non-existent — let me investigate.’
sbh2.0:80.42
sa mīmānsā māṁsā manaskāramadhipatiṁ kṛtvā prahīṇāprahīṇatāṁ mīmānsa māṁsa te samyagevopanidhyāpayati |
Making investigative attention (mīmāṁsā-manaskāra) predominant, he investigates rightly whether they are abandoned or not.
sbh2.0:80.43
tadbahulavihārī ca spṛśati cittasyaikāgratāṁ yena ca nirabhimāno bhavati |
And by frequent dwelling there he touches one-pointedness of mind, by which he becomes free of overestimation.
sbh2.0:80.44
paryavasthānamātrakācya cca cittaṁ vimuktaṁ, na tu sarveṇa sarvaṁmanuśayebhyaḥ |
‘From mere obsession my mind is liberated, but not completely from all latent tendencies;
sbh2.0:80.45
tatpratipakṣāśca me samādhipramukhāḥ kuśalā dharmā ś ca pratilabdhā, bhāvitā, na tvanuśayaprātipākṣikā iti yathābhūtaṁ prajānāti |
and the concentration-headed wholesome dharmas antidotal to those obsessions have been acquired and cultivated, but [the dharmas] antidotal to the latent tendencies have not.’ Thus he knows as it actually is.
sbh2.0:80.46
ayamasyocyate mīmānsā māṁsā samādhiḥ |
This is called the investigation-concentration.
sbh2.0:80.47
sa taṁ caturvidhaṁ samādhimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā paryavasthāneṣu dūrīkṛteṣu |
Making the fourfold concentration predominant, when the obsessions are distanced,
sbh2.0:80.48
sarveṇa sarvamanuśayasamudghātāya pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ, tatprātipākṣikāṇāñca kuśalānāṁ |
for the complete eradication of the latent tendencies of evil unwholesome dharmas, and for the accomplishment of the wholesome
sbh2.0:80.49
dharmāṇāṁ samudāgamāya c chandaṁ janayati |
dharmas antidotal to them, he generates desire;
sbh2.0:80.50
vyāyacchata iti vistareṇa caturbhiḥ samyakprahāṇaiḥ prayujyate |
he exerts himself, and so on — in detail he applies the four right exertions.
sbh2.0:80.51
tathā prayujyamānasya tathabhūtasyāṣṭau prahāṇasaṁskārā bhavanti |
And thus for him so applied, eight formations of abandonment (prahāṇa-saṁskāra) arise,
sbh2.0:80.52
ye a syānuśayasamudghātāya ca pravarttante |
which serve for his eradication of latent tendency
sbh2.0:80.53
samādhiparipūraye ca tadyathā- chandaḥ kadācitsamādhiṁ paripūrayiṣyāmi |
and for the fullness of concentration. Namely: desire — ‘when shall I fulfill concentration
sbh2.0:80.54
anuśayāṁśca prahāsyāmi |
and abandon the latent tendencies
sbh2.0:80.55
pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ vyāyāmo yāvatpratipakṣabhāvanāyāmavinyastaprayogayā, śraddhāyāmavinyastayogasya viharataḥ |
of evil unwholesome dharmas?’ Exertion: on up to his application not being laid down in cultivation of the antidote. Faith: for one who dwells with application not laid down,
sbh2.0:80.56
uttare a dhigame śradddhānatā |
trust in further attainment,
sbh2.0:80.57
abhisaṁpratyayaḥ |
conviction.
sbh2.0:80.58
tatra praśrabdhiḥ |
Pliancy:
sbh2.0:80.59
yacchraddhāpramādapūrvaṁgamaṁ prāmodyaṁ, prītiḥ, prītamanasaścānupūrvā pāpakākuśalā dharmapakṣasya dauṣṭhulyasya pratipraśrabdhiḥ |
the gladness preceded by faith and heedfulness, the joy, and, for one whose mind is joyful, the gradual tranquillization of the encumbrance on the side of evil unwholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:80.60
tatra smṛtiryā navākārā navākārāyāścittasthiteḥ śamathapakṣyāyāḥ saṁgrāhikā |
Mindfulness: of nine modes, the upholder of nine-mode mental abiding (navākārā cittasthiti) on the śamatha side.
sbh2.0:80.61
chaṁdasaṁprajanye |
Full awareness (saṁprajanya):
sbh2.0:80.62
yā vipaśyanāpakṣyā prajñā |
the wisdom that is on the vipaśyanā side.
sbh2.0:80.63
tatra cetanāyāścittābhisaṁskāraḥ |
Volition: the mental conditioning (cittābhisaṁskāra) —
sbh2.0:80.64
prahīṇāprahīṇato mīmānsa māṁsa mānasya yaścittābhisaṁskāraḥ śamathavipaśyanānukūlaḥ kāyakarma vākkarma samutthāpayati |
for him investigating what is abandoned and what is not, the mental conditioning, favorable to śamatha-vipaśyanā, sets up bodily and verbal action.
sbh2.0:80.65
tatropekṣayā atītānāgatapratyutpanneṣu pāpakākuśalādharmāsthānīyeṣu carataḥ cittābhisaṁkleśaścittaṁgamatā |
Equanimity: for one moving among past, future, and present dharmas that serve as bases for evil unwholesome [dharmas], the mind’s [Conjectural: non-defilement and evenness].
Printed cittābhisaṁkleśaś cittaṁgamatā; read cittānabhisaṁkleśaś cittasamatā (Xuanzang 心無染污、心平等性).
sbh2.0:80.66
ābhyāṁ dvābhyāṁ kāraṇābhyāṁ prahīṇatāmanuśayānāṁ paricchinatti jānāti |
By these two causes he ascertains the abandonment of latent tendencies and knows it —
sbh2.0:80.67
yaduta viṣayaviparokṣayā cetanayā viṣayaviparokṣayā cā co pekṣayā |
namely, by volition with its object out of sight and by equanimity with its object [Conjectural: in sight].
Printed viparokṣayā twice; read aviparokṣayā with upekṣayā (Xuanzang 境界不現見思 … 境界現見捨).
sbh2.0:80.68
ime a ṣṭau prahāṇasaṁskārā bhavanti |
These eight are the formations of abandonment.
sbh2.0:80.69
te caiteṣṭau sa caiṣo ’ṣṭa prahāṇasaṁskārayogo bhavatyanuśayasamudghātāya |
And this eightfold yoga of formations of abandonment is for the eradication of latent tendencies.
sbh2.0:80.70
tatra chanda cchanda śca, eta ṣa eva yo vyāyāmaḥ idaṁ vīryaṁ, yāśraddhā sā śuddhā, yā ca praśrabdhiryā ca smṛtiryacca saṁprajanyaṁ |
Of these, the desire is [that] desire; what is striving, this is [that] energy; the faith is [that] faith; and pliancy, mindfulness, full awareness,
sbh2.0:80.71
yā ca cetanā, yā copekṣā |
volition, and equanimity —
sbh2.0:80.72
aya idaṁ mupādāya |
[Conjectural: this is the means].
Printed ayam upādāya (Shukla queries idam); read ayam upāyaḥ (Xuanzang 即彼方便; the four yogas of 59.70).
sbh2.0:80.73
tadidaṁ sarvamabhisamasya ye ca pūrvakāśchandasamādhayaḥ |
Summing all this up: the previously stated concentrations of desire [etc.]
sbh2.0:80.74
ye ca ime prahāṇasaṁskārāḥ prahīṇeṣvanuśayeṣu, parikṣipte samādhau, chandasamādhiprahāṇasaṁskārasamanvāgata ṛddhipāda ityucyante te |
and these formations of abandonment — when the latent tendencies are abandoned and concentration is complete — are all called ‘the base of psychic power (ṛddhi-pāda) endowed with desire-concentration and formations of abandonment’;
sbh2.0:80.75
vīryacittamīmānsā māṁsā samādhiprahāṇasaṁskārasamanvāgata ṛddhipāda ityucyate |
and ‘the base of psychic power endowed with energy-, mind-, or investigation-concentration and formations of abandonment’.
sbh2.0:80.76
kena kāraṇena ṛddhipāda ityucte |
For what reason is it called ‘base of psychic power’?
sbh2.0:80.77
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:80.78
tadyathā |
namely,
sbh2.0:80.79
yasya pādaḥ samvidyate so a bhikramapratikrama ma parākramasamartho bhavati |
one whose foot is present is able to step forward and back, to stride.
sbh2.0:80.80
evameva yasyaite dharmāḥ saṁvidyante |
Just so, one for whom these dharmas are present,
sbh2.0:80.81
eṣa ca samādhiḥ saṁvidyayate |
and for whom this concentration is present,
sbh2.0:80.82
paripūrṇaḥ |
complete —
sbh2.0:80.83
sa evaṁ pariśuddhe citte, paryavadāte, anaṁgaṇe, vigatopakleśe, ṛjubhūte karmaṇyasthite, āniṁjyaprāpte, abhikramapratikramasamartho bhavati |
he, with mind thus purified, cleansed, unblemished, free of secondary defilement, made straight, made workable, established, having attained imperturbability, is able to step forward and back
sbh2.0:80.84
lokottarāṇāṁ dharmāṇāṁ prāptaye, sparśanāyai |
for the attainment and touching of supramundane dharmas.
sbh2.0:80.85
eṣā hi parā ṛddhiḥ, parā samṛddhiḥ |
For this is the supreme psychic-power, the supreme prosperity —
sbh2.0:80.86
yaduta lokottarā dharmāstenocyante te ṛddhipāda iti |
namely, the supramundane dharmas. Therefore they are called bases of psychic power.
sbh2.0:81.1
sa evaṁ samādhipratiṣṭhitaḥ |
Thus established in concentration
sbh2.0:81.2
samādhiṁ niśritya |
and depending on concentration,
sbh2.0:81.3
adhi cittaṁ śikṣā yā madhiprajñaṁ śikṣāyāṁ yogaṁ karoti |
he exerts himself in the training in higher mind and in the training in higher wisdom.
sbh2.0:81.4
tatrāsya yogaṁ kurvataḥ |
While he is so exerting himself,
sbh2.0:81.5
pareṣāṁ cādhigame śāstuḥ śrāvakāṇāṁ ca yo a bhisaṁpratyayaḥ |
with respect to others’ attainment — that of the Teacher and the disciples — the trust,
sbh2.0:81.6
prasādaḥ, śraddhānatā |
serenity, and faith (śraddhā),
sbh2.0:81.7
samāpattyarthena śraddhendriyamityucyate |
in the sense of [Conjectural: predominance], is called the faculty of faith (śraddhendriya).
Printed samāpattyarthena; read ādhipatyārthena (81.8; Xuanzang 增上義).
sbh2.0:81.8
kutra punarasyādhipatyaṁ |
And in what does its predominance consist?
sbh2.0:81.9
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:81.10
lokottaradharmotpattipramukhānāṁ vīryasmṛtisamādhiprajñānāmutpattaye ādhipatyaṁ |
in predominance for the arising of energy, mindfulness, concentration, and wisdom that lead to the arising of supramundane dharma.
sbh2.0:81.11
ye a pi te vīryādayaḥ teṣāmapi lokottaradharmotpattaye ādhipatyaṁ |
Those too, energy and so on, have predominance for the arising of supramundane dharma,
sbh2.0:81.12
yāvat pratipattaye ādhipatyaṁ |
and predominance for the attainment [of the next],
sbh2.0:81.13
yāvat prajñayā lokottaradharmotpattaye |
up to wisdom, [which has] for the arising of supramundane dharma
sbh2.0:81.14
ādhiopatyaṁ |
predominance.
sbh2.0:81.15
tainaitāni śraddhādīni paṁcendriyāṇi bhavanti |
These are the five faculties (paṁcendriya) beginning with faith.
sbh2.0:81.16
yā punaḥ pūrveṇāparaṁ viśeṣādhigamaṁ sajānataḥ saṁjānataḥ |
And when, recognizing the attainment of distinction of the later over the earlier,
sbh2.0:81.17
tadanusāreṇa taduttaralokottaradharmādhigamāyābhisaṁpratyayaḥ, prasādaḥ, śraddadhānatā |
he accordingly has trust, serenity, and faith toward the attainment of the still higher supramundane dharma —
sbh2.0:81.18
sā anavamṛdyanārthena śraddhābalamityucyate |
that, in the sense of being uncrushable, is called the power of faith (śraddhā-bala).
sbh2.0:81.19
kena punarna śakyate |
By what cannot it
sbh2.0:81.20
avamṛdituṁ |
be crushed?
sbh2.0:81.21
asaṁhāyā ryā sā śraddhā devena vā, māreṇa vā, brahmaṇā vā |
That faith cannot be taken away (asaṁhāryā) — by a deva, a māra, a brahma,
sbh2.0:81.22
kenacidvā punarloke, sahadharmeṇa kleśaparyavasthānena vā tena sā anavamṛdyetyucyate |
or by anyone else in the world legitimately, nor by an obsession of defilement; therefore it is called uncrushable.
sbh2.0:81.23
tatpramukhāstatpūrvaṁgamā ye vīryādayastepibalānītyucyante |
Headed and led by it, those — energy and so on — are also called powers.
sbh2.0:81.24
taiḥ sa balairbalavān sarvaṁ mārabalaṁ vijitya prayujyate |
Endowed with those powers, the mighty one overcomes all the māra’s armies and applies himself
sbh2.0:81.25
āsravāṇāṁ kṣayāya |
to the destruction of the fluxes.
sbh2.0:81.26
tasmādbalānītyucyante |
Therefore they are called powers.
sbh2.0:81.27
tatra yaśca yacca śraddhendriyaṁ, yacca śraddhābalaṁ caturṣvetadavetya prasādeṣu draṣṭavyaṁ |
Now, the faculty of faith and the power of faith are to be seen in the four serenities of clear knowing (avetya-prasāda).
sbh2.0:81.28
tatkasya hetoḥ |
Why so?
sbh2.0:81.29
yo ’sau samyaktvanyāmāvakrāntasyāvetya prasādaḥ |
Because the serenity of clear knowing of one who has entered the fixed course of rightness (samyaktvanyāma)
sbh2.0:81.30
sa taddhetukastatpratyastannidānaḥ |
has that [faith-faculty/power] as its cause, condition, and source.
sbh2.0:81.31
tasmāddhetuphalasambandhena tasyāstadadhipatiphalamiti kṛtvā |
Therefore, by cause-effect connection, that [serenity] is its predominant-effect — so it is said.
sbh2.0:81.32
tatra draṣṭavyamityuktaṁ bhagavatā |
‘There it is to be seen,’ said the Blessed One —
sbh2.0:81.33
na tu taccharīratāṁ tallakṣaṇatāṁ |
but not that it is its very body or its very characteristic.
sbh2.0:81.34
tatra vīryendriyaṁ caturṣu samyakprahāṇeṣu draṣṭavyaṁ |
The faculty of energy is to be seen in the four right exertions.
sbh2.0:81.35
tatkasya hetoḥ yāni tāni katamāni |
Which right exertions?
sbh2.0:81.36
samyakprahāṇāni yāni darśanaprahātavyakleśaprahāṇāya prāyogikāṇi samyakprahāṇāni, tānyatra samyakprahāṇānyabhipretāni tāni hyatyantatāyai pāpakānāmakuśalānāṁ dharmāṇāṁ prahāṇāya samvarttante |
The right exertions which are preparatory for the abandonment of the defilements abandonable by seeing (darśana-prahātavya-kleśa) — those right exertions are here intended; for they lead to the abandonment, even unto its absolute end, of evil unwholesome dharmas.
sbh2.0:82.1
tatra smṛtīndriyaṁ caturṣu smṛtyupasthāneṣu draṣṭavyamitīmāni catvāri smṛtyupasthānānyaviśeṣaviśeṣa viparyāsaprahāṇāya samvarttante |
The faculty of mindfulness is to be seen in the four foundations of mindfulness — these four foundations of mindfulness lead to the abandonment of all perversions without distinction.
Printed aviśeṣaviśeṣa-, the second word repeated.
sbh2.0:82.2
tatra samādhīndriyaṁ caturṣu sthā dhyāneṣu draṣṭavyaṁ |
The faculty of concentration is to be seen in the four dhyānas,
sbh2.0:82.3
yāni dhyānānyagāmitāyāṁ prāyogikāni ṇi tatra prajñendriyaṁ caturdhvāryasatyeṣu draṣṭavyamiti |
the dhyānas which are preparatory for [Conjectural: non-returning]; and the faculty of wisdom is to be seen in the four noble truths —
Printed agāmitāyāṁ; read anāgāmitāyāṁ (Xuanzang 證不還果).
sbh2.0:82.4
yatsatyajñānaṁ caturdhṇāmāryasatyānāmabhisamāya samvarttante te |
in the truth-knowledge which leads to the realization of the four noble truths.
sbh2.0:82.5
śrāmaṇyaphalaprāptate, pa ya pendriyāṇi |
The five faculties are for the attainment of the fruit of asceticism (śrāmaṇya-phala).
sbh2.0:82.6
evaṁ balāni veditavyāni |
Likewise the [five] powers should be understood.
sbh2.0:82.7
sa eṣāmindriyāṇāme teṣāṁ ca balānāmāsevanānvayādbhāvanānvayādabahulīkārānvayānnirvedhabhāgīyāni kuśalamūlānyutpādayati |
From his resort to, cultivation of, and making-much-of those faculties and powers, he produces the wholesome roots conducive to penetration (nirvedhabhāgīya).
sbh2.0:82.8
mṛdumadhyādhimātrāṇi |
[They are of three grades:] mild, middling, and strong.
sbh2.0:82.9
tadyathā ūṣmagatāni |
Namely: warmth (ūṣmagata),
sbh2.0:82.10
mūrdhagatāni |
summits (mūrdhan),
sbh2.0:82.11
mūrdhānaḥ satyānulomāḥ kṣāntayaḥ laukikāna da gradharmāttadyathā |
patiences in conformity with truth, and supreme worldly dharmas. Now,
sbh2.0:82.12
kaścideva puruṣaḥ agninā agnikāyaṁ karttukāmaḥ |
just as some person wishing to make a fire-mass with fire,
sbh2.0:82.13
agninārthī adharāraṇyāmuttarāraṇiṁ pratiṣṭhāpyāsa - nnutsahate, ghaṭate, vyāyacchate |
needing fire, places the upper stick on the lower fire-stick, makes effort, exerts himself, strives —
sbh2.0:82.14
tathotsahato, ghaṭato, vyāyacchataśca |
and thus, making effort, exerting, striving,
sbh2.0:82.15
tatprathamato ’dharāraṇyāmūṣmā jāyate |
first warmth is born in the lower stick;
sbh2.0:82.16
saiva coṣmā |
that same warmth,
sbh2.0:82.17
abhivardhamānā ūrdhvamāgacchati |
increasing, rises upward;
sbh2.0:82.18
bhūyasyā mātrayā abhivarddhamānā |
increasing further in greater measure,
sbh2.0:82.19
nirarcciṣamagniṁ pātayatyagnipatanasamanantarameva cārcirjāyate |
it brings down a flameless fire; and immediately upon the falling of the fire, a flame is born.
sbh2.0:82.20
yathā arcciṣā utpannayā nena jātayā tena saṁjātayā tena agnikāyaṁ karoti |
As by the flame arisen, born, come to be, he makes the fire-mass —
sbh2.0:82.21
yathā abhimanthana vyāyāma evaṁ pañcānāmindriyāṇāmāsevanā draṣṭavyā |
so by the effort of the rubbing-stick, the resort to the five faculties is to be seen.
sbh2.0:82.22
yathā adharaṇyā tatprathamata eva ūṣmagataṁ bhavati |
As in the lower stick first there is warmth,
sbh2.0:82.23
evamūṣmagatāni draṁṣṭavyāni |
so the warmths [are to be seen].
sbh2.0:82.24
pūrvaṁgamāni |
They are forerunners,
sbh2.0:82.25
nimittabhūtāni |
becoming signs
sbh2.0:82.26
agnisthānīyānāmanāsravāṇāṁ dharmāṇāṁ kleśaparidāhakānāmutpattaye |
for the arising of the fire-like fluxless dharmas that burn the defilements.
sbh2.0:82.27
yathā tasyaivoṣmaṇa ūrdhva mā gamanamevaṁ mūrdhānodraṣṭavyāḥ |
As that warmth rises upward, so the summits are to be seen.
sbh2.0:82.28
yathā dhūmaprādurbhāva evaṁ satyānulomāḥ kṣāntayo draṣṭavyāḥ |
As the appearance of smoke, so the truth-conformity patiences are to be seen.
sbh2.0:83.1
yathāgneḥ patanaṁ nirarcciṣa evaṁ laukikā agradharmā draṣṭavyāḥ |
As the falling of a flameless fire, so the supreme worldly dharmas are to be seen.
sbh2.0:83.2
yathā tadanantaramarcciṣaḥ |
As immediately after, the flame’s
sbh2.0:83.3
utpāda evaṁ lokottarā anāsravā dharmā draṣṭavyā |
arising — so the supramundane, fluxless dharmas are to be seen,
sbh2.0:83.4
ye laukikā agradharmasagṛhītānāṁ pañcānāmindriyāṇāṁ samanantaramutpadyante |
which arise immediately after the five faculties subsumed under supreme worldly dharma.
sbh2.0:83.5
te punaḥ katame āha |
Which are they? Answer:
sbh2.0:83.6
saptabodhyaṁgāni |
the seven awakening-factors (saptabodhyaṁga).
sbh2.0:83.7
yo ’sau yathābhūtāvabodhaḥ |
That awakening as it actually is
sbh2.0:83.8
samyaktvanyāmāvakrāntasya pudgalasyaitānyaṁgāni |
of the person who has entered the fixed course of rightness (samyaktvanyāma) — these are its factors.
sbh2.0:83.9
sa hi yathābhūtāvabodhaḥ |
For that awakening as it actually is
sbh2.0:83.10
saptāṁgaparigṛhītaḥ |
is taken up by seven factors:
sbh2.0:83.11
tribhiḥ śamathapakṣyaiḥ tribhirvipaśyanāpakṣyairekenobhayapakṣyeṇa |
three on the śamatha side, three on the vipaśyanā side, and one belonging to both.
sbh2.0:83.12
tasmādbodhyaṁgānītyucyante |
Therefore they are called awakening-factors.
sbh2.0:83.13
tatra yaśca dharmavinayaḥ |
[Conjectural: Dharma-discrimination],
sbh2.0:83.14
yacca vīryaṁ, yā ca prītiritīmāni trīṇi vipaśyanāpakṣyāṇi |
energy (vīrya), and joy (prīti) — these three are on the vipaśyanā side.
sbh2.0:83.15
tatra yā ca praśrabdhiryaśca samādhiryā copekṣā itīmāni trīṇi śamathapakṣyāṇi |
Pliancy (praśrabdhi), concentration (samādhi), and equanimity (upekṣā) — these three are on the śamatha side.
sbh2.0:83.16
smṛtirabhayapakṣyā s sarvatragetyucyate |
Mindfulness (smṛti), belonging to both, is called all-pervading.
sbh2.0:83.17
sa tasmin samaye tatprathamato bodhyaṁgalābhācchaikṣo bhavati |
At that moment, by the first attainment of the awakening-factors, he becomes a trainee (śaikṣa),
sbh2.0:83.18
prātipadaḥ |
one progressing [on the path].
sbh2.0:83.19
darśanaprahātavyāścāsya kleśāḥ prahīṇā bhavanti |
His defilements to be abandoned by seeing (darśana-prahātavya) are abandoned.
sbh2.0:83.20
bhāvanāprahātavyāścāvaśiṣṭāḥ |
Those abandonable by cultivation (bhāvanā-prahātavya) remain.
sbh2.0:83.21
sa teṣāṁ prahāṇāya triskandhamāryāṣṭāṁgaṁ mārgaṁ bhāvayati | tatra yā ca samyagdṛṣṭiryaśca samyaksaṁkalpaḥ, yaśca samyagvyāyāmaḥ | ayaṁ prajñāskandhaḥ | tatra ye samyakkarmāntājīvāḥ |
For their abandonment he cultivates the three-aggregate noble eightfold path. Of these, right view, right intention, and right effort make up the wisdom-aggregate (prajñāskandha); [Lacuna] right action and right livelihood
Printed samyakkarmāntājīvāḥ; right speech is lost (86.38; Xuanzang 正語、正業、正命).
sbh2.0:83.22
ayaṁ śīlaskandhaḥ |
make up the virtue-aggregate (śīla-skandha).
sbh2.0:83.23
tatra yā ca samyaksmṛtiḥ, yaśca samyaksamādhirayaṁ samādhiskandhaḥ |
His right mindfulness and right concentration make up the concentration-aggregate (samādhi-skandha).
sbh2.0:83.24
kena kāraṇenāryāṣṭāṁgo mārga ityucyate |
For what reason is it called the noble eightfold path?
sbh2.0:83.25
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:83.26
āryasya śaikṣasya dṛṣṭapadasyāyaṁ mārga iyaṁ pratipadaṣṭābhiraṁgaiḥ saṁgṛhītā ḥ |
for the noble trainee who has seen the track, this is the path, this is the practice, comprised in eight factors.
sbh2.0:84.1
apariśeṣaḥ |
Without omission,
sbh2.0:84.2
sarvakleśaprahāṇāya vimuktisākṣātkriyāyai tenocyate āryāṣṭāṁgo mārgaḥ |
for the abandonment of all defilements and for the realization of liberation — therefore it is called the noble eightfold path.
sbh2.0:84.3
tatra yaśca bodhyaṁgakāle tattvāvabodhaḥ |
At the time of [attaining] the awakening-factors, realization of reality
sbh2.0:85.1
pratilabdhaḥ, pratilabhya ca yattasyaiva prajñayā vyavasthānaṁ karoti |
is acquired; and having acquired it, by that very wisdom one makes a delineation
sbh2.0:86.1
yathāvigatasyāvabodhasya, tadubhayamekatyamabhisaṁkṣipya samyagdṛṣṭirityucyate |
of that realization just as it has been attained — these two, summed up, are called right view.
sbh2.0:86.2
tāṁ samyagdṛṣṭimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā |
Making that right view predominant,
sbh2.0:86.3
yannaiṣkramyasaṁkalpaṁ saṁkalpayatyavyāpādasaṁkalpamavihinsā hiṁsā saṁkalpamayamucyate samyaksaṁkalpaḥ |
what he wills as the intention of renunciation, the intention of non-malevolence, the intention of non-cruelty — this is called right intention.
sbh2.0:86.4
sa cettāvadvitarkeṣu cittaṁ krāmati |
If his mind moves in applied thoughts (vitarka),
sbh2.0:86.5
sa evaṁ rūpādvitarkādvitarkayati |
he thinks such applied thoughts [accordingly].
sbh2.0:86.6
sa cetpunaḥ kathāyāṁ cittaṁ krāmati |
If his mind moves to speech,
sbh2.0:86.7
samyagdṛṣṭimadhipatiṁ kṛtvā tene na kuśalātsaṁkalpāṁ lasaṁkalpāṁ dharmyāṁ kathāṁ kathayati |
making right view predominant, he speaks the dharma-talk born of that wholesome intention.
sbh2.0:86.8
sāsya bhavati samyagvāk |
That becomes for him right speech.
sbh2.0:86.9
sa ceccīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārairatīthī rthī bhavati |
If he needs robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [or other] requisites,
sbh2.0:86.10
tatparyeṣaṇāmvāpadyate |
he undertakes their seeking.
sbh2.0:86.11
so ’bhikramaḥ |
in going forward
sbh2.0:86.12
pratikrame saṁprajānamvi dvi hārī bhavatyālokitavyavalokite |
and returning, he dwells with full awareness; in looking ahead and looking around,
sbh2.0:86.13
tasmiṁjita saṁmiṁjita prasārite |
in bending and stretching,
sbh2.0:86.14
sāṁghaṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇe, aśitapītakhāditāsvādite |
in wearing the saṅghāṭi and robes, and carrying the bowl; in eating, drinking, chewing, tasting;
sbh2.0:86.15
vihāragato vā punaḥ paryeṣiteṣu cīvarādiṣu gate, sthite, niṣaṇṇe |
or, back in the dwelling, with respect to the robes etc. already sought — in walking, standing, sitting,
sbh2.0:86.16
yāvannidrāklama prati vinodane saṁprajānadvihārī bhavati |
up to the dispelling of sleep-fatigue — he dwells with full awareness.
sbh2.0:86.17
ayamasyocyate samyakkarmāntaḥ |
This for him is called right action.
sbh2.0:86.18
sa taccīvaraṁ yāvadbhaiṣajyapariṣkāraṁ dharmeṇa paryeṣate |
He seeks robes [and] up to medicine and requisites by means of the dharma.
sbh2.0:86.19
yāvanmithyā .......... dharmavivarjitaḥ so ’sya bhavati |
up to ‘free of the dharma of wrong [Damaged]’ — that for him is
After mithyā syllables are illegible (Shukla’s note).
sbh2.0:86.20
samyagājīvaḥ |
right livelihood.
sbh2.0:86.21
ye punarviratisaṁgṛhītāḥ |
Those subsumed under the abstinences (virati),
sbh2.0:86.22
samyakkarmāntājīvāḥ |
[Lacuna] right action and right livelihood —
Printed samyakkarmāntājīvāḥ; right speech is lost (83.21; Xuanzang 正語、業、命).
sbh2.0:86.23
te anena pūrvameva manaskāralābhādbodhyaṁgaireva saha labdhā bhavanti |
these have been obtained along with the awakening-factors by virtue of the prior attainment of attention.
sbh2.0:86.24
yopyāpakāntāni śīlānyucyante |
And [they are] called ‘virtues [Conjectural: dear to the noble ones]’.
Printed āpakānta (86.24, 86.29, 86.30, 86.37); read āryakānta (86.25; Xuanzang 聖所愛戒).
sbh2.0:86.25
kena kāraṇena dīrgha kālaṁ hyetadāryāṇāṁ satāṁ samyaggatānāmiṣṭaṁ kāntaṁ priyaṁ mana āpaṁ kaccidahaṁ tadvāgduścaritasya, kāyaduścaritasya, mithyājīvasyākaraṇaṁ |
For what reason? For a long time those virtues had been esteemed, dear, beloved, and agreeable to the noble, the good, the rightly-gone: ‘would that I might attain the restraint of non-doing of misconduct of speech, body, and wrong livelihood —
sbh2.0:86.26
samvaraṁ pratilabheyaṁ |
the restraint.’
sbh2.0:86.27
yadasya dīrgharātramiṣṭaṁ |
What had long been for him desired,
sbh2.0:86.28
kāntaṁ priyaṁ mana āpaṁ tadanena tasminsamaye pratilabdhaṁ bhavati |
dear, beloved, agreeable — that is now attained by him at that moment.
sbh2.0:86.29
tasmādāpakāntamityucyate |
Therefore they are called ‘[Conjectural: dear to the noble ones]’.
sbh2.0:86.30
tathā hi sa labdheṣvāpakānteṣu śīleṣu, na saṁprajā nā no mṛṣāṁ vācaṁ bhāṣate |
For when he has attained the virtues [Conjectural: dear to the noble ones], he does not knowingly speak false speech,
sbh2.0:86.31
na saṁvidhya prāṇinaṁ |
does not deliberately
sbh2.0:86.32
jīvitād vyaparopayati |
deprive a living being of life,
sbh2.0:86.33
nādattamādatte |
does not take what is not given,
sbh2.0:86.34
na |
does not
sbh2.0:86.35
kāmeṣu mithyā carati |
engage in wrong conduct in sense-pleasures,
sbh2.0:86.36
na cādharmeṇa cīvarādīni paryeṣate |
and does not seek robes and so on by non-dharma.
sbh2.0:86.37
iti tānyāpakāntāni śīlānyadhipatiṁ kṛtvā mārgabhāvanākāle yāvatpravartate |
Thus, making those virtues [Conjectural: dear to the noble ones] predominant while cultivating the path,
sbh2.0:86.38
yacca kāyakarma yaścājīvaḥ tepi samyagvākkarmāntājīvā ityucyante |
the bodily action and the livelihood — these too are called right speech, action, and livelihood.
sbh2.0:86.39
tasya samyagadṛṣṭisamyaksaṁkalpa ḥ |
For him who, with right view and right intention,
sbh2.0:86.40
vākkarmāntājīvasanniśrayeṇa bhāvanāprayuktasya |
speech, action, and livelihood as his support, is applied to cultivation,
sbh2.0:86.41
yacchando yaśchando , vīryaṁ, vyāyāmo, niṣkramaḥ, parākramasthāma āraṁbhaḥ |
whatever desire, energy, effort, going forth, advance, stamina, undertaking,
sbh2.0:86.42
cetasaḥ saṁpragrahaḥ |
uplifting of the mind,
sbh2.0:86.43
sātatyamayamucyate |
and continuity — this is called
sbh2.0:86.44
samyagvyāyāmaśamathaḥ |
right effort.
Printed samyagvyāyāmaśamathaḥ; śamathaḥ is stray (86.46) and not rendered.
sbh2.0:86.45
yaccatvāri smṛtyupasthānānyadhipatiṁ kṛtvā aviparyāyasaṁgṛhītā smṛtiḥ navākārā navākāracittasthitisaṁgrāhikā | iyamucyate samyaksmṛtiḥ | samyaksamādhiśca | tadetatsarvamabhisamasya āryāṣṭāṁgo mārgaścārakaraṇīye ca vihārakaraṇīye cāvasthitaḥ | tatra samyagvākkarmāntājīvāścārakaraṇīye | vihārakaraṇīyaṁ punardvividhaṁ |
Making the four foundations of mindfulness predominant, the mindfulness subsumed under non-perversion, of nine modes, the upholder of the nine-mode mental abiding — this is called right mindfulness and right concentration. Summing all this up, the noble eightfold path stands in the task of conduct and the task of dwelling. Of these, right speech, right action, and right livelihood are in the task of conduct; the task of dwelling is again twofold:
sbh2.0:86.46
śamatho vipaśyanā ca |
śamatha and vipaśyanā.
sbh2.0:86.47
tatra yā samyagdṛṣṭiḥ |
Right view,
sbh2.0:86.48
yaśca samyaksaṁkalpaḥ |
right intention,
sbh2.0:86.49
yaśca samyagvyāyāma iyaṁ vipaśyanā |
and right effort are vipaśyanā.
sbh2.0:86.50
tatra yā ca samyaksamṛtiryaśca samyaksamādhisyaṁ śamathaḥ |
Right mindfulness and right concentration are śamatha.
sbh2.0:86.51
evaṁ pariśuddhān samyagvākkarmāntājīvānniśritya śamathavipaśyanāṁ bhāvayati |
Thus, depending on the purified right speech, action, and livelihood, he cultivates śamatha and vipaśyanā from time to time,
sbh2.0:86.52
kālena kālaṁ niravaśeṣasaṁyojanaprahāṇaṁ sākṣātkarotyagraphalamarhattvaṁ, prāpnoti |
realizes the abandonment of the fetters without remainder, and attains the supreme fruit, arhatship.
sbh2.0:86.53
prākarṣikaśca kañca bhāvanāmārgaḥ rgaṁ |
And the cultivation-path (bhāvanā-mārga) is protracted:
sbh2.0:86.54
kālāntarābhyāsena kleśān prajahāti |
it abandons defilements by practice over a long time.
sbh2.0:86.55
jñānamātrapratibaddhavastudarśanamārgaḥ jñānotpattimātreṇa kleśān prajahātyanena kāraṇena vākkarmāntājīvā bhāvanāmārge vyavasthāpitāḥ |
The path of seeing (darśana-mārga), a thing bound up with mere knowledge, abandons defilements by the mere arising of knowledge. For this reason speech, action, and livelihood are established in the cultivation-path.
sbh2.0:86.56
iti ya evameṣāmanayā ānupūrvyā saptatriṁśatāṁ bodhapakṣyāṇāṁ dharmāṇāmabhyāsaḥ, paricayaḥ |
Thus, this sequence of practice — familiarization with the thirty-seven dharmas on the side of awakening —
sbh2.0:86.57
iyamucyate bodhipakṣyā bhāvanā |
is called the cultivation of the [dharmas] on the side of awakening.
sbh2.0:86.58
tatra bhāvanāphalaṁ katamat |
Now, what is the fruit of cultivation?
sbh2.0:86.59
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:86.60
catvāri śrāmaṇyaphalāni |
the four fruits of asceticism —
sbh2.0:86.61
srota āpattiphalaṁ, sakṛdāgāmiphalaṁ |
the fruit of stream-entry, the fruit of once-returning,
sbh2.0:86.62
anāgāmiphalamagraphalamarhattvaṁ |
the fruit of non-returning, and the supreme fruit, arhatship.
sbh2.0:86.63
tatra katamacchrāmaṇyaṁ |
What is asceticism (śrāmaṇya)?
sbh2.0:86.64
katamatphalaṁ |
What is its fruit?
sbh2.0:86.65
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:86.66
mārgaḥ, kleśaprahāṇaṁ phalaṁ |
asceticism is the path; the abandonment of defilements is the fruit.
sbh2.0:86.67
api ca pūrvotpannasya mārgasya paścādutpanno mārgaḥ |
Moreover, the path arisen later is the fruit of the path arisen earlier —
sbh2.0:86.68
phalaṁ, madhyo, viśiṣṭo vā, punaḥ |
middling or superior.
sbh2.0:86.69
tatra kena kāraṇena catvāri vyavasthāpitāni |
For what reason are four [fruits] established?
sbh2.0:86.70
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:86.71
caturvidhakleśaprahāṇapratipakṣatayā |
as antidotes for the abandonment of four kinds of defilement.
sbh2.0:86.72
tadyathā nirvastukānāṁ kleśānāmapāyagamanahetubhūtānāṁ prahāṇātpratipakṣotpādācca srota āpattiphalaṁ vyavasthāpitaṁ |
Namely: from the abandonment of the baseless defilements (nirvastuka) that cause going to the lower realms, and from the arising of antidote, the fruit of stream-entry is established —
sbh2.0:86.73
trayāṇāṁ tu saṁyojanānāṁ prahāṇādvyavasthāpitaṁ |
But [that] it is established from the abandonment of the three fetters [is because]
sbh2.0:86.74
bhagavatā triṣu pakṣeṣu, gṛhipakṣe, durākhyātadharmavinaya pakṣe ca, trayāṇāṁ saṁyojanānāṁ mārgotpattaye vivṛddhākaratvāt |
the Blessed One [said it] in respect of three positions — the householder’s, that of the ill-stated dharma-vinaya, [Lacuna] — since the three fetters are [Conjectural: obstructive] to the arising of the path.
Printed text omits the third position; Xuanzang has 善說法毘奈耶品 (86.80). Printed vivṛddhākaratvāt; read vibandhakaratvāt (86.83; Xuanzang 障礙聖道令不生).
sbh2.0:86.75
tatra gṛhipakṣe satkāyadṛṣṭiḥ |
In the householder, [the fetter is] identity-view (satkāya-dṛṣṭi),
sbh2.0:86.76
yayā yamādita eva na prayutyata ityādita u t trāsikā satkāyadṛṣṭiḥ |
by which from the very start he does not engage in the path — terror-bringing identity-view.
sbh2.0:86.77
durākhyātedharmavinaye śīlavrataparāmarśaḥ |
In the ill-stated dharma-vinaya, [the fetter is] clinging-to-virtue-and-vow (śīla-vrata-parāmarśa).
sbh2.0:86.78
uccalitasyāpi mithyāpratipādayati |
Even one who has set out, it makes him practice wrongly,
sbh2.0:86.79
yenāryamārgo notpadyate |
by which the noble path does not arise.
sbh2.0:86.80
svākhyāte dharmavinaye vicikitsātaścoccalitaśca bhavati |
In the well-stated dharma-vinaya, [the obstacle is] from doubt (vicikitsā); he has set out
sbh2.0:86.81
na ca mithyāpratipannaḥ |
and is not practising wrongly,
sbh2.0:86.82
api svābhyāsāttasya yāvad yathābhūtadarśanaṁ na bhavati |
but, even with his own training, as long as he has no seeing as it actually is
sbh2.0:86.83
jñeyavastuni tāvatkāṁkṣā vimatayo vibandhakarā bhavanti |
of the knowable thing, so long doubts and indecisions are obstructive
sbh2.0:86.84
mārgasyotpattaye |
to the arising of the path.
sbh2.0:86.85
anena tāvatkāraṇena srota āpattiphalavyavasthānaṁ |
Thus the delineation of the fruit of stream-entry.
sbh2.0:87.1
tasyāsya srota āpannasya paraṁ sapta bhavā avaśiṣṭā bhavanti |
For this stream-enterer, at most seven existences remain.
sbh2.0:87.2
sa cāsyajanma prabandhaḥ |
And this is the continuity of his birth.
sbh2.0:87.3
yadā janmaprābandhikānkleśānprajahāti |
When he abandons the defilements that bind to birth —
sbh2.0:87.4
devabhavasaṁgṛhītānmanuṣyasaṁgṛhītāṁśca |
those subsumed under deva-existence and human-existence —
sbh2.0:87.5
yeṣāṁ prahāṇātparamekaṁ devabhavamabhinirvarttayatyekaṁ manuṣyabhavaṁ |
by the abandonment of which he produces only one further deva-existence and one human-existence,
sbh2.0:87.6
tasminsamaye sakṛdāgāmiphalaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
at that moment the fruit of once-returning (sakṛd-āgāmin) is established.
sbh2.0:87.7
yadā tu devabhavameva kevalamabhinirvarttayati |
When, however, he produces only a deva-existence,
sbh2.0:87.8
iha pratyāgamajanmikaṁ kleśaṁ prahāya tadā anāgāmiphalaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
having abandoned the defilement that brings to a return-birth here — at that moment the fruit of non-returning (anāgāmin) is established.
sbh2.0:88.1
sarvabhavopapattisaṁvarttanīyakleśaprahāṇādagraphalamarhattvaphalaṁ vyavasthāpyate |
From the abandonment of all the defilements conducive to existence and rebirth, the supreme fruit, arhatship, is established.
sbh2.0:88.2
tatpunaḥ sakṛdāgāmiphalaṁ trayāṇāṁ saṁyojanānāṁ prahāṇādrāgadveṣamohānāṁ ca |
Moreover, the fruit of once-returning: from the abandonment of the three fetters and, of lust, hatred and delusion,
sbh2.0:88.3
tanutvād bhagavatā vyavasthāpitaṁ |
from their attenuation, it was established by the Blessed One.
sbh2.0:88.4
paṁcānāmavarabhāgīyānāṁ saṁyojanānāṁ prahāṇādanāgāmiphalaṁ |
From the abandonment of the five lower fetters (avarabhāgīya), the fruit of non-returning.
sbh2.0:88.5
paryādāya sarvakleśaprahāṇādarhattvaphala midamucyate bhāvanāphalaṁ |
From the complete abandonment of all defilements, the fruit of arhatship — this is called the fruit of cultivation.
sbh2.0:88.6
tatra ye rāgadveṣamohamānavitarkacariteṣu tāḥ pudgaleṣu lāḥ pūrvaṁ taiḥ tāvaccaritaviśodhane ālambane caritaṁ viśodhayitavyaṁ |
Now, in those persons of lust-, hatred-, delusion-, conceit-, or applied-thought conduct, the conduct should first be purified by means of a conduct-purifying object.
sbh2.0:88.7
tataḥ paścāccittasthitimadhigacchanti |
Afterwards they attain mental abiding.
sbh2.0:88.8
teṣāṁ pratiniyatameva tadālambanamavaśyaṁ taistenālambanena prayoktavyaṁ |
For them, that object is fixed; they must necessarily apply themselves to that object.
sbh2.0:88.9
samabhāgacaritasya tu yatra priyārohitā |
But for one of even-balanced conduct (sama-bhāga-carita), wherever there is delight,
sbh2.0:88.10
tatra tena prayoktavyaṁ kevalaṁ cittasthitaye |
with that he should apply himself, simply for mental abiding,
sbh2.0:88.11
na tu caritaviśuddhaye |
not for conduct-purification.
sbh2.0:88.12
yathā samabhāgacarita evaṁ mandarajasko veditavyaḥ |
As with the even-balanced, so with the one of slight dust (manda-rajaska) is to be understood.
sbh2.0:88.13
ayaṁ tveṣāṁ viśeṣo rāgādicaritaḥ prayujyamānaścireṇādhigantā bhavati |
Their distinction is this: one of lust-conduct etc., applying himself, becomes one who attains [mental abiding] only after a long time;
sbh2.0:88.14
samabhāgacarito nāticireṇa, mandarajaskastu āśu tvarita tvaritaṁ cittasthitamadhigacchati |
the even-balanced, not in too long a time; the one of slight dust, however, swiftly, very swiftly attains mental abiding.
sbh2.0:88.15
tatroktāni pūrvaṁ rāgacaritānāṁ pudgalānāṁ liṁgāni |
The marks of persons of lust-conduct etc. were stated before.
sbh2.0:88.16
samabhāgacaritasya pudgalasya mandarajaskasya ca katamāni liṁgāni |
What are the marks of the person of even-balanced conduct and of the one of slight dust?
sbh2.0:88.17
āha |
Answer:
sbh2.0:88.18
samabhāgacaritasya pudgalasya sarvāṇi tāni liṁgāni saṁvidyante |
in the person of even-balanced conduct, all those marks are present
sbh2.0:88.19
yāni rāgādicaritānāṁ, tāni rāgā dī ni tu nādhimātrāṇi, na pradhānāni |
which belong to persons of lust-conduct etc., but those [marks] of lust and the rest are not intense, not pre-eminent.
sbh2.0:88.20
tathā rāgādicaritānāṁ samaprāptāni bhavanti pratyayeṣu satsu na prajñāyante |
Thus they come to be on a par with those of the lust-conducted and the rest; when the conditions are present they are not distinguished.
sbh2.0:88.21
tatra mandarajaskasya pudgalasya liṁgāni |
Now, the marks of the person of slight dust:
sbh2.0:88.22
anāvṛto bhavatyādiśuddhasaṁbhārasaṁbhṛtaḥ |
he is unobstructed, originally pure, and equipped with the requisites.
sbh2.0:88.23
prasādabahulo medhāvī puṇyavān guṇānvitaśca bhavati |
He is full of serenity, intelligent, of merit, and endowed with virtues.
sbh2.0:88.24
tatra trīṇyāvaraṇāni |
There are three obstacles (āvaraṇa):
sbh2.0:88.25
karmāvaraṇaṁ |
the obstacle of action (karma-āvaraṇa),
sbh2.0:88.26
kleśāvaraṇaṁ |
the obstacle of defilement (kleśa-āvaraṇa),
sbh2.0:88.27
vipākāvaraṇaṁ |
and the obstacle of result (vipāka-āvaraṇa).
sbh2.0:88.28
tatra karmāvaraṇaṁ |
The obstacle of action consists in
sbh2.0:88.29
pañcānantaryāṇi karmāṇi |
the five actions of immediate retribution (ānantarya),
sbh2.0:88.30
yaccānyadapi kiṁcitkarmāṇi karma |
and whatever other action
sbh2.0:88.31
sāṁcetanīyaṁ |
is intentional,
sbh2.0:88.32
gurukarma, vipakvavipākaṁ |
heavy, which, when its result has ripened,
sbh2.0:88.33
mārgotpattaye |
for the arising of the path
sbh2.0:88.34
nibaddhakārakaṁ |
makes an obstruction.
sbh2.0:88.35
tatra kleśāvaraṇaṁ |
The obstacle of defilement consists in
sbh2.0:88.36
tīvrakleśatā |
intense-defilement-state
sbh2.0:88.37
āyatakleśatā ca |
and long-defilement-state,
sbh2.0:88.38
yā dṛṣṭe dharme caritaviśodhanenālambanaviśodhanena na śakyate viśodhayituṁ |
which in this very life cannot be purified by the conduct-purifying or the object-purifying [object].
sbh2.0:88.39
tatra vipākāvaraṇaṁ yatrāryamārgasya apravṛttiraprasāda upapattyāyatane |
The obstacle of result: a place of rebirth where the noble path does not operate, does not [Conjectural: grow];
Printed aprasāda, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 不生不長.
sbh2.0:88.40
tatra vā vipākamabhinirvarttayati |
there he brings forth a result;
sbh2.0:88.41
yatra vā āryamārgasya pravṛttiḥ |
Or [it occurs] where the noble path can function,
sbh2.0:88.42
tatropapatro jāto bhavatyeḍamūko hastasaṁbādhikaḥ apratibalo bhavati |
Born and present there, he is deaf-mute, [Conjectural: speaking by hand signs], incapable
sbh2.0:88.43
subhāvitadurbhāvitānāṁ dharmāṇāmarthamājñātuṁ |
of understanding the meaning of dharmas well- or ill-cultivated.
sbh2.0:88.44
tatrādiśuddhiḥ, śīlaṁ ca suviśuddhaṁ dṛṣṭiśca ṛjvī |
Here, original purity is virtue thoroughly pure and upright view.
sbh2.0:88.45
tatra śīlaṁ suviśuddhaṁ |
Virtue thoroughly pure
sbh2.0:88.46
daśabhiḥ kāraṇairveditavyam |
is to be understood by ten causes.
sbh2.0:88.47
tatra dṛṣṭi tṛptitā jātā śradadhāṁ ddhā saṁprayogāt |
[Conjectural: Uprightness of view] is born from conjunction with faith,
Printed dṛṣṭi tṛptitā jātā, letters illegible (Shukla’s note); read dṛṣṭyṛjutā jātā (88.44, 88.63; Xuanzang 正直見).
sbh2.0:88.48
adhimuktisaṁprayogāddhi tamāryā a śāṭhyatayā sucintita dharmārthasya niḥkāṁkṣanirvicikitsāprayoganiryāṇatayā yā dṛṣṭiḥ śraddhayā saṁprayuktā |
from conjunction with conviction, from [Conjectural: freedom from deceit and guile], and from practice that leads out, free of doubt and indecision, with the meaning of the dharma well-considered. The view that is conjoined with faith
Printed dhi tam āryā[a]śāṭhyatayā; read vigatamāyāśāṭhyatayā (88.53; Xuanzang 遠離誑諂).
sbh2.0:88.49
asmāddharma vinayādasaṁhāryādadhimuktyā ca saṁprayuktā buddhānāṁ buddhaśrāvakāṇāṁ ca |
is not carried away from this Dharma-Vinaya; and, conjoined with conviction, in the Buddhas’ and the Buddha-disciples’
sbh2.0:88.50
anityamanubhava na manityāni copapattyāyatanā ni |
[Conjectural: inconceivable power and inconceivable places of rebirth,]
Printed anityam anubhava[na]m anityāni; read acintyam anubhāvam acintyāni (Xuanzang 不可思議威德神力、不可思議生處差別).
sbh2.0:88.51
gaṁbhīrāṁ ca deśanāṁ, avyākṛtavastu cādhimucyate |
in the deep teaching and in the undeclared matters, he has conviction.
sbh2.0:88.52
no t trasati, na saṁtrāsamā padyate |
He is not alarmed, does not fall into alarm.
sbh2.0:88.53
vigatamāyāśāṭhyā ca yā dṛṣṭiḥ yayā ṛjuko bhavati |
Free of guile and pretense, the view by which he becomes straight,
sbh2.0:88.54
ṛjukajātīyaḥ |
of straight nature,
sbh2.0:88.55
yathānuśiṣṭaśca pratipadyate |
he practices as instructed.
sbh2.0:88.56
yathābhūtaṁ cātmānamāviṣkaroti |
He reveals himself as he actually is.
sbh2.0:88.57
dharmāṇāṁ vānityatāmārabhyaṁ, duḥkhatāṁ, śūnyatāmanātmatāmarthaḥ suvicintitobhavati |
Or, the meaning of dharmas with regard to impermanence, suffering, emptiness and selflessness is well-considered,
sbh2.0:88.58
sutulitaḥ sūpaparīkṣitaḥ |
well-weighed, well-examined;
sbh2.0:88.59
yaddhetorayaṁ niḥkāṅkṣo bhavati |
by which cause he is without doubt,
sbh2.0:88.60
nirvicikitsaḥ |
without indecision.
sbh2.0:88.61
dvedhā pathā gato viśeṣāya paraiti |
Gone past the forked road, he goes on to distinction.
sbh2.0:88.62
itīyaṁ caturākārā dṛṣṭiryathoddiṣṭā |
Thus this four-mode view as taught
sbh2.0:88.63
dṛṣṭiṛjutetyucyate |
is called the straightness of view.
sbh2.0:88.64
tatra saṁbhārasaṁbhṛtatāvistareṇa saṁbhāraḥ |
Being equipped with the requisites: the requisites in detail
sbh2.0:88.65
pūrvameva nirdiṣṭaḥ samāsataḥ |
were set out before; in brief,
sbh2.0:88.66
punaścarvidho bhavati |
they are again fourfold:
sbh2.0:88.67
puṇyasaṁbhāro, jñānasaṁbhāraḥ, pūrvako, dṛṣṭadhārmikaśca |
merit-requisite, knowledge-requisite, of the previous [life], and of the present life.
sbh2.0:88.68
tatra puṇyasabhāro yenāpyetarhi ānulomikāḥ pariṣkārāḥ prādurābhavanti |
The merit-requisite is that by which conforming requisites manifest,
sbh2.0:88.69
pradakṣiṇāḥ |
auspicious.
sbh2.0:88.70
kalyāṇamitrāṇi ca pratilabhate |
And he gains good friends.
sbh2.0:88.71
anantarāyaśca prayuktasya bhavati |
And he becomes without obstacle when applied.
sbh2.0:88.72
tatra jñānasaṁbhāraḥ |
The knowledge-requisite is
sbh2.0:88.73
yena medhāvī bhavati, pratibalaḥ |
that by which he becomes intelligent, capable
sbh2.0:88.74
subhāṣitadurbhāṣitānāṁ dharmāṇāmarthamājñātuṁ |
of understanding the meaning of dharmas well- or ill-stated.
sbh2.0:88.75
lābhī bhavati |
He becomes a recipient
sbh2.0:88.76
ānulobhikāyā dharmadeśanāyāḥ, arthadeśanāyāḥ, avavādānuśāsanyāḥ |
of conforming dharma-teaching, meaning-teaching, and admonition.
sbh2.0:88.77
tatra pūrvako yenaitahi rhi pa ri pakvānīndriyāṇi labhate |
The [requisite] of the previous [life] is that by which now the faculties become mature,
sbh2.0:88.78
pūrvakuśalamūlopacayāt |
by accumulation of prior wholesome roots.
sbh2.0:88.79
tatra dṛṣṭadhārmikastadyathā |

The death, the dying, of these and those beings
sbh2.0:94.11
tasmāt tasmāt sattvanikāyāḥ yayādyanmaraṇaṁ kālakriyā maraṇamāraḥ |
from this and that group of beings is the death-māra.
sbh2.0:94.12
yo 'pyakuśalapakṣaprayuktasya skandhakleśamṛtyusamatikramāya kāmadhātūpapanno devaputraḥ |
And the deva-son born in the realm of desire who, for one engaged on the [Conjectural: wholesome] side in passing beyond the aggregates, the defilements and death,
Printed akuśalapakṣa-; read kuśalapakṣa- (Xuanzang 勝善品).
sbh2.0:94.13
niścayaprāptaḥ antarāyamupasaṁharati |
[Conjectural: having attained mastery,] brings obstruction
Printed niścayaprāptaḥ, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 得大自在.
sbh2.0:94.14
vyākṣepakaraṇe |
in the making of distraction —
sbh2.0:94.15
ayamucyate devaputramāraḥ |
this is the deva-son-māra.
sbh2.0:94.16
tatra yatra ca mriyate |
Now: where one dies,
sbh2.0:94.17
yaścāsau mṛtyuryena ca mṛtyuṁ namayati, krāmayatyantarāyikena vastunā |
and that which is death, and that by which one is made to yield to death, [and that which] turns [one] aside by an obstructive thing —
sbh2.0:94.18
ityetadadhikṛtya catvāro mārā vyavasthāpitāḥ |
by reference to this, the four māras are established.
sbh2.0:94.19
tatra pañcasūpādānaskandheṣu jāteṣu varddhamāneṣu mriyate |
Here, one dies when the five aggregates of clinging have been born and are growing;
sbh2.0:94.20
kleśāṁ śān janayatyāyatyāṁ jātaśca mriyate |
one generates defilements and, born again in the future, dies;
sbh2.0:94.21
cyutiśca cyavanatā satvānāṁ jīvitendriyanirodhaḥ |
and the falling, the falling-away of beings, the cessation of the life-faculty,
sbh2.0:94.22
kālakriyā svabhāva eva mṛtyuḥ |
the dying: this is death in its own nature.
sbh2.0:94.23
devaputramāraśca maraṇa samatikramāya prayuktasyāntarāyamupasaṁharati |
And the deva-son-māra creates obstacle for one applied to the surmounting of death,
sbh2.0:94.24
yena naiva vā śaknoti maraṇadharmatāṁ samatikramitum |
by which he is unable to surmount the death-nature,
sbh2.0:95.1
kālāntareṇa vā samatikrāmati |
or only surmounts it after a long time.
sbh2.0:95.2
tatrāvaśagato mārasya bhavati laukikamārgavītarāgaḥ pṛthagjanaḥ |
Now, the worldling free of lust by the worldly path is not under Māra’s control —
sbh2.0:96.1
ihasthastatropapanno vā, a vaśagataḥ |
whether staying here or reborn there, [he is] not under [his] control;
sbh2.0:96.2
punaryaḥ avītarāgaḥ |
but one who is not free of lust [is].
sbh2.0:96.3
tatra yo vītarāgaḥ |
[Of these,] the one [Conjectural: not] free of lust
Printed yo vītarāgaḥ; read yo ’vītarāgaḥ (96.2; Xuanzang 若未離欲).
sbh2.0:96.4
eva hastagato yathākāmaṁ karaṇīyaḥ |
is as if in [his] hand, to be dealt with as he wishes.
sbh2.0:96.5
vītarāgo vā punarbaddho mārabandhanaiḥ |
Or again, the one free of lust [by the worldly path] is bound by Māra’s bonds,
sbh2.0:96.6
aparimukto mārapāśairyasmātsa punarapyāgantī ntā imāṁ maṁ dhārntum dhātum |
not released from māra’s snares, since he must [eventually] return to this realm [of desire].
sbh2.0:96.7
tatra mārakarmāṇi |
Now, māra-actions:
sbh2.0:96.8
yasya kasyacitkarmaṇo dharmacchandaḥ samutpanno naiṣkramyopasaṁhitaḥ |
When anyone’s desire for the dharma has arisen, connected with renunciation,
sbh2.0:96.9
kāmagredhamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā pravarttante |
and, making greed for sense-pleasures predominant, he [Conjectural: turns back from it] —
Printed pravartante, corrupt; the sense follows Xuanzang 退捨.
sbh2.0:96.10
veditavyaṁ mārakarmai taditi |
this is to be known as a māra-action.
sbh2.0:96.11
indriyairguptadvārasya viharataḥ |
For one dwelling with guarded sense-doors,
sbh2.0:96.12
yasya raṁjanīyeṣu rūpeṣu sahagatvarasampraṣṭavyadharmeṣu nimittagrāhitāyāmanuvyaṁjanagrāhitāyāṁ cittaṁ praskandati |
whose mind, in lust-arousing forms, sounds, smells, tastes, tangibles, dharmas, in sign-seizing and feature-seizing, leaps forth —
sbh2.0:96.13
veditavyaṁ mārakarmaitaditi |
this is to be known as māra-action.
sbh2.0:96.14
evaṁ bhojaneṣu mātrajñasya viharataḥ praṇīteṣu raseṣu chandarāgamanunayena cittaṁ praskandati |
Likewise for one dwelling with knowledge of right measure in food, whose mind leaps forth in delight-and-lust toward exquisite tastes;
sbh2.0:96.15
bhaktavaiṣamye evaṁ pūrvarātrāpararātraṁ jāgarikāyo gamananuyuktasya viharataḥ |
or toward irregularity in eating. Likewise for one dwelling applied to wakefulness in the early and late night,
sbh2.0:96.16
nidrāsukhe, śayanasukhe, pārśvasukhe cittaṁ praskandati |
whose mind leaps forth to sleeping-pleasure, lying-down-pleasure, side-lying-pleasure —
sbh2.0:96.17
veditavyaṁ mārakarmaitaditi |
this is to be known as māra-action.
sbh2.0:96.18
tathā saṁprajānadvihāriṇo viharataḥ |
Likewise for one dwelling with full awareness,
sbh2.0:96.19
abhikramapratikramādiṣu śiśumudāravarṇaṁ raṁjanīyaṁ mātṛgrāmaṁ dṛṣṭvā ayoniśo nimittagrāheṇa cittaṁ praskandati |
in setting forth and returning etc., seeing a youthful, beautiful-complexioned, lust-arousing woman, with mind unwisely seizing the sign, leaping forth;
sbh2.0:96.20
lokacitrāṇi vā dṛṣṭvā cittaṁ praskandati |
or seeing varied worldly spectacles, mind leaps forth;
sbh2.0:96.21
bahvarthatāṁ tāyāṁ , bahukṛtyatāyāṁ, cittaṁ praskandati |
in much-business, much-task, mind leaps forth —
sbh2.0:96.22
tadyathā gṛhasthapravrajitaiḥ saṁsargārāmatāyāṁ, pāpamitraiḥ saha ekavyavasitāyāṁ, dṛṣṭyanumate cittaṁ praskandati |
namely in delight in mixing with householders and renunciants, in being of one resolve with evil friends, and, [Conjectural: having seen them,] in approval, the mind leaps forth.
Printed dṛṣṭyanumate; read dṛṣṭvānumate (Xuanzang 便生隨喜).
sbh2.0:96.23
veditavyaṁ mārakarmaitaditi |
This is to be known as māra-action.
sbh2.0:97.1

Gone to the forest, or to the foot of a tree, or to an empty hut, he sees a great alarming fearsomeness, terror-causing, hair-raising.
sbh2.0:97.5
brāhmaṇaveṣeṇa, vā manuṣyaveṣeṇa vā, amanuṣyaveṣeṇa vā, kaścidupasaṁkramyāyoniśaḥ |
In the guise of a brāhmaṇa, or of a human, or of a non-human, someone approaches, and unwisely,

sbh2.0:97.8

This is to be known as māra-action.
sbh2.0:97.9

sbh2.0:97.13

sbh2.0:97.14
veditavyaṁ mārakarmaitaditi |
This is to be known as māra-action.
sbh2.0:97.15

Such are the kinds of māra-actions to be understood — those of the four māras as appropriate.

Summary verse:
